01 Android Fundamentals

01_android_fundamentals.kt · Kotlin reference

/**
 * ============================================================================
 * 01 — ANDROID FUNDAMENTALS
 * Google SDE-2 Interview Prep
 * ============================================================================
 *
 * Topics Covered:
 *   1. Activity Lifecycle (deep dive)
 *   2. Fragment Lifecycle
 *   3. Activity ↔ Fragment interleaved lifecycle
 *   4. Configuration Changes & State Restoration
 *   5. Intent System (Explicit, Implicit, PendingIntent)
 *   6. Android Manifest & Permissions
 *   7. Context Types (Application vs Activity)
 *   8. Launch Modes & Task Affinity
 *   9. Process Death & Restoration
 *
 * ============================================================================
 */

// ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
// 1. ACTIVITY LIFECYCLE — DEEP DIVE
// ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

/**
 * The full Activity lifecycle:
 *
 *   onCreate()    → Activity is first created. Initialize views, bind data.
 *                   Bundle? is non-null on recreation (config change / process death).
 *
 *   onStart()     → Activity becomes VISIBLE but not interactive.
 *                   Register UI-related observers here.
 *
 *   onResume()    → Activity is in FOREGROUND and interactive.
 *                   Resume animations, camera, sensors.
 *                   ⚠️ Google expects P99 onResume < 80ms for smooth UX.
 *
 *   onPause()     → Activity is PARTIALLY VISIBLE (dialog / multi-window).
 *                   Release exclusive resources (camera), pause animations.
 *                   ⚠️ Keep this fast — next Activity's onResume() waits for this.
 *
 *   onStop()      → Activity is NO LONGER VISIBLE.
 *                   Release heavy resources, unregister broadcast receivers.
 *                   Good place for database writes / analytics events.
 *
 *   onDestroy()   → Activity is being DESTROYED.
 *                   Final cleanup. isFinishing() distinguishes:
 *                     - true  → user navigated away (back press, finish())
 *                     - false → system is destroying for config change
 *
 *   onRestart()   → Called when Activity is coming back from STOPPED state
 *                   (user navigated away and came back).
 *                   Sequence: onStop() → onRestart() → onStart()
 *
 * 💡 Interview Tip: Draw the lifecycle diagram on the whiteboard.
 *    Know which callbacks are guaranteed (onPause/onStop) vs. not (onDestroy).
 */

class LifecycleAwareActivity : AppCompatActivity() {

    private lateinit var sensorManager: SensorManager
    private var cameraSource: CameraSource? = null

    override fun onCreate(savedInstanceState: Bundle?) {
        super.onCreate(savedInstanceState)
        setContentView(R.layout.activity_main)

        // savedInstanceState is non-null when recreating after config change or process death
        if (savedInstanceState != null) {
            val scrollPosition = savedInstanceState.getInt("SCROLL_POS", 0)
            val draftText = savedInstanceState.getString("DRAFT_TEXT", "")
            restoreUIState(scrollPosition, draftText)
        }

        initializeComponents()
    }

    override fun onStart() {
        super.onStart()
        // Register lifecycle-aware observers
        registerConnectivityObserver()
    }

    override fun onResume() {
        super.onResume()
        // Resume camera preview, start animations
        cameraSource?.start()
        startUIAnimations()
    }

    override fun onPause() {
        super.onPause()
        // Release camera — another activity might need it
        cameraSource?.stop()
        pauseUIAnimations()
    }

    override fun onStop() {
        super.onStop()
        // Heavy cleanup — Activity is no longer visible
        saveAnalyticsEvent("screen_exit")
        unregisterConnectivityObserver()
    }

    override fun onSaveInstanceState(outState: Bundle) {
        super.onSaveInstanceState(outState)
        // Save transient UI state (NOT business data — that goes in ViewModel/DB)
        outState.putInt("SCROLL_POS", getCurrentScrollPosition())
        outState.putString("DRAFT_TEXT", getDraftText())
    }

    override fun onDestroy() {
        super.onDestroy()
        if (isFinishing) {
            // User is leaving for good — final cleanup
            releaseAllResources()
        }
        // If !isFinishing, system is destroying for config change — ViewModel survives
    }
}


// ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
// 2. FRAGMENT LIFECYCLE
// ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

/**
 * Fragment has additional lifecycle callbacks beyond Activity:
 *
 *   onAttach(context)    → Fragment attached to Activity. Get reference to Activity here.
 *   onCreate(bundle)     → Initialize non-view logic.
 *   onCreateView()       → Inflate the view hierarchy. Return the root view.
 *   onViewCreated()      → View is ready. Set up RecyclerView, observers, click listeners.
 *                          ⚠️ This is where you should observe LiveData/StateFlow.
 *   onStart()            → Fragment is visible.
 *   onResume()           → Fragment is interactive.
 *   onPause()            → Fragment loses focus.
 *   onStop()             → Fragment not visible.
 *   onDestroyView()      → View hierarchy is destroyed. Clean up view references.
 *                          ⚠️ Fragment instance may survive (backstack). Null out _binding here.
 *   onDestroy()          → Fragment instance destroyed.
 *   onDetach()           → Fragment detached from Activity.
 *
 * 💡 Key Insight: Fragment VIEW lifecycle ≠ Fragment INSTANCE lifecycle.
 *    When on backstack, view is destroyed but instance stays alive.
 *    This is the #1 source of memory leaks in Fragments.
 */

class ExampleFragment : Fragment(R.layout.fragment_example) {

    private var _binding: FragmentExampleBinding? = null
    private val binding get() = _binding!!

    private val viewModel: ExampleViewModel by viewModels()

    override fun onViewCreated(view: View, savedInstanceState: Bundle?) {
        super.onViewCreated(view, savedInstanceState)
        _binding = FragmentExampleBinding.bind(view)

        // ✅ Use viewLifecycleOwner, NOT this (the Fragment instance)
        viewLifecycleOwner.lifecycleScope.launch {
            viewLifecycleOwner.repeatOnLifecycle(Lifecycle.State.STARTED) {
                viewModel.uiState.collect { state ->
                    updateUI(state)
                }
            }
        }
    }

    override fun onDestroyView() {
        super.onDestroyView()
        // ⚠️ MUST null out binding to prevent memory leak
        // Fragment instance survives on backstack but view is destroyed
        _binding = null
    }
}

/**
 * ⚠️ GOTCHA — viewLifecycleOwner vs this (Fragment)
 *
 *   viewLifecycleOwner → tied to Fragment's VIEW lifecycle (onCreateView → onDestroyView)
 *   this               → tied to Fragment INSTANCE lifecycle (onCreate → onDestroy)
 *
 *   Using `this` to observe LiveData/StateFlow can cause:
 *   - Multiple active observers after backstack navigation
 *   - Updates delivered to a destroyed view → crash
 *
 *   ✅ ALWAYS use viewLifecycleOwner for UI observations
 */


// ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
// 3. ACTIVITY ↔ ACTIVITY INTERLEAVED LIFECYCLE
// ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

/**
 * ❓ Interview Question: Activity A launches Activity B. What's the lifecycle order?
 *
 *   A.onPause()
 *   B.onCreate()
 *   B.onStart()
 *   B.onResume()
 *   A.onStop()        ← A.onStop() happens AFTER B is fully visible
 *
 * ❓ Follow-up: What if B is a transparent/dialog Activity?
 *
 *   A.onPause()       ← A is partially visible, so onStop() is NOT called
 *   B.onCreate()
 *   B.onStart()
 *   B.onResume()
 *                     ← A stays in PAUSED state (still partially visible)
 *
 * ❓ Follow-up: User presses Back on Activity B?
 *
 *   B.onPause()
 *   A.onRestart()
 *   A.onStart()
 *   A.onResume()
 *   B.onStop()
 *   B.onDestroy()
 *
 * 💡 Interview Tip: The key insight is that A.onStop() only fires
 *    when A is COMPLETELY invisible. If B is translucent, A stays PAUSED.
 */


// ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
// 4. CONFIGURATION CHANGES & STATE RESTORATION
// ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

/**
 * Configuration changes that trigger Activity destruction + recreation:
 *   - Screen rotation (orientation)
 *   - Language change (locale)
 *   - Screen size change (foldable, multi-window)
 *   - Dark mode toggle (uiMode)
 *   - Font scale change
 *
 * State preservation layers (in order of precedence):
 *
 *   1. ViewModel             → Survives config change. NOT process death.
 *   2. onSaveInstanceState   → Survives config change AND process death.
 *                               Limited to ~1MB (Binder transaction limit).
 *   3. Persistent storage    → Survives everything (Room, DataStore, SharedPrefs).
 *
 * ⚠️ GOTCHA: ViewModel does NOT survive process death.
 *    Use SavedStateHandle in ViewModel for critical transient state.
 */

class RobustViewModel(
    private val savedStateHandle: SavedStateHandle
) : ViewModel() {

    // Survives config change (in ViewModel memory)
    private val _items = MutableStateFlow<List<Item>>(emptyList())
    val items: StateFlow<List<Item>> = _items.asStateFlow()

    // Survives config change AND process death (via SavedStateHandle → Bundle)
    val searchQuery: StateFlow<String> =
        savedStateHandle.getStateFlow("search_query", "")

    fun updateSearchQuery(query: String) {
        savedStateHandle["search_query"] = query
    }

    // ❓ Follow-up: What data goes where?
    //   ViewModel memory  → fetched data, computed lists (re-fetchable)
    //   SavedStateHandle   → user input, scroll position, selected tab
    //   Room/DataStore     → user preferences, cached API data
}

/**
 * android:configChanges — Opting out of destruction
 *
 * You CAN declare in AndroidManifest:
 *   android:configChanges="orientation|screenSize"
 *
 * This prevents destruction. Instead, onConfigurationChanged() is called.
 *
 * ⚠️ Google discourages this except for specific cases (video players, games).
 * Reason: You must manually handle ALL config-dependent resources.
 */


// ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
// 5. INTENT SYSTEM
// ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

/**
 * Intents are the messaging system for inter-component communication.
 *
 * Types:
 *   Explicit Intent → targets a specific component (class name)
 *   Implicit Intent → declares an action; system finds matching components
 */

// Explicit Intent — navigate within your app
fun launchDetailActivity(context: Context, itemId: String) {
    val intent = Intent(context, DetailActivity::class.java).apply {
        putExtra("ITEM_ID", itemId)
        flags = Intent.FLAG_ACTIVITY_SINGLE_TOP
    }
    context.startActivity(intent)
}

// Implicit Intent — delegate to another app
fun shareText(context: Context, text: String) {
    val intent = Intent(Intent.ACTION_SEND).apply {
        type = "text/plain"
        putExtra(Intent.EXTRA_TEXT, text)
    }
    // ⚠️ Always check if any app can handle the intent
    if (intent.resolveActivity(context.packageManager) != null) {
        context.startActivity(Intent.createChooser(intent, "Share via"))
    }
}

/**
 * PendingIntent — a token given to another app to execute an Intent on your behalf.
 *
 * Used in: Notifications, AlarmManager, Widgets, MediaSession
 *
 * Flags:
 *   FLAG_IMMUTABLE     → the PendingIntent cannot be modified (API 31+ default)
 *   FLAG_MUTABLE       → allows modification (e.g., inline reply in notifications)
 *   FLAG_UPDATE_CURRENT → updates extras of existing PendingIntent
 *   FLAG_ONE_SHOT       → can only be used once
 *
 * ⚠️ Security: API 31+ requires explicit mutability flag.
 */
fun createNotificationPendingIntent(context: Context): PendingIntent {
    val intent = Intent(context, MainActivity::class.java).apply {
        flags = Intent.FLAG_ACTIVITY_NEW_TASK or Intent.FLAG_ACTIVITY_CLEAR_TASK
    }
    return PendingIntent.getActivity(
        context,
        0,
        intent,
        PendingIntent.FLAG_IMMUTABLE or PendingIntent.FLAG_UPDATE_CURRENT
    )
}


// ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
// 6. CONTEXT TYPES
// ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

/**
 * ❓ Interview Question: What's the difference between Application Context and Activity Context?
 *
 * Application Context:
 *   - Lives for the entire app process lifetime
 *   - No reference to any Activity or window
 *   - ✅ Safe for: singletons, repositories, DI, WorkManager
 *   - ❌ Cannot: show dialogs, inflate themed views, start Activities (without FLAG_NEW_TASK)
 *
 * Activity Context:
 *   - Lives for the Activity's lifetime
 *   - Has access to the window, theme, and UI
 *   - ✅ Safe for: dialogs, toasts, inflating views, theme-dependent operations
 *   - ⚠️ Danger: storing a reference in a singleton → MEMORY LEAK
 *
 * Service Context:
 *   - Similar to Application Context (no UI)
 *
 * 💡 Rule of Thumb:
 *    Use Application Context for anything that outlives an Activity.
 *    Use Activity Context for anything UI-related.
 */


// ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
// 7. LAUNCH MODES & TASK AFFINITY
// ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

/**
 * Launch modes control how Activities are instantiated and placed in tasks.
 *
 * standard (default):
 *   - New instance created every time.
 *   - A → B → A → B creates 4 instances in one task.
 *
 * singleTop:
 *   - If already at the top of the stack, reuse via onNewIntent().
 *   - A → B → B (singleTop) → B receives onNewIntent(), not recreated.
 *   - Use case: search results, notification tap.
 *
 * singleTask:
 *   - Only one instance in the entire system. Gets its own task.
 *   - If exists, all Activities above it are destroyed, onNewIntent() called.
 *   - Use case: home/dashboard screen.
 *
 * singleInstance:
 *   - Like singleTask but the Activity is the ONLY one in its task.
 *   - Use case: phone dialer, payment screen.
 *
 * singleInstancePerTask (API 31+):
 *   - One instance per task, but can exist in multiple tasks.
 *
 * ❓ Interview Follow-up: What's the difference between setting launchMode
 *    in the manifest vs. using Intent flags?
 *
 *    - Manifest: affects ALL launches of that Activity
 *    - Intent flags: affect only that specific launch
 *    - Intent flags take precedence over manifest declarations
 *
 * Common Intent Flags:
 *   FLAG_ACTIVITY_NEW_TASK       → behaves like singleTask
 *   FLAG_ACTIVITY_CLEAR_TOP      → destroys all above; combined with NEW_TASK = singleTask
 *   FLAG_ACTIVITY_SINGLE_TOP     → behaves like singleTop
 *   FLAG_ACTIVITY_CLEAR_TASK     → clears entire task before launching
 *   FLAG_ACTIVITY_NO_HISTORY     → Activity not kept in backstack
 */


// ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
// 8. PROCESS DEATH & RESTORATION
// ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

/**
 * Android can kill your app's process when it's in the background.
 *
 * What survives process death:
 *   ✅ onSaveInstanceState() Bundle
 *   ✅ SavedStateHandle in ViewModel
 *   ✅ Persistent storage (Room, DataStore, files)
 *   ❌ ViewModel (destroyed with the process)
 *   ❌ Static variables
 *   ❌ In-memory caches
 *   ❌ Singleton state
 *
 * What happens when user returns after process death:
 *   1. New process is created
 *   2. Application.onCreate() runs
 *   3. Activity is recreated with savedInstanceState Bundle
 *   4. ViewModel is NEW — SavedStateHandle is restored from Bundle
 *   5. Navigation state is restored (NavController backstack)
 *
 * 💡 How to test process death:
 *   - Android Studio: Terminate Application button (⏹️ with red X)
 *   - ADB: adb shell am kill <package>
 *   - Developer Options: "Don't keep activities" (less accurate)
 *
 * ⚠️ Common bug: App crashes after process death because:
 *   1. Fragment arguments not using SafeArgs / savedInstanceState
 *   2. Companion object / singleton state assumed to persist
 *   3. NavArgs or Intent extras not re-read on recreation
 */

// Pattern to handle process death gracefully in ViewModel
class ProcessDeathSafeViewModel(
    private val savedStateHandle: SavedStateHandle,
    private val repository: ItemRepository
) : ViewModel() {

    private val _uiState = MutableStateFlow(UiState())
    val uiState: StateFlow<UiState> = _uiState.asStateFlow()

    init {
        // Check if restoring from process death
        val restoredQuery = savedStateHandle.get<String>("query")
        if (restoredQuery != null) {
            search(restoredQuery)
        } else {
            loadInitialData()
        }
    }

    fun search(query: String) {
        savedStateHandle["query"] = query  // Persists across process death
        viewModelScope.launch {
            _uiState.value = UiState(isLoading = true)
            val results = repository.search(query)
            _uiState.value = UiState(items = results)
        }
    }
}


// ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
// 9. ANDROID MANIFEST — KEY CONCEPTS
// ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

/**
 * Critical manifest declarations to know:
 *
 * <uses-permission>     → declares permissions the app needs
 * <uses-feature>        → declares hardware/software features required
 * <application>         → app-level config (theme, backup, network security)
 * <activity>            → each Activity with intent-filters, launch mode
 * <service>             → foreground service type (API 34+)
 * <receiver>            → broadcast receivers
 * <provider>            → content providers, FileProvider
 * <meta-data>           → configuration for libraries (Google Maps key, etc.)
 *
 * ❓ Interview Question: What is exported=true vs exported=false?
 *
 *   exported=true   → other apps can start this component
 *   exported=false  → only your app (same UID) can start it
 *
 *   ⚠️ API 31+: If a component has intent-filters, you MUST explicitly
 *   declare android:exported. Previously it defaulted to true.
 *
 * ❓ Interview Question: What is android:allowBackup?
 *
 *   Controls Auto Backup. Set to false for sensitive apps (banking).
 *   API 31+: Use android:dataExtractionRules for fine-grained control.
 */


// ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
// PRACTICE QUESTIONS FOR THIS TOPIC
// ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

/**
 * ❓ Q1: Activity A starts Activity B (opaque). User rotates the device.
 *        What lifecycle events fire and in what order?
 *
 *    Answer: Only Activity B is affected (A is already stopped).
 *      B.onPause() → B.onStop() → B.onSaveInstanceState()
 *      → B.onDestroy() → B.onCreate(bundle) → B.onStart() → B.onResume()
 *
 * ❓ Q2: How does a Fragment communicate with its host Activity?
 *
 *    Answer (modern): Use shared ViewModel scoped to Activity.
 *      val sharedVM: SharedViewModel by activityViewModels()
 *    Answer (alternative): Use FragmentResult API.
 *      setFragmentResult("key", bundleOf("data" to value))
 *    ❌ Avoid: Direct Activity reference casts (tight coupling).
 *
 * ❓ Q3: What's the Binder transaction limit and how does it affect
 *        onSaveInstanceState()?
 *
 *    Answer: ~1MB total for all transactions in a process.
 *    Saving too much data in the Bundle can cause TransactionTooLargeException.
 *    Rule: Save only IDs and minimal state; reload data from ViewModel/DB.
 *
 * ❓ Q4: You have a singleton holding an Activity reference.
 *        What happens and how do you fix it?
 *
 *    Answer: Memory leak — Activity can't be GC'd even after onDestroy().
 *    Fix: Use Application Context, WeakReference, or lifecycle-aware components.
 *
 * ❓ Q5: What is the difference between finish() and moveTaskToBack()?
 *
 *    Answer:
 *      finish()          → destroys the Activity, removes from backstack
 *      moveTaskToBack()  → moves entire task to background (like pressing Home)
 */

02 App Architecture

02_app_architecture.kt · Kotlin reference

/**
 * ============================================================================
 * 02 — APP ARCHITECTURE
 * Google SDE-2 Interview Prep
 * ============================================================================
 *
 * Topics Covered:
 *   1. Google's Recommended Architecture (UI → Domain → Data)
 *   2. MVVM Pattern — ViewModel + StateFlow/LiveData
 *   3. MVI Pattern — Unidirectional Data Flow
 *   4. Clean Architecture — UseCases / Interactors
 *   5. Repository Pattern — Single Source of Truth
 *   6. ViewModel Deep Dive
 *   7. MVVM vs MVI — When to Use Which
 *   8. Architecture Components (Lifecycle, Navigation)
 *   9. Multi-Source Data Merging (combine, debounce, flatMapLatest)
 *
 * ============================================================================
 */

// ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
// 1. GOOGLE'S RECOMMENDED ARCHITECTURE
// ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

/**
 * Google's official app architecture has 3 layers:
 *
 *   ┌──────────────────────┐
 *   │      UI Layer        │  Activities, Fragments, Composables
 *   │  (State Holders)     │  ViewModels, UI State classes
 *   ├──────────────────────┤
 *   │    Domain Layer      │  UseCases / Interactors (optional)
 *   │   (Business Logic)   │  Pure Kotlin, no Android deps
 *   ├──────────────────────┤
 *   │     Data Layer       │  Repositories, DataSources
 *   │  (Data Management)   │  API clients, DAOs, DataStore
 *   └──────────────────────┘
 *
 * Key Principles:
 *   - Separation of concerns: Each layer has one job.
 *   - Drive UI from data models: UI should be a pure function of state.
 *   - Single source of truth (SSOT): One authoritative data source per data type.
 *   - Unidirectional data flow (UDF): State flows down, events flow up.
 *
 * Dependency Rule:
 *   UI → Domain → Data (each layer depends only on the layer below)
 *   ❌ Data layer should NEVER reference UI layer
 */


// ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
// 2. MVVM PATTERN — FULL IMPLEMENTATION
// ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

/**
 * MVVM Components:
 *   Model → Data classes, Repository, database
 *   View  → Activity/Fragment/Composable (passive, no business logic)
 *   ViewModel → Holds UI state, processes user events, calls Repository
 *
 * Data flow:
 *   View ---(events)--→ ViewModel ---(calls)--→ Repository
 *   View ←--(state)---- ViewModel ←--(data)---- Repository
 */

// ── UI State ──
// Immutable data class representing the entire screen state
data class ArticleListUiState(
    val articles: List<Article> = emptyList(),
    val isLoading: Boolean = false,
    val errorMessage: String? = null,
    val selectedFilter: ArticleFilter = ArticleFilter.ALL,
    val isRefreshing: Boolean = false
)

enum class ArticleFilter { ALL, BOOKMARKED, RECENT }

// ── ViewModel ──
class ArticleListViewModel(
    private val getArticlesUseCase: GetArticlesUseCase,
    private val bookmarkArticleUseCase: BookmarkArticleUseCase,
    private val savedStateHandle: SavedStateHandle
) : ViewModel() {

    private val _uiState = MutableStateFlow(ArticleListUiState())
    val uiState: StateFlow<ArticleListUiState> = _uiState.asStateFlow()

    // Restore filter from process death
    private val selectedFilter: StateFlow<ArticleFilter> =
        savedStateHandle.getStateFlow("filter", ArticleFilter.ALL)

    init {
        // React to filter changes
        viewModelScope.launch {
            selectedFilter.collect { filter ->
                loadArticles(filter)
            }
        }
    }

    fun onFilterChanged(filter: ArticleFilter) {
        savedStateHandle["filter"] = filter
    }

    fun onRefresh() {
        viewModelScope.launch {
            _uiState.update { it.copy(isRefreshing = true) }
            loadArticles(selectedFilter.value, forceRefresh = true)
        }
    }

    fun onBookmarkClicked(articleId: String) {
        viewModelScope.launch {
            bookmarkArticleUseCase(articleId)
                .onFailure { e ->
                    _uiState.update { it.copy(errorMessage = e.message) }
                }
        }
    }

    fun onErrorDismissed() {
        _uiState.update { it.copy(errorMessage = null) }
    }

    private suspend fun loadArticles(
        filter: ArticleFilter,
        forceRefresh: Boolean = false
    ) {
        _uiState.update { it.copy(isLoading = true, errorMessage = null) }

        getArticlesUseCase(filter, forceRefresh)
            .onSuccess { articles ->
                _uiState.update {
                    it.copy(
                        articles = articles,
                        isLoading = false,
                        isRefreshing = false,
                        selectedFilter = filter
                    )
                }
            }
            .onFailure { e ->
                _uiState.update {
                    it.copy(
                        isLoading = false,
                        isRefreshing = false,
                        errorMessage = e.message
                    )
                }
            }
    }
}

// ── Composable UI (View) ──
@Composable
fun ArticleListScreen(
    viewModel: ArticleListViewModel = hiltViewModel()
) {
    val uiState by viewModel.uiState.collectAsStateWithLifecycle()

    ArticleListContent(
        uiState = uiState,
        onFilterChanged = viewModel::onFilterChanged,
        onRefresh = viewModel::onRefresh,
        onBookmarkClicked = viewModel::onBookmarkClicked,
        onErrorDismissed = viewModel::onErrorDismissed
    )
}

@Composable
fun ArticleListContent(
    uiState: ArticleListUiState,
    onFilterChanged: (ArticleFilter) -> Unit,
    onRefresh: () -> Unit,
    onBookmarkClicked: (String) -> Unit,
    onErrorDismissed: () -> Unit
) {
    // Stateless composable — all state comes from parameters
    // Easy to preview and test
    Scaffold { padding ->
        SwipeRefresh(
            state = rememberSwipeRefreshState(uiState.isRefreshing),
            onRefresh = onRefresh
        ) {
            if (uiState.isLoading && uiState.articles.isEmpty()) {
                LoadingScreen()
            } else {
                LazyColumn(contentPadding = padding) {
                    items(uiState.articles, key = { it.id }) { article ->
                        ArticleItem(
                            article = article,
                            onBookmark = { onBookmarkClicked(article.id) }
                        )
                    }
                }
            }
        }
    }

    uiState.errorMessage?.let { message ->
        ErrorSnackbar(message = message, onDismiss = onErrorDismissed)
    }
}


// ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
// 3. MVI PATTERN — UNIDIRECTIONAL DATA FLOW
// ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

/**
 * MVI: Model-View-Intent
 *
 *   Intent  → user actions / events (not Android Intent)
 *   Model   → immutable state
 *   View    → renders state
 *
 * Flow: View → Intent → Reducer → New State → View
 *
 * Benefits over MVVM:
 *   ✅ Single immutable state — easier to debug, log, replay
 *   ✅ Predictable state transitions — reducer is a pure function
 *   ✅ Time-travel debugging possible
 *   ✅ Better for complex state machines (payments, checkout)
 *
 * Trade-offs:
 *   ❌ More boilerplate (sealed classes for Intent/State)
 *   ❌ Can be overkill for simple CRUD screens
 *
 * 💡 Google Interview: "When would you use MVI over MVVM?"
 *    Answer: Payment flows, fintech, state machines, audit logging.
 *    MVVM is better for simple forms and CRUD.
 */

// ── Sealed classes for Intent and State ──
sealed interface CheckoutIntent {
    data class LoadCart(val userId: String) : CheckoutIntent
    data class UpdateQuantity(val itemId: String, val qty: Int) : CheckoutIntent
    data class ApplyPromoCode(val code: String) : CheckoutIntent
    data object ProceedToPayment : CheckoutIntent
    data class SelectPaymentMethod(val method: PaymentMethod) : CheckoutIntent
    data object ConfirmPayment : CheckoutIntent
}

data class CheckoutState(
    val cartItems: List<CartItem> = emptyList(),
    val subtotal: Double = 0.0,
    val discount: Double = 0.0,
    val total: Double = 0.0,
    val promoCode: String? = null,
    val promoError: String? = null,
    val paymentMethod: PaymentMethod? = null,
    val checkoutStep: CheckoutStep = CheckoutStep.CART,
    val isProcessing: Boolean = false,
    val error: String? = null
)

enum class CheckoutStep { CART, PAYMENT_SELECTION, CONFIRMATION, SUCCESS, FAILED }

// Side effects that should happen once (navigation, toasts)
sealed interface CheckoutEffect {
    data class ShowToast(val message: String) : CheckoutEffect
    data object NavigateToSuccess : CheckoutEffect
    data class NavigateToError(val reason: String) : CheckoutEffect
}

// ── MVI ViewModel ──
class CheckoutViewModel(
    private val cartRepository: CartRepository,
    private val paymentRepository: PaymentRepository,
    private val promoRepository: PromoRepository
) : ViewModel() {

    private val _state = MutableStateFlow(CheckoutState())
    val state: StateFlow<CheckoutState> = _state.asStateFlow()

    // One-time effects via Channel (not StateFlow — avoids re-delivery)
    private val _effects = Channel<CheckoutEffect>(Channel.BUFFERED)
    val effects: Flow<CheckoutEffect> = _effects.receiveAsFlow()

    fun processIntent(intent: CheckoutIntent) {
        viewModelScope.launch {
            when (intent) {
                is CheckoutIntent.LoadCart -> handleLoadCart(intent.userId)
                is CheckoutIntent.UpdateQuantity -> handleUpdateQty(intent.itemId, intent.qty)
                is CheckoutIntent.ApplyPromoCode -> handleApplyPromo(intent.code)
                is CheckoutIntent.ProceedToPayment -> handleProceedToPayment()
                is CheckoutIntent.SelectPaymentMethod -> handleSelectPayment(intent.method)
                is CheckoutIntent.ConfirmPayment -> handleConfirmPayment()
            }
        }
    }

    // Reducer pattern — pure state transformation
    private fun reduce(transform: CheckoutState.() -> CheckoutState) {
        _state.update { it.transform() }
    }

    private suspend fun handleLoadCart(userId: String) {
        reduce { copy(isProcessing = true) }
        cartRepository.getCart(userId)
            .onSuccess { items ->
                val subtotal = items.sumOf { it.price * it.quantity }
                reduce {
                    copy(cartItems = items, subtotal = subtotal,
                         total = subtotal, isProcessing = false)
                }
            }
            .onFailure { e ->
                reduce { copy(error = e.message, isProcessing = false) }
            }
    }

    private suspend fun handleConfirmPayment() {
        reduce { copy(isProcessing = true, checkoutStep = CheckoutStep.CONFIRMATION) }
        val currentState = _state.value

        paymentRepository.processPayment(
            amount = currentState.total,
            method = currentState.paymentMethod!!
        ).onSuccess {
            reduce { copy(checkoutStep = CheckoutStep.SUCCESS, isProcessing = false) }
            _effects.send(CheckoutEffect.NavigateToSuccess)
        }.onFailure { e ->
            reduce { copy(checkoutStep = CheckoutStep.FAILED, isProcessing = false, error = e.message) }
            _effects.send(CheckoutEffect.NavigateToError(e.message ?: "Payment failed"))
        }
    }

    private suspend fun handleApplyPromo(code: String) { /* ... */ }
    private suspend fun handleUpdateQty(itemId: String, qty: Int) { /* ... */ }
    private suspend fun handleProceedToPayment() { /* ... */ }
    private suspend fun handleSelectPayment(method: PaymentMethod) { /* ... */ }
}


// ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
// 4. CLEAN ARCHITECTURE — USECASES
// ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

/**
 * UseCases (Interactors) encapsulate a single piece of business logic.
 *
 * Benefits:
 *   ✅ ViewModels stay thin (delegate to UseCases)
 *   ✅ Reusable across ViewModels
 *   ✅ Easy to unit test (pure Kotlin, no Android deps)
 *   ✅ Clear API boundary between UI and Data layers
 *
 * Convention:
 *   - One public method (invoke / execute)
 *   - Use operator fun invoke() for clean call syntax
 *   - Naming: VerbNounUseCase (GetArticlesUseCase, BookmarkArticleUseCase)
 */

class GetArticlesUseCase(
    private val articleRepository: ArticleRepository,
    private val userRepository: UserRepository
) {
    suspend operator fun invoke(
        filter: ArticleFilter,
        forceRefresh: Boolean = false
    ): Result<List<Article>> {
        return try {
            val userId = userRepository.getCurrentUserId()
            val articles = if (forceRefresh) {
                articleRepository.refreshArticles(userId)
            } else {
                articleRepository.getArticles(userId)
            }

            val filtered = when (filter) {
                ArticleFilter.ALL -> articles
                ArticleFilter.BOOKMARKED -> articles.filter { it.isBookmarked }
                ArticleFilter.RECENT -> articles.sortedByDescending { it.publishedAt }.take(20)
            }
            Result.success(filtered)
        } catch (e: Exception) {
            Result.failure(e)
        }
    }
}

// UseCase returning a Flow (for observing data changes)
class ObserveArticlesUseCase(
    private val articleRepository: ArticleRepository
) {
    operator fun invoke(filter: ArticleFilter): Flow<List<Article>> {
        return articleRepository.observeArticles()
            .map { articles ->
                when (filter) {
                    ArticleFilter.ALL -> articles
                    ArticleFilter.BOOKMARKED -> articles.filter { it.isBookmarked }
                    ArticleFilter.RECENT -> articles.sortedByDescending { it.publishedAt }.take(20)
                }
            }
            .catch { emit(emptyList()) }
    }
}


// ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
// 5. REPOSITORY PATTERN — SINGLE SOURCE OF TRUTH
// ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

/**
 * Repository sits between Domain and Data layers.
 *
 * Responsibilities:
 *   - Abstract data sources (network, local DB, cache)
 *   - Implement caching strategy (offline-first, cache-then-network)
 *   - Single source of truth (usually local DB)
 *   - Expose data as Flows/suspend functions
 *
 * 💡 Interview Tip: Google strongly favors offline-first architecture.
 *    Room is the SSOT. Network is a sync mechanism, not a data source.
 */

class ArticleRepositoryImpl(
    private val articleDao: ArticleDao,
    private val articleApi: ArticleApi,
    private val dispatchers: DispatcherProvider
) : ArticleRepository {

    // Offline-first: observe local DB as the source of truth
    override fun observeArticles(): Flow<List<Article>> {
        return articleDao.observeAllArticles()
            .map { entities -> entities.map { it.toDomain() } }
            .flowOn(dispatchers.io)
    }

    override suspend fun getArticles(userId: String): List<Article> {
        return withContext(dispatchers.io) {
            // Return cached data first
            val cached = articleDao.getAllArticles()
            if (cached.isNotEmpty() && !isStale(cached)) {
                return@withContext cached.map { it.toDomain() }
            }
            // If stale or empty, fetch from network and cache
            refreshArticles(userId)
        }
    }

    override suspend fun refreshArticles(userId: String): List<Article> {
        return withContext(dispatchers.io) {
            val response = articleApi.getArticles(userId)
            val entities = response.map { it.toEntity() }
            // Write to DB — observers will automatically emit
            articleDao.upsertAll(entities)
            entities.map { it.toDomain() }
        }
    }

    private fun isStale(articles: List<ArticleEntity>): Boolean {
        val latestUpdate = articles.maxOfOrNull { it.lastFetched } ?: 0L
        val fifteenMinutes = 15 * 60 * 1000L
        return System.currentTimeMillis() - latestUpdate > fifteenMinutes
    }
}


// ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
// 6. VIEWMODEL DEEP DIVE
// ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

/**
 * ViewModel Key Facts:
 *
 *   - Scoped to a ViewModelStoreOwner (Activity, Fragment, NavBackStackEntry)
 *   - Survives configuration changes (rotation, locale)
 *   - ❌ Does NOT survive process death (use SavedStateHandle)
 *   - Has viewModelScope (auto-cancelled in onCleared())
 *   - Should NOT hold references to Views, Activities, or Contexts
 *
 * ❓ Interview Question: How does ViewModel survive configuration changes?
 *
 *   Answer: ViewModel is stored in ViewModelStore, which is retained via
 *   NonConfigurationInstance mechanism. When Activity is destroyed for config
 *   change, the ViewModelStore is passed to the new Activity instance.
 *
 * ❓ Follow-up: How is ViewModel shared between Fragments?
 *
 *   Answer: Scope ViewModel to the Activity:
 *     val sharedVM: SharedViewModel by activityViewModels()
 *   Or scope to a NavGraph:
 *     val sharedVM: SharedViewModel by navGraphViewModels(R.id.checkout_graph)
 *
 * ⚠️ GOTCHA: ViewModel scoped to Fragment is destroyed when Fragment is destroyed.
 *    ViewModel scoped to Activity lives as long as the Activity.
 */

// Sharing ViewModel between Fragments via Navigation Graph scope
class StepOneFragment : Fragment() {
    // Scoped to the checkout nav graph — shared with StepTwoFragment
    private val checkoutVM: CheckoutViewModel by navGraphViewModels(R.id.checkout_graph)
}

class StepTwoFragment : Fragment() {
    // Same ViewModel instance as StepOneFragment
    private val checkoutVM: CheckoutViewModel by navGraphViewModels(R.id.checkout_graph)
}


// ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
// 7. MVVM vs MVI — DECISION FRAMEWORK
// ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

/**
 * ┌────────────────────┬────────────────────────┬─────────────────────────┐
 * │ Criteria           │ MVVM                   │ MVI                     │
 * ├────────────────────┼────────────────────────┼─────────────────────────┤
 * │ State management   │ Multiple StateFlows    │ Single immutable state  │
 * │ Complexity         │ Lower boilerplate      │ More boilerplate        │
 * │ Debugging          │ Harder (multiple       │ Easier (single state,   │
 * │                    │ state sources)         │ time-travel possible)   │
 * │ Best for           │ CRUD, forms, simple    │ Payments, checkout,     │
 * │                    │ list/detail            │ complex state machines  │
 * │ Testing            │ Test each flow         │ Test reducer function   │
 * │ Side effects       │ In ViewModel methods   │ Explicit Effect channel │
 * │ Scalability        │ Can get messy at scale │ Scales well             │
 * └────────────────────┴────────────────────────┴─────────────────────────┘
 *
 * 💡 Google SDE-2 Answer:
 *   "I use MVVM for most screens because it's simpler and Google-recommended.
 *    I switch to MVI for screens with complex state transitions — payments,
 *    multi-step wizards, or anything where I need an audit trail of state
 *    changes. The key is choosing based on the screen's complexity, not
 *    applying one pattern everywhere."
 */


// ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
// 8. NAVIGATION COMPONENT
// ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

/**
 * Jetpack Navigation:
 *   - NavHost — container for destinations
 *   - NavController — manages backstack and navigation
 *   - NavGraph — defines routes/destinations
 *   - Safe Args — type-safe argument passing
 *
 * Key features:
 *   - Deep linking support
 *   - ViewModel scoping to NavGraph
 *   - Animation transitions
 *   - Conditional navigation (auth flows)
 */

// Compose Navigation example
@Composable
fun AppNavigation() {
    val navController = rememberNavController()

    NavHost(navController = navController, startDestination = "article_list") {
        composable("article_list") {
            ArticleListScreen(
                onArticleClick = { id ->
                    navController.navigate("article_detail/$id")
                }
            )
        }
        composable(
            route = "article_detail/{articleId}",
            arguments = listOf(navArgument("articleId") { type = NavType.StringType })
        ) { backStackEntry ->
            val articleId = backStackEntry.arguments?.getString("articleId") ?: return@composable
            ArticleDetailScreen(articleId = articleId)
        }
    }
}


// ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
// 9. MULTI-SOURCE DATA MERGING
// ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

/**
 * Real production apps rarely show data from a single source.
 * A typical screen might combine:
 *   - Database (Room) for cached/persisted data
 *   - Network (Retrofit) for fresh data
 *   - In-memory state (user input, filters, sort order)
 *
 * Kotlin's combine() operator merges multiple Flows into a single stream.
 * The Repository owns the data merging; the ViewModel owns the presentation logic.
 *
 * 💡 Interview Tip: Google loves asking "How would you build a search screen
 *    with filters, sorting, and offline support?" — this pattern is the answer.
 *
 * Key tools:
 *   combine()        → merge N flows; emits whenever ANY input changes
 *   flatMapLatest {} → switch to a new flow when upstream emits (search query)
 *   debounce()       → delay emission until user stops typing
 *   distinctUntilChanged() → skip duplicate emissions
 */

// ── UI State for a search screen with filters ──
data class SearchScreenUiState(
    val query: String = "",
    val filter: SearchFilter = SearchFilter.ALL,
    val sortOrder: SortOrder = SortOrder.NEWEST,
    val results: List<Article> = emptyList(),
    val isLoading: Boolean = false,
    val error: String? = null
)

enum class SearchFilter { ALL, BOOKMARKED, UNREAD }
enum class SortOrder { NEWEST, OLDEST, RELEVANCE }

// ── Repository: merging DB + network ──
class SearchRepository(
    private val articleDao: ArticleDao,
    private val articleApi: ArticleApi,
    private val dispatchers: DispatcherProvider
) {
    // Observe local DB as SSOT, but trigger network refresh in background
    fun observeSearchResults(query: String, filter: SearchFilter): Flow<List<Article>> {
        return articleDao.observeSearch("%$query%")
            .map { entities -> entities.map { it.toDomain() } }
            .map { articles ->
                when (filter) {
                    SearchFilter.ALL -> articles
                    SearchFilter.BOOKMARKED -> articles.filter { it.isBookmarked }
                    SearchFilter.UNREAD -> articles.filter { !it.isRead }
                }
            }
            .onStart {
                // Trigger network sync in background (fire-and-forget)
                withContext(dispatchers.io) {
                    try {
                        val remote = articleApi.search(query)
                        articleDao.upsertAll(remote.map { it.toEntity() })
                    } catch (_: Exception) { /* offline is OK — DB is SSOT */ }
                }
            }
            .flowOn(dispatchers.io)
    }
}

// ── ViewModel: combine() to merge user input + data ──
class SearchViewModel(
    private val searchRepository: SearchRepository,
    private val savedStateHandle: SavedStateHandle
) : ViewModel() {

    // Each user-controllable dimension is its own StateFlow
    private val queryFlow: MutableStateFlow<String> =
        MutableStateFlow(savedStateHandle["query"] ?: "")

    private val filterFlow: MutableStateFlow<SearchFilter> =
        MutableStateFlow(savedStateHandle["filter"] ?: SearchFilter.ALL)

    private val sortOrderFlow: MutableStateFlow<SortOrder> =
        MutableStateFlow(savedStateHandle["sort"] ?: SortOrder.NEWEST)

    val uiState: StateFlow<SearchScreenUiState> = combine(
        queryFlow
            .debounce(300)                // Wait 300ms after user stops typing
            .distinctUntilChanged(),       // Skip if query hasn't actually changed
        filterFlow,
        sortOrderFlow
    ) { query, filter, sortOrder ->
        Triple(query, filter, sortOrder)
    }
        .flatMapLatest { (query, filter, sortOrder) ->
            // Switch to a new data flow every time inputs change.
            // flatMapLatest cancels the previous collection automatically.
            searchRepository.observeSearchResults(query, filter)
                .map { articles ->
                    val sorted = when (sortOrder) {
                        SortOrder.NEWEST -> articles.sortedByDescending { it.publishedAt }
                        SortOrder.OLDEST -> articles.sortedBy { it.publishedAt }
                        SortOrder.RELEVANCE -> articles // API returns relevance-sorted
                    }
                    SearchScreenUiState(
                        query = query,
                        filter = filter,
                        sortOrder = sortOrder,
                        results = sorted,
                        isLoading = false
                    )
                }
                .onStart {
                    emit(SearchScreenUiState(
                        query = query, filter = filter,
                        sortOrder = sortOrder, isLoading = true
                    ))
                }
                .catch { e ->
                    emit(SearchScreenUiState(
                        query = query, filter = filter,
                        sortOrder = sortOrder, error = e.message
                    ))
                }
        }
        .stateIn(
            scope = viewModelScope,
            started = SharingStarted.WhileSubscribed(5_000),
            initialValue = SearchScreenUiState()
        )

    // ── User actions ──
    fun onQueryChanged(query: String) {
        savedStateHandle["query"] = query
        queryFlow.value = query
    }

    fun onFilterChanged(filter: SearchFilter) {
        savedStateHandle["filter"] = filter
        filterFlow.value = filter
    }

    fun onSortChanged(sortOrder: SortOrder) {
        savedStateHandle["sort"] = sortOrder
        sortOrderFlow.value = sortOrder
    }
}

/**
 * Why this pattern matters at Google scale:
 *
 *   ✅ Reactive — any input change automatically triggers a new result
 *   ✅ Debounced — avoids hammering the DB/network on every keystroke
 *   ✅ Offline-first — Room is SSOT; network sync is best-effort
 *   ✅ Cancellation-safe — flatMapLatest cancels stale queries
 *   ✅ Process-death safe — SavedStateHandle persists user inputs
 *   ✅ Lifecycle-aware — WhileSubscribed stops work when UI is gone
 *
 * ⚠️ GOTCHA: combine() emits when ANY input changes. If you have 5 inputs
 *    and set all 5 in a row, you get 5 emissions. Use debounce() or
 *    conflate() if intermediate states are expensive.
 *
 * ⚠️ GOTCHA: debounce() delays ALL emissions, including the first one.
 *    If you want instant first result + debounced subsequent queries,
 *    use a custom operator or transformLatest with a conditional delay.
 */


// ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
// PRACTICE QUESTIONS
// ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

/**
 * ❓ Q1: Your ViewModel has grown to 500+ lines. How do you refactor?
 *    A: Extract business logic into UseCases. Each UseCase = one operation.
 *       ViewModel becomes an orchestrator that delegates to UseCases.
 *
 * ❓ Q2: How do you handle one-time events (show toast, navigate)?
 *    A: Use Channel + receiveAsFlow() in ViewModel. SharedFlow with replay=0
 *       also works but Channel guarantees delivery. Never use StateFlow for
 *       events (re-delivered on recomposition/collection restart).
 *
 * ❓ Q3: How do you share data between unrelated screens?
 *    A: Options (best → worst):
 *       1. Scope ViewModel to shared NavGraph
 *       2. Use Repository as SSOT — both VMs observe the same data source
 *       3. Navigation arguments for small, serializable data
 *       ❌ Static/global state, EventBus
 *
 * ❓ Q4: What's the difference between StateFlow and LiveData?
 *    A:
 *       - StateFlow requires an initial value; LiveData doesn't
 *       - StateFlow is Kotlin-native; LiveData is Android-specific
 *       - StateFlow doesn't auto-stop on lifecycle; needs repeatOnLifecycle
 *       - StateFlow uses equality check to skip duplicates; LiveData always notifies
 *       - StateFlow works in domain/data layers (no Android dependency)
 *
 * ❓ Q5: How does unidirectional data flow prevent bugs?
 *    A: State only changes in one place (ViewModel/Reducer). UI can't directly
 *       mutate state, so you can't have inconsistent state or race conditions.
 *       Every state change is traceable and testable.
 *
 * ❓ Q6: You have a search screen that combines a search query, filters, and
 *        Room data. The user types fast and the UI lags. How do you fix it?
 *    A: Three-pronged approach:
 *       1. debounce(300) on the query Flow — don't react to every keystroke
 *       2. flatMapLatest on the combined inputs — cancel stale queries
 *       3. Room as SSOT with network sync in onStart — no blocking network call
 *       The ViewModel uses combine(query, filter, sort).flatMapLatest { ... }
 *       to build a single reactive pipeline. WhileSubscribed(5000) in stateIn()
 *       keeps the pipeline alive briefly during config changes but stops work
 *       when the user truly leaves the screen.
 */

03 Jetpack Compose

03_jetpack_compose.kt · Kotlin reference

/**
 * ============================================================================
 * 03 — JETPACK COMPOSE
 * Google SDE-2 Interview Prep
 * ============================================================================
 *
 * Topics Covered:
 *   1. Compose Mental Model — Declarative vs Imperative
 *   2. Composition, Recomposition & Skipping
 *   3. State Management (remember, rememberSaveable, derivedStateOf)
 *   4. State Hoisting Pattern
 *   5. Side Effects (LaunchedEffect, DisposableEffect, SideEffect, etc.)
 *   6. Compose Lifecycle & Phases
 *   7. Stability & Performance (Stable, Immutable annotations)
 *   8. CompositionLocal
 *   9. Custom Layouts & Modifiers
 *  10. Compose Compiler Internals
 *  11. Compose Navigation
 *  12. Material 3, Theming & Accessibility
 *  13. Compose Interop (AndroidView, ComposeView)
 *
 * ============================================================================
 */

// ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
// 1. DECLARATIVE VS IMPERATIVE — MENTAL MODEL
// ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

/**
 * Imperative (View System):
 *   val textView = findViewById<TextView>(R.id.title)
 *   textView.text = "Hello"
 *   textView.visibility = if (show) View.VISIBLE else View.GONE
 *   → You MUTATE existing view objects
 *
 * Declarative (Compose):
 *   @Composable
 *   fun Greeting(name: String) {
 *       Text("Hello, $name")
 *   }
 *   → You DESCRIBE what the UI should look like for a given state.
 *   → Compose framework handles creating/updating the actual UI nodes.
 *
 * 💡 Key Insight: In Compose, you don't update UI — you re-describe it.
 *    The framework diffs the new description against the old one and
 *    applies only the necessary changes (similar to React's virtual DOM).
 */


// ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
// 2. COMPOSITION & RECOMPOSITION
// ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

/**
 * Composition: First time Compose executes your @Composable functions
 *              and builds the UI tree (slot table / composition).
 *
 * Recomposition: When state changes, Compose re-executes ONLY the
 *                composables that read that state. This is "smart recomposition".
 *
 * ❓ Interview Question: When does recomposition happen?
 *
 *    Answer: When a State<T> object that was READ during composition changes.
 *    Compose tracks which composables read which state objects at runtime.
 *    Only those composables are re-executed.
 *
 * ❓ Follow-up: Can recomposition be skipped?
 *
 *    Answer: Yes! If a composable's parameters haven't changed (by equality),
 *    Compose can skip re-executing it. This requires parameters to be:
 *    - Primitive types
 *    - @Stable or @Immutable annotated classes
 *    - Other composable functions (lambda stability)
 *
 * ⚠️ GOTCHA: Recomposition can happen in ANY ORDER and CONCURRENTLY.
 *    Never rely on execution order or side effects in composable functions.
 *    Never write to shared mutable state inside a composable.
 */

@Composable
fun RecompositionDemo() {
    var count by remember { mutableStateOf(0) }

    Column {
        // This Text recomposes when `count` changes
        Text("Count: $count")

        // This Text does NOT recompose — it reads no state
        Text("Static text")

        Button(onClick = { count++ }) {
            Text("Increment")
        }
    }
}

/**
 * Positional Memoization:
 *   Compose identifies composables by their POSITION in the call tree,
 *   not by variable name. This is why:
 *   - The same composable function called twice creates two separate slots
 *   - `key()` is needed when positions can shift (dynamic lists)
 */

@Composable
fun PositionalMemoizationExample(showHeader: Boolean) {
    Column {
        if (showHeader) {
            Text("Header")  // Slot 0 when present
        }
        // ⚠️ Without key(), this Text's slot shifts when showHeader changes,
        // potentially losing its remembered state
        Text("Content")

        // ✅ Fix: Use key() to give stable identity
        key("content") {
            Text("Content")
        }
    }
}


// ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
// 3. STATE MANAGEMENT
// ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

/**
 * State in Compose = any value that can change over time and affects the UI.
 *
 * mutableStateOf()    → creates observable state
 * remember { }        → retains value across recompositions (NOT config change)
 * rememberSaveable {} → retains across recomposition AND config change/process death
 * derivedStateOf {}   → computed state; recomposes only when result changes
 */

@Composable
fun StateManagementShowcase() {
    // ── remember: survives recomposition, lost on config change ──
    var counter by remember { mutableStateOf(0) }

    // ── rememberSaveable: survives recomposition + config change + process death ──
    var userInput by rememberSaveable { mutableStateOf("") }

    // ── derivedStateOf: only recomposes when the RESULT changes ──
    val items = remember { mutableStateListOf<String>() }
    val hasItems by remember { derivedStateOf { items.isNotEmpty() } }
    // Even if items changes from ["a","b"] to ["a","c"], hasItems doesn't recompose
    // because the boolean value (true) didn't change.

    // ── snapshotFlow: convert Compose state to a Flow ──
    val listState = rememberLazyListState()
    LaunchedEffect(listState) {
        snapshotFlow { listState.firstVisibleItemIndex }
            .distinctUntilChanged()
            .filter { it > 5 }
            .collect { /* Show "scroll to top" FAB */ }
    }

    // ── collectAsStateWithLifecycle: safely collect Flow as Compose state ──
    // Lifecycle-aware: stops collecting when UI goes to background
    // ✅ Use this instead of collectAsState()
    val viewModel: MyViewModel = hiltViewModel()
    val uiState by viewModel.uiState.collectAsStateWithLifecycle()
}

/**
 * ❓ Interview Question: What's the difference between remember and rememberSaveable?
 *
 *   remember:
 *     - Stores value in the Composition's slot table
 *     - Survives recomposition
 *     - Lost on configuration change (Activity recreation)
 *     - Can store ANY type
 *
 *   rememberSaveable:
 *     - Backed by onSaveInstanceState Bundle
 *     - Survives config change AND process death
 *     - Limited to Bundle-compatible types (primitives, Parcelable, Serializable)
 *     - Custom types need a Saver
 *
 * ❓ Follow-up: How do you save a custom class with rememberSaveable?
 */

// Custom Saver for rememberSaveable
data class ComposeSearchFilter(
    val query: String,
    val category: String,
    val sortBy: SortOrder
)

val ComposeSearchFilterSaver = run {
    val queryKey = "query"
    val categoryKey = "category"
    val sortKey = "sort"
    mapSaver(
        save = { filter ->
            mapOf(
                queryKey to filter.query,
                categoryKey to filter.category,
                sortKey to filter.sortBy.name
            )
        },
        restore = { map ->
            ComposeSearchFilter(
                query = map[queryKey] as String,
                category = map[categoryKey] as String,
                sortBy = SortOrder.valueOf(map[sortKey] as String)
            )
        }
    )
}

@Composable
fun SaveableSearchScreen() {
    var filter by rememberSaveable(stateSaver = ComposeSearchFilterSaver) {
        mutableStateOf(ComposeSearchFilter("", "all", SortOrder.NEWEST))
    }
}


// ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
// 4. STATE HOISTING
// ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

/**
 * State Hoisting = moving state UP to the caller so the composable is stateless.
 *
 * Pattern:
 *   - Stateful composable: owns state internally (via remember)
 *   - Stateless composable: receives state + event callbacks as parameters
 *
 * Benefits:
 *   ✅ Reusable — no internal state assumptions
 *   ✅ Testable — inject any state for testing
 *   ✅ Previewable — create @Preview with any state
 *   ✅ Single source of truth — parent controls the state
 *
 * Google's Rule: Hoist state to the lowest common ancestor that needs it.
 */

// ❌ Bad: Stateful — hard to test, can't control externally
@Composable
fun SearchBarBad() {
    var query by remember { mutableStateOf("") }
    TextField(value = query, onValueChange = { query = it })
}

// ✅ Good: Stateless — state hoisted to caller
@Composable
fun SearchBar(
    query: String,
    onQueryChange: (String) -> Unit,
    modifier: Modifier = Modifier
) {
    TextField(
        value = query,
        onValueChange = onQueryChange,
        modifier = modifier
    )
}

// Stateful wrapper if needed
@Composable
fun SearchBarStateful() {
    var query by rememberSaveable { mutableStateOf("") }
    SearchBar(query = query, onQueryChange = { query = it })
}


// ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
// 5. SIDE EFFECTS — COMPLETE GUIDE
// ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

/**
 * Side effect = any work that escapes the scope of a composable function.
 * Examples: API calls, analytics, registering listeners, writing to DB.
 *
 * Compose provides specific APIs because:
 *   - Composable functions can be called many times (recomposition)
 *   - They can be called in any order or concurrently
 *   - They can be skipped if the result is the same
 *
 * ⚠️ NEVER perform side effects directly in a composable body.
 */

@Composable
fun SideEffectsShowcase(userId: String, analyticsHelper: AnalyticsHelper) {

    // ── LaunchedEffect ──
    // Runs a suspend function when keys change. Cancels + restarts on key change.
    // Use for: API calls, one-time operations, coroutine-based work
    LaunchedEffect(userId) {
        // This cancels & restarts when userId changes
        val userData = repository.fetchUser(userId)
        // update state...
    }

    // ── LaunchedEffect(Unit) ──
    // Runs ONCE when composable enters composition. Never restarts.
    LaunchedEffect(Unit) {
        // One-time initialization
        analyticsHelper.logScreenView("profile")
    }

    // ── DisposableEffect ──
    // For registering/unregistering listeners. Has cleanup via onDispose.
    // Use for: Lifecycle observers, broadcast receivers, callbacks
    val lifecycleOwner = LocalLifecycleOwner.current
    DisposableEffect(lifecycleOwner) {
        val observer = LifecycleEventObserver { _, event ->
            when (event) {
                Lifecycle.Event.ON_RESUME -> { /* ... */ }
                Lifecycle.Event.ON_PAUSE -> { /* ... */ }
                else -> {}
            }
        }
        lifecycleOwner.lifecycle.addObserver(observer)
        onDispose {
            lifecycleOwner.lifecycle.removeObserver(observer)
        }
    }

    // ── SideEffect ──
    // Runs EVERY recomposition. No coroutine scope. Synchronous.
    // Use for: Updating non-Compose state that needs to stay in sync
    SideEffect {
        analyticsHelper.setUserProperty("screen", "profile_$userId")
    }

    // ── rememberCoroutineScope ──
    // Gets a CoroutineScope tied to the composable's lifecycle.
    // Use for: Launching coroutines from callbacks (onClick, etc.)
    val scope = rememberCoroutineScope()
    Button(onClick = {
        scope.launch {
            // Can do suspend work in response to user action
            repository.toggleBookmark(userId)
        }
    }) {
        Text("Bookmark")
    }

    // ── rememberUpdatedState ──
    // Captures the latest value of a parameter for use in long-running effects.
    // Prevents LaunchedEffect from capturing a stale closure.
    val currentOnTimeout by rememberUpdatedState(onTimeout)
    LaunchedEffect(Unit) {
        delay(5000)
        currentOnTimeout()  // Always calls the LATEST onTimeout, not the initial one
    }

    // ── produceState ──
    // Converts non-Compose state (Flow, callback) into Compose State.
    val imageState by produceState<ImageLoadState>(
        initialValue = ImageLoadState.Loading,
        key1 = imageUrl
    ) {
        value = try {
            val bitmap = imageLoader.load(imageUrl)
            ImageLoadState.Success(bitmap)
        } catch (e: Exception) {
            ImageLoadState.Error(e)
        }
    }
}

/**
 * ⚠️ GOTCHA — Side Effect Pitfalls:
 *
 * 1. LaunchedEffect(Unit) with changing parameters:
 *    If you use a parameter inside LaunchedEffect(Unit), it captures the
 *    INITIAL value. Use rememberUpdatedState or add the param to keys.
 *
 * 2. Forgetting onDispose in DisposableEffect:
 *    → Memory leak (listener not unregistered)
 *
 * 3. Performing network calls directly in composable:
 *    @Composable fun Bad() {
 *        val data = api.fetchData()  // ❌ Called on every recomposition!
 *    }
 *    → Use LaunchedEffect or move to ViewModel
 */


// ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
// 6. COMPOSE PHASES & LIFECYCLE
// ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

/**
 * Compose has 3 phases per frame:
 *
 *   1. Composition  → WHAT to show (runs @Composable functions, builds UI tree)
 *   2. Layout       → WHERE to place it (measure + place each node)
 *   3. Drawing      → HOW to render it (draws pixels to Canvas)
 *
 * Reading state in different phases:
 *   - State read in Composition → triggers recomposition
 *   - State read in Layout (via Modifier.layout) → triggers re-layout only
 *   - State read in Drawing (via Modifier.drawBehind) → triggers re-draw only
 *
 * 💡 Performance Tip: Defer state reads to the latest possible phase.
 *    If state only affects position, read it in Layout phase.
 *    If state only affects visuals, read it in Drawing phase.
 */

@Composable
fun PhaseOptimizationExample() {
    var offset by remember { mutableStateOf(0f) }

    // ❌ Reading offset in Composition — triggers full recomposition
    Box(modifier = Modifier.offset(x = offset.dp))

    // ✅ Reading offset in Layout phase — skips Composition
    Box(modifier = Modifier.offset { IntOffset(offset.toInt(), 0) })

    // ✅ Reading offset in Drawing phase — skips Composition AND Layout
    Box(modifier = Modifier.drawBehind {
        drawCircle(Color.Red, center = Offset(offset, 0f))
    })
}


// ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
// 7. STABILITY & PERFORMANCE
// ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

/**
 * For Compose to skip recomposition, it must know if parameters changed.
 * This requires types to be "stable" — their equals() is reliable.
 *
 * Automatically stable:
 *   - Primitives (Int, String, Boolean, Float, etc.)
 *   - data class with all stable fields
 *   - Enum classes
 *   - Function types (lambdas)
 *
 * Unstable (can't skip):
 *   - List, Set, Map (interfaces — implementation might be mutable)
 *   - Classes from external modules (Compose compiler can't verify)
 *   - var properties
 */

// ❌ Unstable — List<T> is an interface; could be mutable
@Composable
fun UnstableExample(items: List<String>) { /* Always recomposes */ }

// ✅ Stable — ImmutableList is known to be immutable
@Composable
fun StableExample(items: ImmutableList<String>) { /* Can skip recomposition */ }

// ✅ Annotate your own classes
@Immutable  // Promise to Compose: all fields are deeply immutable
data class ArticleUi(
    val id: String,
    val title: String,
    val author: String,
    val tags: ImmutableList<String>
)

@Stable  // Promise to Compose: mutable, but changes are observable via Compose State
class CartState {
    var itemCount by mutableStateOf(0)
        private set
    fun addItem() { itemCount++ }
}

/**
 * Lambda Stability:
 *
 *   ❌ Unstable lambda (captures unstable variable):
 *       items.forEach { item ->
 *           Button(onClick = { onItemClick(item) })  // New lambda each recomposition
 *       }
 *
 *   ✅ Stable lambda via remember:
 *       val onClick = remember(item.id) { { onItemClick(item) } }
 *       Button(onClick = onClick)
 *
 *   ✅ Or use method reference:
 *       Button(onClick = viewModel::onItemClick)
 */


// ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
// 8. COMPOSITIONLOCAL
// ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

/**
 * CompositionLocal provides implicit data down the composition tree.
 * Like React's Context — avoids threading parameters through every composable.
 *
 * Built-in examples:
 *   LocalContext.current           → Android Context
 *   LocalLifecycleOwner.current   → Lifecycle owner
 *   LocalDensity.current          → Screen density
 *   LocalConfiguration.current    → Device configuration
 *
 * staticCompositionLocalOf   → Value rarely changes (theme, DI). More efficient.
 * compositionLocalOf         → Value may change. Recomposes readers on change.
 */

// Custom CompositionLocal
val LocalAnalytics = staticCompositionLocalOf<AnalyticsHelper> {
    error("No AnalyticsHelper provided")
}

@Composable
fun AppRoot() {
    CompositionLocalProvider(LocalAnalytics provides AnalyticsHelperImpl()) {
        AppNavigation()  // All descendants can access LocalAnalytics.current
    }
}

@Composable
fun DeepNestedScreen() {
    val analytics = LocalAnalytics.current  // No parameter threading needed
    LaunchedEffect(Unit) {
        analytics.logScreenView("deep_nested")
    }
}

/**
 * ⚠️ When NOT to use CompositionLocal:
 *   - For data that only a few composables need (just pass as parameter)
 *   - For frequently changing data (performance overhead)
 *   - When it makes the composable harder to test
 *
 * ✅ Good uses: Theme, analytics, navigation, DI-provided dependencies
 */


// ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
// 9. CUSTOM LAYOUTS & MODIFIERS
// ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

/**
 * Custom Layout — when standard Row/Column/Box aren't enough.
 * You get full control over measuring and placing children.
 */

@Composable
fun StaggeredGrid(
    modifier: Modifier = Modifier,
    columns: Int = 2,
    content: @Composable () -> Unit
) {
    Layout(
        modifier = modifier,
        content = content
    ) { measurables, constraints ->
        val columnWidth = constraints.maxWidth / columns
        val itemConstraints = constraints.copy(
            minWidth = 0, maxWidth = columnWidth
        )

        val placeables = measurables.map { it.measure(itemConstraints) }
        val columnHeights = IntArray(columns)

        layout(constraints.maxWidth, constraints.maxHeight) {
            placeables.forEach { placeable ->
                val shortestColumn = columnHeights.indexOfMin()
                placeable.placeRelative(
                    x = shortestColumn * columnWidth,
                    y = columnHeights[shortestColumn]
                )
                columnHeights[shortestColumn] += placeable.height
            }
        }
    }
}

// Custom Modifier
fun Modifier.shimmer(isLoading: Boolean): Modifier = composed {
    if (!isLoading) return@composed this

    val transition = rememberInfiniteTransition(label = "shimmer")
    val translateAnim by transition.animateFloat(
        initialValue = 0f,
        targetValue = 1000f,
        animationSpec = infiniteRepeatable(
            animation = tween(durationMillis = 1200, easing = LinearEasing),
            repeatMode = RepeatMode.Restart
        ),
        label = "shimmer_translate"
    )

    this.drawWithContent {
        drawContent()
        val brush = Brush.linearGradient(
            colors = listOf(
                Color.LightGray.copy(alpha = 0.6f),
                Color.LightGray.copy(alpha = 0.2f),
                Color.LightGray.copy(alpha = 0.6f)
            ),
            start = Offset(translateAnim - 200f, 0f),
            end = Offset(translateAnim, 0f)
        )
        drawRect(brush = brush)
    }
}


// ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
// 10. COMPOSE COMPILER INTERNALS (ADVANCED)
// ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

/**
 * What the Compose compiler does to your @Composable functions:
 *
 * 1. Adds a Composer parameter to every @Composable function
 *    fun Greeting(name: String) → fun Greeting(name: String, $composer: Composer, $changed: Int)
 *
 * 2. Generates groups in the slot table:
 *    - startGroup / endGroup calls that track composable identity
 *    - Each call position gets a unique key
 *
 * 3. Adds comparison checks ($changed bitmask):
 *    - Tracks which parameters changed since last composition
 *    - If nothing changed, can skip the composable entirely
 *
 * 4. Transforms remember {} calls:
 *    - Stores/retrieves values from the slot table
 *    - Invalidates when keys change
 *
 * 5. Generates restartable groups:
 *    - Each composable that reads state becomes a "restart scope"
 *    - When state changes, only that scope re-executes
 *
 * 💡 Interview Tip: Understanding this helps explain WHY:
 *    - @Composable functions can only call other @Composable functions
 *    - Compose can skip unchanged composables
 *    - Stability matters for performance
 */


// ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
// 11. COMPOSE NAVIGATION
// ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

/**
 * Navigation in Compose replaces the Fragment-based Navigation component.
 *
 * Core components:
 *   NavController       → manages the back stack and navigation actions
 *   NavHost             → container that swaps composables based on the current route
 *   NavGraph            → set of destinations (composable routes)
 *   NavBackStackEntry   → holds arguments and scoped state for a destination
 *
 * 💡 Interview Tip: Know both the string-based route API and the newer
 *    type-safe Kotlin Serializable route API (Navigation 2.8+).
 */

// ── String-based route approach ──

@Composable
fun StringRouteNavigation() {
    val navController = rememberNavController()

    NavHost(
        navController = navController,
        startDestination = "home"
    ) {
        composable("home") {
            HomeScreen(
                onArticleClick = { articleId ->
                    navController.navigate("article/$articleId")
                },
                onProfileClick = {
                    navController.navigate("profile")
                }
            )
        }

        composable(
            route = "article/{articleId}",
            arguments = listOf(
                navArgument("articleId") { type = NavType.StringType }
            )
        ) { backStackEntry ->
            val articleId = backStackEntry.arguments?.getString("articleId") ?: return@composable
            ArticleDetailScreen(articleId = articleId)
        }

        // Optional arguments with default values
        composable(
            route = "search?query={query}&category={category}",
            arguments = listOf(
                navArgument("query") {
                    type = NavType.StringType
                    defaultValue = ""
                },
                navArgument("category") {
                    type = NavType.StringType
                    defaultValue = "all"
                }
            )
        ) { backStackEntry ->
            val query = backStackEntry.arguments?.getString("query") ?: ""
            val category = backStackEntry.arguments?.getString("category") ?: "all"
            SearchScreen(initialQuery = query, category = category)
        }

        // Deep link support
        composable(
            route = "article/{articleId}",
            deepLinks = listOf(
                navDeepLink { uriPattern = "https://myapp.com/article/{articleId}" }
            )
        ) { /* ... */ }
    }
}

// ── Type-safe route approach (Navigation 2.8+, Kotlin Serializable) ──

@Serializable
data class ArticleRoute(val articleId: String)

@Serializable
data class SearchRoute(
    val query: String = "",
    val category: String = "all"
)

@Serializable
object HomeRoute

@Serializable
object ProfileRoute

@Composable
fun TypeSafeNavigation() {
    val navController = rememberNavController()

    NavHost(
        navController = navController,
        startDestination = HomeRoute
    ) {
        composable<HomeRoute> {
            HomeScreen(
                onArticleClick = { id -> navController.navigate(ArticleRoute(id)) }
            )
        }

        composable<ArticleRoute> { backStackEntry ->
            val route = backStackEntry.toRoute<ArticleRoute>()
            ArticleDetailScreen(articleId = route.articleId)
        }

        composable<SearchRoute> { backStackEntry ->
            val route = backStackEntry.toRoute<SearchRoute>()
            SearchScreen(initialQuery = route.query, category = route.category)
        }
    }
}

/**
 * Scoping ViewModels to a NavGraph:
 *   By default, ViewModels are scoped to the NavBackStackEntry of a destination.
 *   You can scope them to a nested nav graph to share across screens.
 */

@Composable
fun SharedViewModelExample(navController: NavHostController) {
    val parentEntry = remember(navController) {
        navController.getBackStackEntry("checkout_graph")
    }
    val sharedViewModel: CheckoutViewModel = hiltViewModel(parentEntry)
}

/**
 * Custom back handling:
 *   BackHandler from activity-compose intercepts the system back button.
 */

@Composable
fun ScreenWithCustomBack(onBack: () -> Unit) {
    var hasUnsavedChanges by remember { mutableStateOf(false) }
    var showDialog by remember { mutableStateOf(false) }

    BackHandler(enabled = hasUnsavedChanges) {
        showDialog = true
    }

    if (showDialog) {
        AlertDialog(
            onDismissRequest = { showDialog = false },
            confirmButton = {
                TextButton(onClick = { showDialog = false; onBack() }) {
                    Text("Discard")
                }
            },
            dismissButton = {
                TextButton(onClick = { showDialog = false }) { Text("Cancel") }
            },
            title = { Text("Unsaved changes") },
            text = { Text("Discard your changes?") }
        )
    }
}

/**
 * ⚠️ Navigation Gotchas:
 *   1. Avoid creating a new NavController on recomposition — always use rememberNavController()
 *   2. navigate() adds to the back stack by default; use popUpTo for login/splash flows
 *   3. Don't pass complex objects as arguments — pass IDs and fetch from ViewModel/repository
 *   4. Use launchSingleTop = true to avoid duplicate destinations on rapid taps
 */


// ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
// 12. MATERIAL 3, THEMING & ACCESSIBILITY
// ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

/**
 * Material 3 (Material You) in Compose:
 *
 * MaterialTheme provides:
 *   colorScheme  → color tokens (primary, secondary, surface, error, etc.)
 *   typography   → text styles (displayLarge, titleMedium, bodySmall, etc.)
 *   shapes       → corner rounding (small, medium, large, extraLarge)
 *
 * Dynamic Color (Material You — Android 12+):
 *   Colors derived from the user's wallpaper. Use dynamicDarkColorScheme()
 *   and dynamicLightColorScheme() with a fallback for older devices.
 */

@Composable
fun AppTheme(
    darkTheme: Boolean = isSystemInDarkTheme(),
    dynamicColor: Boolean = true,
    content: @Composable () -> Unit
) {
    val colorScheme = when {
        dynamicColor && Build.VERSION.SDK_INT >= Build.VERSION_CODES.S -> {
            val context = LocalContext.current
            if (darkTheme) dynamicDarkColorScheme(context) else dynamicLightColorScheme(context)
        }
        darkTheme -> darkColorScheme(
            primary = Color(0xFFBB86FC),
            secondary = Color(0xFF03DAC6),
            background = Color(0xFF121212)
        )
        else -> lightColorScheme(
            primary = Color(0xFF6200EE),
            secondary = Color(0xFF03DAC6),
            background = Color(0xFFFFFBFE)
        )
    }

    MaterialTheme(
        colorScheme = colorScheme,
        typography = Typography(
            titleLarge = TextStyle(
                fontWeight = FontWeight.Bold,
                fontSize = 22.sp,
                lineHeight = 28.sp
            )
        ),
        shapes = Shapes(
            small = RoundedCornerShape(4.dp),
            medium = RoundedCornerShape(12.dp),
            large = RoundedCornerShape(16.dp)
        ),
        content = content
    )
}

/**
 * Accessibility in Compose:
 *
