 * Compose uses a Semantics tree (parallel to the UI tree) that screen readers
 * (TalkBack) and testing tools use to understand your UI.
 *
 * Key APIs:
 *   contentDescription     → describes non-text elements (images, icons)
 *   semantics { }          → attach semantic properties to a composable
 *   mergeDescendants       → treat a subtree as a single accessible element
 *   clearAndSetSemantics   → override all child semantics (useful for custom widgets)
 *
 * ⚠️ Touch target size: Material guidelines require at least 48dp × 48dp.
 *    Use Modifier.sizeIn(minWidth = 48.dp, minHeight = 48.dp) or
 *    Modifier.minimumInteractiveComponentSize() (Material 3).
 */

@Composable
fun AccessibilityExample() {
    // contentDescription for images
    Image(
        painter = painterResource(R.drawable.logo),
        contentDescription = "App logo",  // Read by TalkBack
        modifier = Modifier.size(48.dp)
    )

    // Decorative image — null contentDescription is intentional
    Icon(
        imageVector = Icons.Default.Star,
        contentDescription = null  // Ignored by screen readers
    )

    // mergeDescendants — card read as a single element
    Row(
        modifier = Modifier.semantics(mergeDescendants = true) { }
    ) {
        Image(
            painter = painterResource(R.drawable.avatar),
            contentDescription = null  // Merged into parent
        )
        Column {
            Text("Jane Doe")
            Text("Senior Engineer")
        }
    }
    // TalkBack reads: "Jane Doe, Senior Engineer" as one item

    // clearAndSetSemantics — replace all child semantics
    Row(
        modifier = Modifier.clearAndSetSemantics {
            contentDescription = "Rating: 4 out of 5 stars"
        }
    ) {
        repeat(5) { index ->
            Icon(
                imageVector = if (index < 4) Icons.Filled.Star else Icons.Outlined.Star,
                contentDescription = null
            )
        }
    }

    // Minimum touch target
    IconButton(
        onClick = { /* ... */ },
        modifier = Modifier.minimumInteractiveComponentSize()
    ) {
        Icon(Icons.Default.Close, contentDescription = "Close")
    }
}

/**
 * Focus management for screen readers:
 *   Use FocusRequester to programmatically move focus after state changes
 *   (e.g., after showing an error, move focus to the error message).
 */

@Composable
fun FocusManagementExample() {
    val focusRequester = remember { FocusRequester() }
    var showError by remember { mutableStateOf(false) }

    Column {
        if (showError) {
            Text(
                "Invalid input",
                color = MaterialTheme.colorScheme.error,
                modifier = Modifier.focusRequester(focusRequester)
            )
            LaunchedEffect(Unit) {
                focusRequester.requestFocus()
            }
        }
        TextField(value = "", onValueChange = {})
    }
}

/**
 * 💡 Interview Tip: Google cares deeply about accessibility. Be ready to discuss:
 *    - How you'd make a custom component accessible
 *    - The difference between contentDescription and text for semantics
 *    - How mergeDescendants affects the TalkBack traversal order
 *    - Testing accessibility with Accessibility Scanner or Espresso
 */


// ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
// 13. COMPOSE INTEROP
// ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

/**
 * Compose ↔ View system interoperability is essential for incremental migration.
 *
 * Two directions:
 *   1. Compose inside Views:  ComposeView (embed Compose in Fragment/Activity XML)
 *   2. Views inside Compose:  AndroidView (embed legacy View widgets in Compose)
 *
 * 💡 Interview Tip: Google's recommended migration strategy is bottom-up —
 *    start by replacing individual screens or leaf components with Compose,
 *    while the Activity/Fragment shell remains.
 */

// ── AndroidView: Embedding a View widget inside Compose ──

@Composable
fun MapComposable(
    onMapReady: (MapView) -> Unit,
    modifier: Modifier = Modifier
) {
    var mapView: MapView? by remember { mutableStateOf(null) }

    AndroidView(
        factory = { context ->
            MapView(context).also {
                it.onCreate(null)
                mapView = it
            }
        },
        update = { view ->
            // Called on every recomposition — update the View's state here
            view.getMapAsync { googleMap ->
                onMapReady(view)
            }
        },
        modifier = modifier
    )

    // Handle lifecycle for the MapView
    val lifecycleOwner = LocalLifecycleOwner.current
    DisposableEffect(lifecycleOwner) {
        val observer = LifecycleEventObserver { _, event ->
            when (event) {
                Lifecycle.Event.ON_RESUME -> mapView?.onResume()
                Lifecycle.Event.ON_PAUSE -> mapView?.onPause()
                Lifecycle.Event.ON_DESTROY -> mapView?.onDestroy()
                else -> {}
            }
        }
        lifecycleOwner.lifecycle.addObserver(observer)
        onDispose { lifecycleOwner.lifecycle.removeObserver(observer) }
    }
}

// Wrapping an AdView (common real-world interop case)
@Composable
fun AdBanner(adUnitId: String, modifier: Modifier = Modifier) {
    AndroidView(
        factory = { context ->
            AdView(context).apply {
                setAdSize(AdSize.BANNER)
                this.adUnitId = adUnitId
                loadAd(AdRequest.Builder().build())
            }
        },
        modifier = modifier
    )
}

// ── ComposeView: Embedding Compose inside a Fragment ──

class ProfileFragment : Fragment() {
    override fun onCreateView(
        inflater: LayoutInflater,
        container: ViewGroup?,
        savedInstanceState: Bundle?
    ): View {
        return ComposeView(requireContext()).apply {
            setViewCompositionStrategy(
                ViewCompositionStrategy.DisposeOnViewTreeLifecycleDestroyed
            )
            setContent {
                AppTheme {
                    ProfileScreen()
                }
            }
        }
    }
}

/**
 * ViewCompositionStrategy — controls when Compose disposes:
 *
 *   DisposeOnDetachedFromWindow        → default; disposes when view detaches
 *   DisposeOnDetachedFromWindowOrReleasedFromPool → for RecyclerView items
 *   DisposeOnViewTreeLifecycleDestroyed → ✅ best for Fragments (matches view lifecycle)
 *   DisposeOnLifecycleDestroyed        → ties to a specific Lifecycle
 *
 * ⚠️ Common Pitfalls:
 *   1. Theme inheritance: ComposeView doesn't inherit Material theme from XML.
 *      You must wrap content in your Compose theme.
 *   2. Lifecycle mismatch: Fragment's view lifecycle ≠ Fragment lifecycle.
 *      Always use DisposeOnViewTreeLifecycleDestroyed in Fragments.
 *   3. Inflation order: If mixing XML and Compose in the same layout,
 *      remember that AndroidView factory runs once, update runs on recomposition.
 *   4. Keyboard handling: IME behavior can differ between View and Compose text fields.
 */


// ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
// PRACTICE QUESTIONS
// ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

/**
 * ❓ Q1: What's the difference between State and MutableState?
 *    A: State<T> is read-only interface. MutableState<T> extends it with a setter.
 *       Expose State<T> publicly, keep MutableState<T> private — same as
 *       StateFlow/MutableStateFlow pattern.
 *
 * ❓ Q2: Why shouldn't you use collectAsState for StateFlow from a ViewModel?
 *    A: collectAsState keeps collecting even when the app is in background.
 *       Use collectAsStateWithLifecycle() instead — it stops collecting in
 *       onStop and resumes in onStart, saving resources.
 *
 * ❓ Q3: What happens if you use var x = 0 instead of mutableStateOf?
 *    A: Compose won't track changes. The variable will change but
 *       recomposition won't be triggered. UI stays stale.
 *
 * ❓ Q4: How do you animate between two screens in Compose?
 *    A: AnimatedNavHost (Navigation Compose + animation), AnimatedContent,
 *       Crossfade, or SharedElement transitions (Compose 1.7+).
 *
 * ❓ Q5: What is the Snapshot system in Compose?
 *    A: Compose uses a snapshot system (similar to software transactional memory)
 *       to track state reads/writes. When mutableStateOf() is read during
 *       composition, the snapshot records it. When written to, all compositions
 *       that read it are scheduled for recomposition.
 *
 * ❓ Q6: How do you handle lists in Compose efficiently?
 *    A: LazyColumn/LazyRow with key parameter for stable identity.
 *       Use ImmutableList for skippable recomposition.
 *       Use derivedStateOf for computed list properties.
 *       Avoid creating new list instances in every recomposition.
 *
 * ❓ Q7: How would you implement a multi-screen checkout flow with shared state
 *        using Compose Navigation?
 *    A: Create a nested NavGraph for the checkout flow. Scope a shared
 *       CheckoutViewModel to the nested graph's NavBackStackEntry using
 *       hiltViewModel(navController.getBackStackEntry("checkout_graph")).
 *       All screens in the graph share the same ViewModel instance.
 *       On completion, pop the entire graph: navController.popBackStack("checkout_graph", inclusive = true).
 *
 * ❓ Q8: How do you make a custom rating bar accessible for TalkBack users?
 *    A: Use clearAndSetSemantics on the container to replace individual star
 *       semantics with a single description like "Rating: 4 out of 5 stars".
 *       Also add a stateDescription for the current value and an adjustable
 *       action (via semantics { }) so users can change the rating with
 *       swipe-up/swipe-down gestures in TalkBack.
 */

04 Coroutines And Flows

04_coroutines_and_flows.kt · Kotlin reference

/**
 * ============================================================================
 * 04 — KOTLIN COROUTINES & FLOWS
 * Google SDE-2 Interview Prep
 * ============================================================================
 *
 * Topics Covered:
 *   1. Coroutine Fundamentals (suspend, Scope, Context, Job)
 *   2. Dispatchers (Main, IO, Default, Unconfined)
 *   3. Structured Concurrency
 *   4. launch vs async — Concurrency Patterns
 *   5. Exception Handling (try-catch, SupervisorJob, CEH)
 *   6. Cancellation & Cooperative Cancellation
 *   7. Flow — Cold Streams
 *   8. StateFlow & SharedFlow — Hot Streams
 *   9. Flow Operators & Transformations
 *  10. Coroutines in Android (viewModelScope, lifecycleScope)
 *  11. Channel — Communication Between Coroutines
 *  12. Mutex & Thread Safety
 *  13. Testing Coroutines
 *  14. callbackFlow & channelFlow
 *  15. shareIn vs stateIn
 *
 * ============================================================================
 */

// ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
// 1. COROUTINE FUNDAMENTALS
// ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

/**
 * What is a coroutine?
 *   A lightweight thread that can be SUSPENDED without blocking.
 *   Millions of coroutines can run on just a few threads.
 *
 * Key concepts:
 *   suspend function → can be paused and resumed
 *   CoroutineScope   → lifecycle boundary for coroutines
 *   CoroutineContext  → set of elements: Job + Dispatcher + Name + ExceptionHandler
 *   Job              → handle to a coroutine; used for cancellation
 *
 * ❓ Interview Question: What's the difference between a thread and a coroutine?
 *
 *   Thread: OS-level, heavy (~1MB stack), blocks when waiting
 *   Coroutine: Library-level, lightweight (~few bytes), suspends when waiting
 *
 *   A suspended coroutine does NOT occupy a thread. The thread is returned
 *   to the pool and can execute other coroutines.
 */

// Basic coroutine anatomy
suspend fun fetchUserProfile(userId: String): UserProfile {
    // This function can be suspended at any suspension point (delay, withContext, etc.)
    // The calling thread is freed during suspension
    val user = userApi.getUser(userId)           // Suspension point
    val posts = postApi.getUserPosts(userId)      // Suspension point
    return UserProfile(user, posts)
}


// ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
// 2. DISPATCHERS
// ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

/**
 * Dispatchers determine WHICH thread(s) a coroutine runs on.
 *
 * Dispatchers.Main:
 *   - Main/UI thread
 *   - Use for: UI updates, lightweight work
 *   - ⚠️ Never do heavy work here (ANR after 5 seconds)
 *
 * Dispatchers.IO:
 *   - Shared pool of threads (default: 64 threads)
 *   - Use for: Disk I/O, network calls, database operations
 *   - Optimized for blocking I/O operations
 *
 * Dispatchers.Default:
 *   - Shared pool = number of CPU cores
 *   - Use for: CPU-intensive work (JSON parsing, sorting, image processing)
 *   - ⚠️ Don't use for I/O — limited thread count can cause congestion
 *
 * Dispatchers.Unconfined:
 *   - Starts in caller's thread, resumes in whatever thread the suspending function used
 *   - ⚠️ Rarely used in production. Mainly for testing.
 *
 * 💡 Interview Tip: Always explain your Dispatcher choice and WHY.
 */

class UserRepository(
    private val api: UserApi,
    private val dao: UserDao,
    private val dispatchers: DispatcherProvider  // Inject for testability
) {
    suspend fun getUser(id: String): User = withContext(dispatchers.io) {
        // Network + DB operations → IO dispatcher
        val cachedUser = dao.getUser(id)
        if (cachedUser != null && !cachedUser.isStale()) {
            return@withContext cachedUser.toDomain()
        }
        val networkUser = api.fetchUser(id)
        dao.upsert(networkUser.toEntity())
        networkUser.toDomain()
    }

    suspend fun processUserData(users: List<User>): AnalyticsReport {
        return withContext(dispatchers.default) {
            // CPU-intensive → Default dispatcher
            val grouped = users.groupBy { it.region }
            val aggregated = grouped.mapValues { (_, users) ->
                computeMetrics(users)  // Heavy computation
            }
            AnalyticsReport(aggregated)
        }
    }
}

// Inject dispatchers for testability
interface DispatcherProvider {
    val main: CoroutineDispatcher
    val io: CoroutineDispatcher
    val default: CoroutineDispatcher
}

class DefaultDispatcherProvider : DispatcherProvider {
    override val main = Dispatchers.Main
    override val io = Dispatchers.IO
    override val default = Dispatchers.Default
}


// ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
// 3. STRUCTURED CONCURRENCY
// ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

/**
 * Structured concurrency = coroutines form a parent-child hierarchy.
 *
 * Rules:
 *   1. A parent coroutine waits for ALL children to complete
 *   2. When a parent is cancelled, ALL children are cancelled
 *   3. When a child fails, the parent (and siblings) are cancelled
 *      (unless using SupervisorJob)
 *   4. Coroutines can only be launched in a CoroutineScope
 *
 * This prevents:
 *   ❌ Leaked coroutines (forgotten background work)
 *   ❌ Lost exceptions (unhandled failures)
 *   ❌ Zombie work (work continues after screen is destroyed)
 *
 * ❓ Interview Question: What happens if you use GlobalScope.launch?
 *
 *   Answer: It breaks structured concurrency. The coroutine:
 *   - Lives for the entire app process
 *   - Is NOT cancelled when the screen/ViewModel is destroyed
 *   - Exceptions are not propagated to any parent
 *   - Can cause memory leaks and wasted resources
 */

// ✅ Structured: coroutines are children of viewModelScope
class StructuredViewModel : ViewModel() {
    init {
        viewModelScope.launch {
            // Automatically cancelled when ViewModel is cleared
            val user = fetchUser()
            val posts = fetchPosts(user.id)
        }
    }
}

// ❌ Unstructured: leaked coroutine
class LeakyViewModel : ViewModel() {
    init {
        GlobalScope.launch {
            // ⚠️ This keeps running even after ViewModel is destroyed
            while (true) {
                pollForUpdates()
                delay(30_000)
            }
        }
    }
}


// ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
// 4. LAUNCH VS ASYNC — CONCURRENCY PATTERNS
// ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

/**
 * launch → Fire-and-forget. Returns a Job. No result.
 * async  → Returns a Deferred<T>. Call await() to get the result.
 *
 * Key difference: async is for PARALLEL decomposition.
 */

// Sequential execution (slow)
suspend fun loadProfileSequential(userId: String): ProfileData {
    val user = fetchUser(userId)         // 500ms
    val posts = fetchPosts(userId)       // 300ms
    val followers = fetchFollowers(userId) // 400ms
    // Total: ~1200ms
    return ProfileData(user, posts, followers)
}

// Parallel execution with async (fast)
suspend fun loadProfileParallel(userId: String): ProfileData {
    return coroutineScope {
        val userDeferred = async { fetchUser(userId) }           // Starts immediately
        val postsDeferred = async { fetchPosts(userId) }         // Starts immediately
        val followersDeferred = async { fetchFollowers(userId) } // Starts immediately
        // Total: ~500ms (max of the three)
        ProfileData(
            user = userDeferred.await(),
            posts = postsDeferred.await(),
            followers = followersDeferred.await()
        )
    }
}

/**
 * ⚠️ GOTCHA: async without await
 *   If you launch async but never call await(), exceptions are SWALLOWED.
 *   The coroutine fails silently.
 *
 * ⚠️ GOTCHA: coroutineScope vs supervisorScope with async
 *   coroutineScope: if ONE async fails, ALL siblings are cancelled
 *   supervisorScope: siblings continue even if one fails
 */

// supervisorScope — partial failure is acceptable
suspend fun loadProfileWithGracefulDegradation(userId: String): ProfileData {
    return supervisorScope {
        val user = async { fetchUser(userId) }
        val posts = async {
            try { fetchPosts(userId) } catch (e: Exception) { emptyList() }
        }
        val followers = async {
            try { fetchFollowers(userId) } catch (e: Exception) { emptyList() }
        }
        // User is required, but posts/followers degrade gracefully
        ProfileData(user.await(), posts.await(), followers.await())
    }
}


// ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
// 5. EXCEPTION HANDLING
// ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

/**
 * Exception propagation rules:
 *
 * launch:
 *   - Exception propagates UP to the parent scope
 *   - Parent cancels all children, then fails itself
 *   - CoroutineExceptionHandler catches it (if installed on scope)
 *
 * async:
 *   - Exception is DEFERRED until await() is called
 *   - Still propagates to parent (cancelling siblings)
 *   - try-catch around await() handles it
 *
 * SupervisorJob / supervisorScope:
 *   - Child failure does NOT cancel parent or siblings
 *   - Each child must handle its own exceptions
 */

// Pattern 1: try-catch in the coroutine
class ExceptionHandlingViewModel(
    private val repository: DataRepository
) : ViewModel() {

    private val _uiState = MutableStateFlow<UiState>(UiState.Loading)

    fun loadData() {
        viewModelScope.launch {
            try {
                val result = repository.fetchData()
                _uiState.value = UiState.Success(result)
            } catch (e: HttpException) {
                _uiState.value = UiState.Error("Server error: ${e.code()}")
            } catch (e: IOException) {
                _uiState.value = UiState.Error("Network error")
            }
        }
    }

    // Pattern 2: CoroutineExceptionHandler (last resort, doesn't recover)
    private val handler = CoroutineExceptionHandler { _, throwable ->
        Log.e("VM", "Unhandled", throwable)
        _uiState.value = UiState.Error(throwable.message)
    }

    fun loadDataWithHandler() {
        viewModelScope.launch(handler) {
            repository.fetchData()
        }
    }
}

// Pattern 3: Result wrapper (recommended for repositories)
class SafeRepository(private val api: Api) {
    suspend fun fetchData(): Result<Data> {
        return try {
            val data = api.getData()
            Result.success(data)
        } catch (e: Exception) {
            Result.failure(e)
        }
    }
}

/**
 * ❓ Interview Question: What happens with this code?
 *
 *   viewModelScope.launch {
 *       launch { throw RuntimeException("crash") }
 *       launch { delay(1000); println("never printed") }
 *   }
 *
 *   Answer: First child throws → parent cancels second child → "never printed"
 *   is never printed. If there's no CoroutineExceptionHandler, app crashes.
 *
 * ❓ Follow-up: How do you prevent the second child from being cancelled?
 *
 *   Answer: Use supervisorScope:
 *   viewModelScope.launch {
 *       supervisorScope {
 *           launch { throw RuntimeException("crash") }
 *           launch { delay(1000); println("this WILL print") }
 *       }
 *   }
 */


// ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
// 6. CANCELLATION
// ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

/**
 * Cancellation is COOPERATIVE in Kotlin coroutines.
 * A coroutine must CHECK for cancellation to actually stop.
 *
 * Automatic checks happen at:
 *   - Every suspension point (delay, withContext, yield, channel operations)
 *   - isActive check in loops
 *
 * ⚠️ CPU-bound loops without suspension points NEVER cancel!
 */

// ❌ Won't cancel — no suspension points
suspend fun cpuBoundBad() = withContext(Dispatchers.Default) {
    var i = 0
    while (i < 1_000_000_000) {
        i++  // No suspension point — cancellation not checked
    }
}

// ✅ Cooperative cancellation
suspend fun cpuBoundGood() = withContext(Dispatchers.Default) {
    var i = 0
    while (i < 1_000_000_000) {
        i++
        if (i % 1000 == 0) ensureActive()  // Check for cancellation
    }
}

// ✅ Alternative: Use yield()
suspend fun cpuBoundAlternative() = withContext(Dispatchers.Default) {
    var i = 0
    while (i < 1_000_000_000) {
        i++
        if (i % 1000 == 0) yield()  // Checks cancellation + gives other coroutines a turn
    }
}

/**
 * NonCancellable — for cleanup that MUST complete even during cancellation
 *
 * When a coroutine is cancelled, its Job enters "cancelling" state.
 * New suspend calls throw CancellationException immediately.
 * Use NonCancellable to run critical cleanup.
 */
suspend fun fetchAndCache(id: String) {
    try {
        val data = api.fetch(id)
        cache.store(id, data)
    } finally {
        // This runs even on cancellation
        withContext(NonCancellable) {
            // Can call suspend functions here despite cancellation
            cache.markComplete(id)
            analytics.logCompletion(id)
        }
    }
}


// ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
// 7. FLOW — COLD STREAMS
// ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

/**
 * Flow is a cold, asynchronous stream that emits values sequentially.
 *
 * Cold = doesn't produce values until collected. Each collector gets
 *        its own independent execution of the flow.
 *
 * Think of it as: suspend fun that returns multiple values over time.
 */

// Creating flows
fun searchResults(query: String): Flow<List<SearchResult>> = flow {
    // This block runs for EACH collector independently
    val results = api.search(query)
    emit(results)

    // Can emit multiple values over time
    delay(5000)
    val freshResults = api.search(query)
    emit(freshResults)
}

// Flow from Room (automatically emits when DB changes)
@Dao
interface ArticleDao {
    @Query("SELECT * FROM articles ORDER BY created_at DESC")
    fun observeArticles(): Flow<List<ArticleEntity>>
}

// ❓ Interview Question: What's the difference between Flow and suspend function?
//    suspend fun → single value
//    Flow        → multiple values over time (stream)


// ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
// 8. STATEFLOW & SHAREDFLOW — HOT STREAMS
// ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

/**
 * StateFlow:
 *   - Hot stream (emits regardless of collectors)
 *   - Always has a current value (initial value required)
 *   - Conflated: only the LATEST value is delivered
 *   - Uses equality check: won't emit if new value == old value
 *   - ✅ Perfect for UI state
 *
 * SharedFlow:
 *   - Hot stream
 *   - No initial value required
 *   - Configurable replay cache and buffer
 *   - Does NOT use equality check — always emits
 *   - ✅ Perfect for events/signals
 *
 * ┌───────────────────┬──────────────────┬──────────────────┐
 * │ Feature           │ StateFlow        │ SharedFlow       │
 * ├───────────────────┼──────────────────┼──────────────────┤
 * │ Initial value     │ Required         │ Optional         │
 * │ Replay            │ Always 1 (latest)│ Configurable     │
 * │ Equality check    │ Yes (conflated)  │ No               │
 * │ Use case          │ UI State         │ Events, signals  │
 * │ Collectors missed │ Only latest value│ Depends on buffer│
 * │ values            │ matters          │ config           │
 * └───────────────────┴──────────────────┴──────────────────┘
 */

class EventDrivenViewModel : ViewModel() {

    // StateFlow for UI state — always has latest value
    private val _uiState = MutableStateFlow(ScreenUiState())
    val uiState: StateFlow<ScreenUiState> = _uiState.asStateFlow()

    // SharedFlow for one-time events — replay = 0 means no re-delivery
    private val _events = MutableSharedFlow<UiEvent>(
        replay = 0,
        extraBufferCapacity = 1,
        onBufferOverflow = BufferOverflow.DROP_OLDEST
    )
    val events: SharedFlow<UiEvent> = _events.asSharedFlow()

    // ✅ Alternative for events: Channel (guarantees delivery to one collector)
    private val _navigationEvents = Channel<NavigationEvent>(Channel.BUFFERED)
    val navigationEvents = _navigationEvents.receiveAsFlow()

    fun onSubmit() {
        viewModelScope.launch {
            val result = repository.submit()
            if (result.isSuccess) {
                _navigationEvents.send(NavigationEvent.GoToSuccess)
            } else {
                _events.emit(UiEvent.ShowError(result.exceptionOrNull()?.message ?: "Error"))
            }
        }
    }
}

/**
 * ❓ Interview Question: Why use Channel instead of SharedFlow for navigation events?
 *
 *   Answer: Channel guarantees exactly-once delivery to a single collector.
 *   SharedFlow with replay=0 can LOSE events if no collector is active
 *   (e.g., during configuration change). SharedFlow with replay=1 can
 *   RE-DELIVER events after config change.
 *   Channel buffers the event and delivers when a collector is available.
 */


// ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
// 9. FLOW OPERATORS
// ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

/**
 * Essential Flow operators for interviews:
 */

fun flowOperatorsShowcase() {
    val searchFlow: Flow<String> = TODO()

    // map — transform each value
    searchFlow.map { query -> query.trim().lowercase() }

    // filter — keep values matching predicate
    searchFlow.filter { it.length >= 3 }

    // debounce — wait for pause in emissions (search-as-you-type)
    searchFlow.debounce(300)

    // distinctUntilChanged — skip consecutive duplicates
    searchFlow.distinctUntilChanged()

    // flatMapLatest — cancel previous inner flow when new value arrives
    searchFlow
        .debounce(300)
        .filter { it.isNotBlank() }
        .distinctUntilChanged()
        .flatMapLatest { query ->
            // Previous search is CANCELLED when new query arrives
            repository.search(query)
        }

    // combine — merge multiple flows, emit when ANY flow emits
    val filterFlow: StateFlow<Filter> = TODO()
    val sortFlow: StateFlow<SortOrder> = TODO()
    val itemsFlow: Flow<List<Item>> = TODO()

    combine(itemsFlow, filterFlow, sortFlow) { items, filter, sort ->
        items.filter { filter.matches(it) }
             .sortedWith(sort.comparator)
    }

    // zip — pair values 1:1 from two flows
    val namesFlow = flowOf("Alice", "Bob")
    val agesFlow = flowOf(30, 25)
    namesFlow.zip(agesFlow) { name, age -> "$name is $age" }

    // catch — handle upstream errors
    searchFlow
        .map { riskyOperation(it) }
        .catch { e -> emit("Error: ${e.message}") }

    // flowOn — change dispatcher for UPSTREAM operations
    flow {
        emit(heavyComputation())  // Runs on Default
    }.flowOn(Dispatchers.Default)
     .collect { /* Runs on caller's dispatcher (Main) */ }

    // onEach — perform action for each value without consuming
    searchFlow.onEach { analytics.log("search: $it") }

    // stateIn — convert cold Flow to StateFlow
    val stateFlow: StateFlow<List<Item>> = itemsFlow
        .stateIn(
            scope = viewModelScope,
            started = SharingStarted.WhileSubscribed(5000),
            initialValue = emptyList()
        )
}

/**
 * SharingStarted strategies:
 *
 *   Eagerly          → starts immediately, never stops
 *   Lazily           → starts on first subscriber, never stops
 *   WhileSubscribed  → starts on first subscriber, stops when last unsubscribes
 *     - stopTimeoutMillis: delay before stopping (default 0)
 *
 * 💡 WhileSubscribed(5000) is the recommended default:
 *    - Survives quick config changes (5s buffer)
 *    - Stops when screen is truly gone (saves resources)
 */


// ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
// 10. COROUTINES IN ANDROID
// ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

/**
 * viewModelScope:
 *   - Uses Dispatchers.Main.immediate
 *   - Cancelled in ViewModel.onCleared()
 *   - ✅ Use for all ViewModel coroutines
 *
 * lifecycleScope:
 *   - Uses Dispatchers.Main.immediate
 *   - Cancelled in onDestroy()
 *   - ✅ Use for UI-related coroutines in Activity/Fragment
 *
 * repeatOnLifecycle:
 *   - Restarts the block when lifecycle reaches given state
 *   - Cancels when lifecycle falls below that state
 *   - ✅ The correct way to collect Flows in Activities/Fragments
 */

// ✅ Correct way to collect Flow in Fragment
class ArticleFragment : Fragment() {
    private val viewModel: ArticleViewModel by viewModels()

    override fun onViewCreated(view: View, savedInstanceState: Bundle?) {
        super.onViewCreated(view, savedInstanceState)

        viewLifecycleOwner.lifecycleScope.launch {
            viewLifecycleOwner.repeatOnLifecycle(Lifecycle.State.STARTED) {
                // This block:
                // - Starts when lifecycle reaches STARTED
                // - Cancels when lifecycle drops below STARTED (onStop)
                // - Restarts when lifecycle reaches STARTED again (onStart)
                viewModel.uiState.collect { state ->
                    updateUI(state)
                }
            }
        }

        // Collecting multiple flows in parallel
        viewLifecycleOwner.lifecycleScope.launch {
            viewLifecycleOwner.repeatOnLifecycle(Lifecycle.State.STARTED) {
                launch { viewModel.uiState.collect { updateUI(it) } }
                launch { viewModel.events.collect { handleEvent(it) } }
            }
        }
    }
}

/**
 * ❓ Why not just use lifecycleScope.launch { flow.collect {} }?
 *
 *    Because it only cancels on DESTROY, not STOP.
 *    Your app keeps processing updates while in the background,
 *    wasting CPU/battery and potentially crashing (updating invisible UI).
 *
 *    repeatOnLifecycle(STARTED) cancels collection in onStop
 *    and restarts in onStart — matching what the user can see.
 */


// ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
// 11. CHANNEL — INTER-COROUTINE COMMUNICATION
// ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

/**
 * Channel = a concurrent queue for sending values between coroutines.
 *
 * Types:
 *   RENDEZVOUS (0)   → sender suspends until receiver is ready (handshake)
 *   BUFFERED (64)    → buffer of 64 elements before suspending
 *   UNLIMITED        → unlimited buffer (can cause OOM)
 *   CONFLATED        → only keeps the latest value (drops old ones)
 *
 * ✅ Use Channel for: one-time events, fan-out, producer-consumer patterns
 * ❌ Don't use for: observable state (use StateFlow instead)
 */

// Producer-Consumer pattern with Channel
fun CoroutineScope.produceImages(urls: List<String>): ReceiveChannel<Bitmap> {
    return produce(Dispatchers.IO, capacity = 5) {
        for (url in urls) {
            val bitmap = downloadImage(url)
            send(bitmap)  // Suspends if buffer is full
        }
    }
}


// ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
// 12. MUTEX & THREAD SAFETY
// ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

/**
 * Coroutines can access shared mutable state from different threads.
 * Need synchronization to prevent race conditions.
 *
 * Options:
 *   Mutex              → coroutine-friendly lock (non-blocking)
 *   Single-thread      → confine to one dispatcher
 *   Atomic operations  → AtomicInteger, AtomicReference
 *   StateFlow          → thread-safe state updates via update {}
 */

class ThreadSafeCounter {
    private val mutex = Mutex()
    private var count = 0

    suspend fun increment() {
        mutex.withLock {
            count++  // Safe — only one coroutine at a time
        }
    }
}

// ❓ Interview Question: Why Mutex instead of synchronized?
//    synchronized BLOCKS the thread. Mutex SUSPENDS the coroutine.
//    The thread is free to execute other coroutines while waiting.


// ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
// 13. TESTING COROUTINES
// ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

class ArticleViewModelTest {

    @get:Rule
    val mainDispatcherRule = MainDispatcherRule()

    private lateinit var viewModel: ArticleViewModel
    private val fakeRepository = FakeArticleRepository()

    @Before
    fun setup() {
        viewModel = ArticleViewModel(fakeRepository)
    }

    @Test
    fun `loading state emitted before data`() = runTest {
        fakeRepository.setDelay(1000)

        viewModel.loadArticles()

        // advanceUntilIdle() or turbine for Flow testing
        val states = mutableListOf<UiState>()
        val job = launch {
            viewModel.uiState.collect { states.add(it) }
        }

        advanceUntilIdle()
        job.cancel()

        assertEquals(UiState.Loading, states[0])
        assertTrue(states.last() is UiState.Success)
    }
}

// MainDispatcherRule — replaces Main dispatcher in tests
class MainDispatcherRule(
    private val testDispatcher: TestDispatcher = UnconfinedTestDispatcher()
) : TestWatcher() {
    override fun starting(description: Description) {
        Dispatchers.setMain(testDispatcher)
    }
    override fun finished(description: Description) {
        Dispatchers.resetMain()
    }
}


// ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
// 14. CALLBACKFLOW & CHANNELFLOW
// ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

/**
 * callbackFlow:
 *   Bridges callback-based APIs into Flow. Creates a Channel internally
 *   and allows sending values from callback lambdas.
 *
 *   Common use cases:
 *     - Location updates (FusedLocationProviderClient)
 *     - Firebase Realtime Database / Firestore listeners
 *     - Sensor events
 *     - BroadcastReceiver events
 *
 * channelFlow:
 *   Like flow { } but allows concurrent emissions from multiple coroutines.
 *   Use when you need to send from different coroutine contexts.
 *
 * ⚠️ CRITICAL: callbackFlow MUST call awaitClose { } to:
 *    1. Keep the flow alive (otherwise it completes immediately)
 *    2. Clean up the callback registration when the flow is cancelled
 */

// ── callbackFlow: wrapping a location callback ──

fun FusedLocationProviderClient.locationUpdates(
    request: LocationRequest
): Flow<Location> = callbackFlow {
    val callback = object : LocationCallback() {
        override fun onLocationResult(result: LocationResult) {
            for (location in result.locations) {
                trySend(location)  // Non-suspending send — drops if buffer full
            }
        }
    }

    requestLocationUpdates(request, callback, Looper.getMainLooper())
        .addOnFailureListener { e -> close(e) }

    awaitClose {
        removeLocationUpdates(callback)
    }
}

// Usage in ViewModel
class LocationViewModel(
    locationClient: FusedLocationProviderClient
) : ViewModel() {

    val locationState: StateFlow<Location?> = locationClient
        .locationUpdates(
            LocationRequest.Builder(Priority.PRIORITY_HIGH_ACCURACY, 10_000L).build()
        )
        .catch { emit(Location("default")) }
        .stateIn(viewModelScope, SharingStarted.WhileSubscribed(5000), null)
}

// ── callbackFlow: wrapping a Firebase Firestore listener ──

fun documentFlow(docRef: DocumentReference): Flow<DocumentSnapshot> = callbackFlow {
    val listener = docRef.addSnapshotListener { snapshot, error ->
        if (error != null) {
            close(error)
            return@addSnapshotListener
        }
        if (snapshot != null) {
            trySend(snapshot)
        }
    }

    awaitClose { listener.remove() }
}

// ── channelFlow: concurrent emissions ──

fun fetchAllPages(api: PaginatedApi): Flow<List<Item>> = channelFlow {
    val firstPage = api.getPage(1)
    send(firstPage.items)

    // Fan out remaining pages concurrently
    val totalPages = firstPage.totalPages
    (2..totalPages).map { page ->
        launch {
            val result = api.getPage(page)
            send(result.items)  // Safe — channelFlow allows concurrent sends
        }
    }
}

/**
 * ❓ Interview Question: callbackFlow vs channelFlow?
 *
 *   callbackFlow: designed for wrapping external callbacks. Requires awaitClose.
 *   channelFlow:  designed for concurrent coroutine emissions inside the flow.
 *
 *   Both use a Channel internally, but callbackFlow enforces the awaitClose
 *   contract with a lint warning if omitted.
 *
 * ⚠️ GOTCHA: trySend vs send
 *   send()    → suspends if buffer is full (must be called from a coroutine)
 *   trySend() → non-suspending; returns ChannelResult. Use in callbacks.
 */


// ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
// 15. SHAREIN VS STATEIN
// ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

/**
 * Both convert a cold Flow into a hot shared Flow.
 *
 * stateIn  → produces StateFlow (always has current value, conflated)
 * shareIn  → produces SharedFlow (configurable replay, no required initial value)
 *
 * ┌───────────────────┬──────────────────────────┬──────────────────────────┐
 * │ Feature           │ stateIn                  │ shareIn                  │
 * ├───────────────────┼──────────────────────────┼──────────────────────────┤
 * │ Return type       │ StateFlow<T>             │ SharedFlow<T>            │
 * │ Initial value     │ Required                 │ Not required             │
 * │ Replay            │ Always 1 (latest)        │ Configurable (0, 1, N)  │
 * │ Equality check    │ Yes (conflated)          │ No                       │
 * │ Best for          │ UI state (single latest) │ Events, multi-collector  │
 * │ Collectors get    │ Latest value immediately │ replay cache + new values│
 * └───────────────────┴──────────────────────────┴──────────────────────────┘
 */

class ArticleListViewModel(
    private val repository: ArticleRepository
) : ViewModel() {

    // ── stateIn: UI state — collectors always get the latest value ──
    val articles: StateFlow<List<Article>> = repository
        .observeArticles()
        .stateIn(
            scope = viewModelScope,
            started = SharingStarted.WhileSubscribed(5_000),
            initialValue = emptyList()
        )

    // ── shareIn: share an expensive upstream among multiple collectors ──
    val priceUpdates: SharedFlow<PriceUpdate> = repository
        .observePrices()
        .shareIn(
            scope = viewModelScope,
            started = SharingStarted.WhileSubscribed(5_000),
            replay = 1  // New collectors get the last emitted price
        )
}

/**
 * SharingStarted strategies — detailed comparison:
 *
 *   Eagerly:
 *     - Starts collecting the upstream IMMEDIATELY when stateIn/shareIn is called
 *     - Never stops, even if no collectors are active
 *     - ✅ Use for: data that must always be up-to-date (rare)
 *     - ⚠️ Keeps the upstream alive forever → potential resource waste
 *
 *   Lazily:
 *     - Starts on the FIRST collector
 *     - Never stops after starting
 *     - ✅ Use for: expensive init that should only happen when needed
 *     - ⚠️ First collector may miss values emitted before it subscribed
 *       (mitigated by replay in shareIn, or initialValue in stateIn)
 *
 *   WhileSubscribed(stopTimeoutMillis):
 *     - Starts on first collector, stops when last collector unsubscribes
 *       (after stopTimeoutMillis delay)
 *     - ✅ Recommended default: WhileSubscribed(5000)
 *       → survives config changes (within 5s)
 *       → stops upstream when screen is truly gone (saves battery/network)
 *
 * 💡 Interview Tip: Always explain why you chose a specific SharingStarted
 *    strategy. WhileSubscribed(5000) is the safest default for most ViewModel use cases.
 */

// Multi-collector scenario
class DashboardViewModel(repo: DashboardRepository) : ViewModel() {

    private val _refreshTrigger = MutableSharedFlow<Unit>(replay = 1).apply {
        tryEmit(Unit)
    }

    // Shared upstream: expensive query, collected by multiple UI elements
    val dashboardData: StateFlow<DashboardData> = _refreshTrigger
        .flatMapLatest { repo.fetchDashboard() }
        .stateIn(viewModelScope, SharingStarted.WhileSubscribed(5_000), DashboardData.Loading)

    fun refresh() {
        _refreshTrigger.tryEmit(Unit)
    }
}


// ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
// PRACTICE QUESTIONS
// ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

/**
 * ❓ Q1: What's the difference between Dispatchers.Main and Dispatchers.Main.immediate?
 *    A: Main always posts to the queue. Main.immediate executes immediately
 *       if already on the main thread (avoids one frame delay).
 *       viewModelScope uses Main.immediate by default.
 *
 * ❓ Q2: What happens if you emit from a different thread in a flow { } builder?
 *    A: It throws IllegalStateException. Flow builder is sequential.
 *       Use channelFlow { } for concurrent emissions from multiple coroutines.
 *
 * ❓ Q3: How do you implement search-as-you-type with debouncing?
 *    A: searchQuery
 *         .debounce(300)
 *         .filter { it.length >= 2 }
 *         .distinctUntilChanged()
 *         .flatMapLatest { query -> repository.search(query) }
 *         .catch { emit(emptyList()) }
 *
 * ❓ Q4: What's the difference between flatMapLatest, flatMapConcat, flatMapMerge?
 *    A: flatMapLatest  → cancels previous; use for search (latest query wins)
 *       flatMapConcat  → sequential; use when order matters
 *       flatMapMerge   → concurrent; use when all results needed in parallel
 *
 * ❓ Q5: CancellationException — is it treated as a failure?
 *    A: No. CancellationException is considered NORMAL cancellation.
 *       It propagates up but doesn't trigger CoroutineExceptionHandler.
 *       ⚠️ Never catch CancellationException without rethrowing.
 *
 * ❓ Q6: What's the difference between withTimeout and withTimeoutOrNull?
 *    A: withTimeout throws TimeoutCancellationException (which IS a CancellationException).
 *       withTimeoutOrNull returns null on timeout. Safer for non-critical timeouts.
 *
 * ❓ Q7: How would you convert Firebase Realtime Database listeners into a Flow?
 *    A: Use callbackFlow:
 *       fun observeNode(ref: DatabaseReference): Flow<DataSnapshot> = callbackFlow {
 *           val listener = object : ValueEventListener {
 *               override fun onDataChange(snapshot: DataSnapshot) { trySend(snapshot) }
 *               override fun onCancelled(error: DatabaseError) { close(error.toException()) }
 *           }
 *           ref.addValueEventListener(listener)
 *           awaitClose { ref.removeEventListener(listener) }
 *       }
 *       Key points: use trySend (not send) in callbacks, always call awaitClose
 *       for cleanup, and use close(exception) to propagate errors.
 *
 * ❓ Q8: Why should you NEVER catch CancellationException without rethrowing?
 *    A: CancellationException is the mechanism coroutines use to propagate
 *       cancellation through the hierarchy. If you swallow it:
 *       - The parent doesn't know the child was cancelled
 *       - Structured concurrency breaks (parent waits forever)
 *       - Resources are leaked (coroutine continues running)
 *       ⚠️ CRITICAL pattern:
 *       try { suspendingWork() }
 *       catch (e: Exception) {
 *           if (e is CancellationException) throw e  // Always rethrow!
 *           handleError(e)
 *       }
 *       Or better: use runCatching only on non-suspending code, and catch
 *       specific exception types instead of broad Exception.
 */

05 Data Persistence

05_data_persistence.kt · Kotlin reference

/**
 * ============================================================================
 * 05 — DATA PERSISTENCE
 * Google SDE-2 Interview Prep
 * ============================================================================
 *
 * Topics Covered:
 *   1. Room Database (Entities, DAOs, Database)
 *   2. Room Relations (One-to-One, One-to-Many, Many-to-Many)
 *   3. Room Type Converters
 *   4. Room Migrations (Auto & Manual)
 *   5. DataStore (Preferences)
 *   6. SharedPreferences (Legacy but still asked)
 *   7. Content Providers
 *   8. Choosing the Right Storage
 *   9. Paging 3 + Room (PagingSource, RemoteMediator)
 *  10. Proto DataStore
 *  11. Room Advanced (@DatabaseView, FTS4, Testing)
 *
 * ============================================================================
 */

// ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
// 1. ROOM DATABASE — COMPLETE SETUP
// ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

/**
 * Room = SQLite abstraction layer with compile-time query verification.
 *
 * Three core components:
 *   @Entity    → defines a table
 *   @Dao       → defines operations (queries)
 *   @Database  → ties entities and DAOs together
 *
 * 💡 Interview Tip: Google uses Room as the single source of truth
 *    in offline-first architectures. Know it deeply.
 */

// ── Entity ──
@Entity(
    tableName = "articles",
    indices = [
        Index(value = ["author_id"]),        // Query optimization
        Index(value = ["slug"], unique = true) // Unique constraint
    ]
)
data class ArticleEntity(
    @PrimaryKey
    val id: String,

    @ColumnInfo(name = "title")
    val title: String,

    @ColumnInfo(name = "content")
    val content: String,

    @ColumnInfo(name = "author_id")
    val authorId: String,

    @ColumnInfo(name = "slug")
    val slug: String,

    @ColumnInfo(name = "tags")
    val tags: List<String>,              // Needs TypeConverter

    @ColumnInfo(name = "published_at")
    val publishedAt: Long,

    @ColumnInfo(name = "is_bookmarked")
    val isBookmarked: Boolean = false,

    @ColumnInfo(name = "last_fetched")
    val lastFetched: Long = System.currentTimeMillis()
)

// ── Embedded objects ──
data class Address(
    val street: String,
    val city: String,
    val zipCode: String
)

@Entity(tableName = "users")
data class UserEntity(
    @PrimaryKey val id: String,
    val name: String,
    @Embedded(prefix = "address_") val address: Address
    // Creates columns: address_street, address_city, address_zipCode
)


// ── DAO ──
@Dao
interface ArticleDao {

    // Basic CRUD
    @Insert(onConflict = OnConflictStrategy.REPLACE)
    suspend fun insert(article: ArticleEntity)

    @Insert(onConflict = OnConflictStrategy.REPLACE)
    suspend fun insertAll(articles: List<ArticleEntity>)

    @Update
    suspend fun update(article: ArticleEntity)

    @Delete
    suspend fun delete(article: ArticleEntity)

    // Queries
    @Query("SELECT * FROM articles ORDER BY published_at DESC")
    suspend fun getAllArticles(): List<ArticleEntity>

    @Query("SELECT * FROM articles WHERE id = :id")
    suspend fun getArticleById(id: String): ArticleEntity?

    @Query("SELECT * FROM articles WHERE is_bookmarked = 1 ORDER BY published_at DESC")
    suspend fun getBookmarkedArticles(): List<ArticleEntity>

    // Reactive query — emits new list whenever data changes
    @Query("SELECT * FROM articles ORDER BY published_at DESC")
    fun observeAllArticles(): Flow<List<ArticleEntity>>

    @Query("SELECT * FROM articles WHERE id = :id")
    fun observeArticle(id: String): Flow<ArticleEntity?>

    // Upsert (insert or update) — Room 2.5+
    @Upsert
    suspend fun upsert(article: ArticleEntity)

    @Upsert
    suspend fun upsertAll(articles: List<ArticleEntity>)

    // Transaction — multiple operations atomically
    @Transaction
    suspend fun replaceAll(articles: List<ArticleEntity>) {
        deleteAll()
        insertAll(articles)
    }

    @Query("DELETE FROM articles")
    suspend fun deleteAll()

    // Pagination with LIMIT/OFFSET
    @Query("SELECT * FROM articles ORDER BY published_at DESC LIMIT :limit OFFSET :offset")
    suspend fun getArticlesPaginated(limit: Int, offset: Int): List<ArticleEntity>

    // Full-text search (requires FTS4 setup)
    @Query("SELECT * FROM articles WHERE title LIKE '%' || :query || '%' OR content LIKE '%' || :query || '%'")
    suspend fun search(query: String): List<ArticleEntity>
}


// ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
// 2. ROOM RELATIONS
// ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

/**
 * Room doesn't allow direct object references between entities.
 * Use @Relation for querying related data.
 *
 * Types of relationships:
 *   - One-to-One  (@Relation with entity reference)
 *   - One-to-Many (@Relation with parentColumn + entityColumn)
 *   - Many-to-Many (junction table with @Junction)
 */

// One-to-Many: User has many Articles
data class UserWithArticles(
    @Embedded val user: UserEntity,
    @Relation(
        parentColumn = "id",       // UserEntity.id
        entityColumn = "author_id" // ArticleEntity.authorId
    )
    val articles: List<ArticleEntity>
)

@Dao
interface UserDao {
    @Transaction  // ⚠️ Required for @Relation queries (ensures consistency)
    @Query("SELECT * FROM users WHERE id = :userId")
    suspend fun getUserWithArticles(userId: String): UserWithArticles

    @Transaction
    @Query("SELECT * FROM users")
    fun observeAllUsersWithArticles(): Flow<List<UserWithArticles>>
}

// Many-to-Many: Articles can have many Tags, Tags can have many Articles
@Entity(tableName = "tags")
data class TagEntity(
    @PrimaryKey val tagId: String,
    val name: String
)

@Entity(
    tableName = "article_tag_cross_ref",
    primaryKeys = ["articleId", "tagId"]  // Composite primary key
)
data class ArticleTagCrossRef(
    val articleId: String,
    val tagId: String
)

data class ArticleWithTags(
    @Embedded val article: ArticleEntity,
    @Relation(
        parentColumn = "id",
        entityColumn = "tagId",
        associateBy = Junction(ArticleTagCrossRef::class)
    )
    val tags: List<TagEntity>
)


// ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
// 3. TYPE CONVERTERS
// ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

/**
 * Room only stores primitive types. TypeConverters handle custom types.
 */

class Converters {
    private val json = Json { ignoreUnknownKeys = true }

    @TypeConverter
    fun fromStringList(value: List<String>): String {
        return json.encodeToString(value)
    }

    @TypeConverter
    fun toStringList(value: String): List<String> {
        return json.decodeFromString(value)
    }

    @TypeConverter
    fun fromTimestamp(value: Long?): Date? {
        return value?.let { Date(it) }
    }

    @TypeConverter
    fun toTimestamp(date: Date?): Long? {
        return date?.time
    }

    @TypeConverter
    fun fromInstant(value: Long?): Instant? {
        return value?.let { Instant.ofEpochMilli(it) }
    }

    @TypeConverter
    fun toInstant(instant: Instant?): Long? {
        return instant?.toEpochMilli()
    }
}


// ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
// 4. ROOM MIGRATIONS
// ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

/**
 * When you change the database schema, you MUST provide a migration path.
 * Otherwise, Room throws IllegalStateException on app update.
 *
 * Options:
 *   1. Auto Migration (Room 2.4+) — for simple changes
 *   2. Manual Migration — for complex changes
 *   3. Destructive migration — drop and recreate (data loss!)
 */

// Database class with migrations
@Database(
    entities = [ArticleEntity::class, UserEntity::class, TagEntity::class],
    version = 3,
    autoMigrations = [
        AutoMigration(from = 1, to = 2),  // Simple column add
        AutoMigration(from = 2, to = 3, spec = Migration2To3::class)
    ]
)
@TypeConverters(Converters::class)
abstract class AppDatabase : RoomDatabase() {
    abstract fun articleDao(): ArticleDao
    abstract fun userDao(): UserDao
}

// Auto Migration spec for complex changes (rename, delete)
@RenameColumn(tableName = "articles", fromColumnName = "body", toColumnName = "content")
class Migration2To3 : AutoMigrationSpec

// Manual migration for complex schema changes
val MIGRATION_3_4 = object : Migration(3, 4) {
    override fun migrate(db: SupportSQLiteDatabase) {
        // Create new table with desired schema
        db.execSQL("""
            CREATE TABLE articles_new (
                id TEXT NOT NULL PRIMARY KEY,
                title TEXT NOT NULL,
                content TEXT NOT NULL,
                author_id TEXT NOT NULL,
                slug TEXT NOT NULL,
                published_at INTEGER NOT NULL DEFAULT 0,
                word_count INTEGER NOT NULL DEFAULT 0
            )
        """)

        // Copy data from old table
        db.execSQL("""
            INSERT INTO articles_new (id, title, content, author_id, slug, published_at)
            SELECT id, title, content, author_id, slug, published_at
            FROM articles
        """)

        // Drop old table and rename
        db.execSQL("DROP TABLE articles")
        db.execSQL("ALTER TABLE articles_new RENAME TO articles")

        // Create indices
        db.execSQL("CREATE INDEX index_articles_author_id ON articles(author_id)")
        db.execSQL("CREATE UNIQUE INDEX index_articles_slug ON articles(slug)")
    }
}

// Building the database
fun provideDatabase(context: Context): AppDatabase {
    return Room.databaseBuilder(
        context.applicationContext,
        AppDatabase::class.java,
        "app_database"
    )
        .addMigrations(MIGRATION_3_4)
        // ⚠️ Only for development — destroys data on failed migration
        // .fallbackToDestructiveMigration()
        .build()
}


// ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
// 5. DATASTORE
// ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

/**
 * DataStore = modern replacement for SharedPreferences.
 *
 * Two flavors:
 *   Preferences DataStore → key-value pairs (like SharedPreferences but async)
 *   Proto DataStore        → typed objects (Protocol Buffers)
 *
 * Advantages over SharedPreferences:
 *   ✅ Kotlin Coroutines & Flow API (async, no blocking)
 *   ✅ Handles errors via Flow's catch operator
 *   ✅ No runtime exceptions on bad types
 *   ✅ Transactional — no partial updates
 *   ✅ No apply() / commit() confusion
 */

// Preferences DataStore
private val Context.userPreferencesStore: DataStore<Preferences> by preferencesDataStore(
    name = "user_preferences"
)

class UserPreferencesRepository(private val dataStore: DataStore<Preferences>) {

    private object PreferencesKeys {
        val DARK_MODE = booleanPreferencesKey("dark_mode")
        val FONT_SIZE = intPreferencesKey("font_size")
        val USERNAME = stringPreferencesKey("username")
        val SORT_ORDER = stringPreferencesKey("sort_order")
    }

    val userPreferences: Flow<UserPreferences> = dataStore.data
        .catch { exception ->
            if (exception is IOException) {
                emit(emptyPreferences())
            } else {
                throw exception
            }
        }
        .map { preferences ->
            UserPreferences(
                darkMode = preferences[PreferencesKeys.DARK_MODE] ?: false,
                fontSize = preferences[PreferencesKeys.FONT_SIZE] ?: 14,
                username = preferences[PreferencesKeys.USERNAME] ?: "",
                sortOrder = SortOrder.valueOf(
                    preferences[PreferencesKeys.SORT_ORDER] ?: SortOrder.NEWEST.name
                )
            )
        }

    suspend fun updateDarkMode(enabled: Boolean) {
        dataStore.edit { preferences ->
            preferences[PreferencesKeys.DARK_MODE] = enabled
        }
    }

    suspend fun updateSortOrder(sortOrder: SortOrder) {
        dataStore.edit { preferences ->
            preferences[PreferencesKeys.SORT_ORDER] = sortOrder.name
        }
    }
}

data class UserPreferences(
    val darkMode: Boolean,
    val fontSize: Int,
    val username: String,
    val sortOrder: SortOrder
)


// ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
// 6. SHAREDPREFERENCES (LEGACY)
// ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

/**
 * Still used in many apps. Know the issues for interviews.
 *
 * ⚠️ Problems:
 *   1. apply() is async but blocks the main thread on Activity stop (ANR risk)
 *   2. commit() is synchronous on the calling thread (blocks UI if on Main)
 *   3. No type safety — getString() on an int key returns null, not a crash
 *   4. No error handling — fails silently
 *   5. No transactional guarantees — partial writes possible
 *   6. getAll() returns a mutable reference to the underlying map
 *
 * 💡 Interview: Always mention migrating to DataStore for new code.
 */

// Migration from SharedPreferences to DataStore
private val Context.dataStore by preferencesDataStore(
    name = "settings",
    produceMigrations = { context ->
        listOf(SharedPreferencesMigration(context, "old_shared_prefs"))
    }
)


// ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
// 7. CONTENT PROVIDERS
// ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

/**
 * Content Providers expose data to other apps via a structured URI-based API.
 *
 * Used for:
 *   - Contacts, MediaStore, Calendar
 *   - Sharing data between apps (your app → other apps)
 *   - Required for SearchRecentSuggestionsProvider
 *   - CursorLoader compatibility
 *
 * URI format: content://authority/path/id
 *   Example: content://com.google.app.provider/articles/123
 *
 * 💡 Interview Tip: For most modern apps, you don't need to create a
 *    ContentProvider. Use them to ACCESS system data (Contacts, Media).
 */

// Querying the MediaStore (Scoped Storage)
suspend fun getImages(context: Context): List<MediaImage> {
    return withContext(Dispatchers.IO) {
        val images = mutableListOf<MediaImage>()
        val projection = arrayOf(
            MediaStore.Images.Media._ID,
            MediaStore.Images.Media.DISPLAY_NAME,
            MediaStore.Images.Media.SIZE,
            MediaStore.Images.Media.DATE_ADDED
        )
        val sortOrder = "${MediaStore.Images.Media.DATE_ADDED} DESC"

        context.contentResolver.query(
            MediaStore.Images.Media.EXTERNAL_CONTENT_URI,
            projection, null, null, sortOrder
        )?.use { cursor ->
            val idColumn = cursor.getColumnIndexOrThrow(MediaStore.Images.Media._ID)
            val nameColumn = cursor.getColumnIndexOrThrow(MediaStore.Images.Media.DISPLAY_NAME)

            while (cursor.moveToNext()) {
                val id = cursor.getLong(idColumn)
                val name = cursor.getString(nameColumn)
                val uri = ContentUris.withAppendedId(
                    MediaStore.Images.Media.EXTERNAL_CONTENT_URI, id
                )
                images.add(MediaImage(id, name, uri))
            }
        }
        images
    }
}


// ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
// 8. CHOOSING THE RIGHT STORAGE
// ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

/**
 * ┌──────────────────┬─────────────────────┬──────────────────────────────────┐
 * │ Storage          │ Use Case            │ Notes                            │
 * ├──────────────────┼─────────────────────┼──────────────────────────────────┤
 * │ Room             │ Structured data,    │ SSOT, reactive queries,          │
 * │                  │ complex queries     │ offline-first                    │
 * ├──────────────────┼─────────────────────┼──────────────────────────────────┤
 * │ DataStore        │ Key-value prefs,    │ Async, type-safe,                │
 * │ (Preferences)    │ settings            │ replaces SharedPreferences       │
 * ├──────────────────┼─────────────────────┼──────────────────────────────────┤
 * │ DataStore        │ Complex settings,   │ Schema evolution,                │
 * │ (Proto)          │ typed config        │ protocol buffers                 │
 * ├──────────────────┼─────────────────────┼──────────────────────────────────┤
 * │ Internal Storage │ Private app files   │ No permissions needed,           │
 * │                  │                     │ deleted on uninstall             │
 * ├──────────────────┼─────────────────────┼──────────────────────────────────┤
 * │ Scoped Storage   │ Media, documents    │ MediaStore for shared media,     │
 * │                  │                     │ SAF for user-selected files      │
 * ├──────────────────┼─────────────────────┼──────────────────────────────────┤
 * │ EncryptedSharedP │ Sensitive data      │ Backed by AndroidX Security,     │
 * │                  │ (tokens, keys)      │ AES-256 encryption               │
 * └──────────────────┴─────────────────────┴──────────────────────────────────┘
 */


// ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
// 9. PAGING 3 + ROOM
// ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

/**
 * Paging 3 loads data in chunks (pages), reducing memory usage and network calls.
 *
 * Core components:
 *   PagingSource        → loads pages from a single source (DB or network)
 *   RemoteMediator      → orchestrates network + DB for offline-first paging
 *   Pager               → creates a PagingData flow from PagingSource/RemoteMediator
 *   PagingDataAdapter   → RecyclerView adapter (or collectAsLazyPagingItems in Compose)
 *
 * 💡 Interview Tip: Google's recommended architecture for large lists is
 *    Network → RemoteMediator → Room (single source of truth) → PagingSource → UI
 */

// ── Room DAO returning PagingSource ──

@Dao
interface PagingArticleDao {
    @Query("SELECT * FROM articles ORDER BY published_at DESC")
    fun pagingSource(): PagingSource<Int, ArticleEntity>

    @Insert(onConflict = OnConflictStrategy.REPLACE)
    suspend fun insertAll(articles: List<ArticleEntity>)

    @Query("DELETE FROM articles")
    suspend fun clearAll()
}

// ── RemoteMediator: offline-first paging ──

@OptIn(ExperimentalPagingApi::class)
class ArticleRemoteMediator(
    private val api: ArticleApi,
    private val database: AppDatabase
) : RemoteMediator<Int, ArticleEntity>() {

    private val articleDao = database.articleDao()

    override suspend fun initialize(): InitializeAction {
        val cacheTimeout = TimeUnit.HOURS.toMillis(1)
        val lastFetched = database.remoteKeyDao().getLastFetchTime() ?: 0L
        return if (System.currentTimeMillis() - lastFetched < cacheTimeout) {
            InitializeAction.SKIP_INITIAL_REFRESH
        } else {
            InitializeAction.LAUNCH_INITIAL_REFRESH
        }
    }

    override suspend fun load(
        loadType: LoadType,
        state: PagingState<Int, ArticleEntity>
    ): MediatorResult {
        val page = when (loadType) {
            LoadType.REFRESH -> 1
            LoadType.PREPEND -> return MediatorResult.Success(endOfPaginationReached = true)
            LoadType.APPEND -> {
                val lastItem = state.lastItemOrNull()
                    ?: return MediatorResult.Success(endOfPaginationReached = true)
                database.remoteKeyDao().getNextPage(lastItem.id)
                    ?: return MediatorResult.Success(endOfPaginationReached = true)
            }
        }

        return try {
            val response = api.getArticles(page = page, pageSize = state.config.pageSize)
            val endReached = response.articles.size < state.config.pageSize

            database.withTransaction {
                if (loadType == LoadType.REFRESH) {
                    articleDao.clearAll()
                    database.remoteKeyDao().clearAll()
                }
                articleDao.insertAll(response.articles.map { it.toEntity() })
                database.remoteKeyDao().insertKey(
                    RemoteKey(
                        articleId = response.articles.last().id,
                        nextPage = if (endReached) null else page + 1,
                        lastFetchTime = System.currentTimeMillis()
                    )
                )
            }

            MediatorResult.Success(endOfPaginationReached = endReached)
        } catch (e: IOException) {
            MediatorResult.Error(e)
        } catch (e: HttpException) {
            MediatorResult.Error(e)
        }
    }
}

// ── Remote key entity for tracking pagination state ──

@Entity(tableName = "remote_keys")
data class RemoteKey(
    @PrimaryKey val articleId: String,
    val nextPage: Int?,
    val lastFetchTime: Long
)

@Dao
interface RemoteKeyDao {
    @Insert(onConflict = OnConflictStrategy.REPLACE)
    suspend fun insertKey(key: RemoteKey)

    @Query("SELECT nextPage FROM remote_keys WHERE articleId = :articleId")
    suspend fun getNextPage(articleId: String): Int?

    @Query("SELECT MAX(lastFetchTime) FROM remote_keys")
    suspend fun getLastFetchTime(): Long?

    @Query("DELETE FROM remote_keys")
    suspend fun clearAll()
}

// ── Wiring it together: Pager + cachedIn ──

class ArticleRepository(
    private val api: ArticleApi,
    private val database: AppDatabase
) {
    @OptIn(ExperimentalPagingApi::class)
    fun getArticlesPaged(): Flow<PagingData<ArticleEntity>> {
        return Pager(
            config = PagingConfig(
                pageSize = 20,
                prefetchDistance = 5,
                enablePlaceholders = false
            ),
            remoteMediator = ArticleRemoteMediator(api, database),
            pagingSourceFactory = { database.articleDao().pagingSource() }
        ).flow
    }
}

class PagingViewModel(repository: ArticleRepository) : ViewModel() {
    val articles: Flow<PagingData<ArticleEntity>> = repository
        .getArticlesPaged()
        .cachedIn(viewModelScope)  // Survives config changes
}

// ── Compose UI with LazyPagingItems ──

@Composable
fun ArticleListScreen(viewModel: PagingViewModel = hiltViewModel()) {
    val articles = viewModel.articles.collectAsLazyPagingItems()

    LazyColumn {
        items(
            count = articles.itemCount,
            key = articles.itemKey { it.id }
        ) { index ->
            val article = articles[index]
            if (article != null) {
                ArticleCard(article)
            }
        }

        // Loading/error states
        when (articles.loadState.append) {
            is LoadState.Loading -> item { CircularProgressIndicator() }
            is LoadState.Error -> item {
                RetryButton(onClick = { articles.retry() })
            }
            else -> {}
        }
    }
}

/**
 * ⚠️ Paging Gotchas:
 *   1. Always call cachedIn(viewModelScope) to avoid re-fetching on config change
 *   2. PagingSource is invalidated automatically by Room when data changes
 *   3. RemoteMediator.initialize() controls whether to skip initial network call
 *   4. Never load all pages eagerly — defeats the purpose of paging
 */


// ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
// 10. PROTO DATASTORE
// ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

/**
 * Proto DataStore stores typed objects using Protocol Buffers.
 *
 * When to use Proto vs Preferences DataStore:
 *   Preferences DataStore → simple key-value pairs (dark mode, font size)
 *   Proto DataStore        → complex/nested settings with schema evolution
 *
 * Proto advantages:
 *   ✅ Full type safety at compile time (no string keys)
 *   ✅ Schema evolution with backward compatibility
 *   ✅ Nested objects and repeated fields
 *   ✅ Smaller serialized size than JSON
 *
 * Setup requires:
 *   1. .proto schema file
 *   2. Serializer implementation
 *   3. protobuf Gradle plugin
 */

// ── Step 1: Proto schema (app/src/main/proto/user_settings.proto) ──

/**
 * syntax = "proto3";
 *
 * option java_package = "com.example.app";
 * option java_multiple_files = true;
 *
 * message UserSettings {
 *     bool dark_mode = 1;
 *     int32 font_size = 2;
 *     string language = 3;
 *     NotificationConfig notifications = 4;
 *
 *     message NotificationConfig {
 *         bool enabled = 1;
 *         bool sound = 2;
 *         bool vibrate = 3;
 *     }
 * }
 */

// ── Step 2: Serializer ──

object UserSettingsSerializer : Serializer<UserSettings> {
    override val defaultValue: UserSettings = UserSettings.getDefaultInstance()

    override suspend fun readFrom(input: InputStream): UserSettings {
        try {
            return UserSettings.parseFrom(input)
        } catch (e: InvalidProtocolBufferException) {
            throw CorruptionException("Cannot read proto.", e)
        }
    }

    override suspend fun writeTo(t: UserSettings, output: OutputStream) {
        t.writeTo(output)
    }
}

// ── Step 3: Create the DataStore ──

private val Context.userSettingsStore: DataStore<UserSettings> by dataStore(
    fileName = "user_settings.pb",
    serializer = UserSettingsSerializer
)

// ── Step 4: Repository for reading/writing ──

class UserSettingsRepository(private val dataStore: DataStore<UserSettings>) {

    val settings: Flow<UserSettings> = dataStore.data
        .catch { exception ->
            if (exception is IOException) {
                emit(UserSettings.getDefaultInstance())
            } else {
                throw exception
            }
        }

    suspend fun setDarkMode(enabled: Boolean) {
        dataStore.updateData { current ->
            current.toBuilder().setDarkMode(enabled).build()
        }
    }

    suspend fun updateNotifications(block: UserSettings.NotificationConfig.Builder.() -> Unit) {
        dataStore.updateData { current ->
            current.toBuilder().setNotifications(
                current.notifications.toBuilder().apply(block).build()
            ).build()
        }
    }
}

/**
 * 💡 Interview Tip: Proto DataStore is the right choice when you have:
 *    - More than ~5 related settings
 *    - Nested configuration objects
 *    - Need for schema evolution (adding/removing fields safely)
 *    - Strong type safety requirements
 *    For simple boolean flags or a single preference, Preferences DataStore is simpler.
 */


// ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
// 11. ROOM ADVANCED
// ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

/**
 * Advanced Room features that come up in SDE-2+ interviews:
 *   - @DatabaseView (virtual table from a query)
 *   - FTS4 (full-text search)
 *   - Migration testing with MigrationTestHelper
 *   - In-memory database for unit tests
 */

// ── @DatabaseView — precomputed query as a virtual table ──

@DatabaseView(
    value = """
        SELECT a.id, a.title, a.published_at, u.name AS authorName,
               COUNT(c.id) AS commentCount
        FROM articles a
        INNER JOIN users u ON a.author_id = u.id
        LEFT JOIN comments c ON c.article_id = a.id
        GROUP BY a.id
    """,
    viewName = "article_summary"
)
data class ArticleSummary(
    val id: String,
    val title: String,
    val publishedAt: Long,
    val authorName: String,
    val commentCount: Int
)

@Dao
interface ArticleSummaryDao {
    @Query("SELECT * FROM article_summary ORDER BY publishedAt DESC")
    fun observeSummaries(): Flow<List<ArticleSummary>>

    @Query("SELECT * FROM article_summary WHERE commentCount > :minComments")
    suspend fun getPopularArticles(minComments: Int): List<ArticleSummary>
}

// Register the view in @Database
// @Database(entities = [...], views = [ArticleSummary::class], version = ...)

/**
 * 💡 @DatabaseView benefits:
 *    - Encapsulates complex JOIN queries
 *    - Can be queried like a regular table
 *    - Updated automatically when underlying tables change
 *    - ⚠️ Read-only — no INSERT/UPDATE/DELETE
 */

// ── FTS4 — Full-Text Search ──

@Fts4(contentEntity = ArticleEntity::class)
@Entity(tableName = "articles_fts")
data class ArticleFts(
    val title: String,
    val content: String
)

@Dao
interface ArticleFtsDao {
    @Query("""
        SELECT articles.* FROM articles
        JOIN articles_fts ON articles.rowid = articles_fts.rowid
        WHERE articles_fts MATCH :query
        ORDER BY rank
    """)
    suspend fun search(query: String): List<ArticleEntity>

    @Query("""
        SELECT snippet(articles_fts, '<b>', '</b>', '...', 1, 30) AS snippet
        FROM articles_fts WHERE articles_fts MATCH :query
    """)
    suspend fun searchWithSnippets(query: String): List<SearchSnippet>
}

data class SearchSnippet(val snippet: String)

/**
 * FTS4 tips:
 *   - contentEntity links FTS table to the source entity (keeps them in sync)
 *   - MATCH operator uses FTS syntax: "android kotlin" (AND), "android OR kotlin"
 *   - snippet() highlights matches in results
 *   - ⚠️ FTS tables use more disk space (duplicate indexed text)
 *   - Register FTS entity in @Database(entities = [..., ArticleFts::class])
 */

// ── Room Testing: MigrationTestHelper ──

/**
 * MigrationTestHelper validates that migrations work correctly by:
 *   1. Creating the database at an older version
 *   2. Running the migration
 *   3. Validating the schema matches expectations
 *
 * ⚠️ ALWAYS test migrations. A bad migration can corrupt user data or crash on launch.
 */

class MigrationTest {
    @get:Rule
    val helper = MigrationTestHelper(
        InstrumentationRegistry.getInstrumentation(),
        AppDatabase::class.java.canonicalName,
        FrameworkSQLiteOpenHelperFactory()
    )

    @Test
    fun migrate3to4() {
        // Create database at version 3
        helper.createDatabase("test_db", 3).apply {
            execSQL("""
                INSERT INTO articles (id, title, content, author_id, slug, published_at)
                VALUES ('1', 'Test', 'Body', 'author1', 'test-slug', 1234567890)
            """)
            close()
        }

        // Run migration and validate
        val db = helper.runMigrationsAndValidate("test_db", 4, true, MIGRATION_3_4)

        // Verify data survived
        val cursor = db.query("SELECT * FROM articles WHERE id = '1'")
        assertTrue(cursor.moveToFirst())
        assertEquals("Test", cursor.getString(cursor.getColumnIndex("title")))
        assertEquals(0, cursor.getInt(cursor.getColumnIndex("word_count")))
        cursor.close()
    }
}

// ── In-Memory Database for Unit Tests ──

class ArticleDaoTest {

    private lateinit var database: AppDatabase
    private lateinit var articleDao: ArticleDao

    @Before
    fun setup() {
        database = Room.inMemoryDatabaseBuilder(
            ApplicationProvider.getApplicationContext(),
            AppDatabase::class.java
        )
            .allowMainThreadQueries()  // Only in tests!
            .build()

        articleDao = database.articleDao()
    }

    @After
    fun teardown() {
        database.close()
    }

    @Test
    fun insertAndRetrieveArticle() = runTest {
        val article = ArticleEntity(
            id = "1", title = "Test", content = "Body",
            authorId = "a1", slug = "test", tags = emptyList(),
            publishedAt = System.currentTimeMillis()
        )
        articleDao.insert(article)
        val result = articleDao.getArticleById("1")
        assertEquals(article.title, result?.title)
    }

    @Test
    fun observeArticlesEmitsOnChange() = runTest {
        val emissions = mutableListOf<List<ArticleEntity>>()
        val job = launch {
            articleDao.observeAllArticles().take(2).collect { emissions.add(it) }
        }

        val article = ArticleEntity(
            id = "1", title = "Test", content = "Body",
            authorId = "a1", slug = "test", tags = emptyList(),
            publishedAt = System.currentTimeMillis()
        )
        articleDao.insert(article)
        advanceUntilIdle()
        job.join()

        assertEquals(2, emissions.size)
        assertTrue(emissions[0].isEmpty())
        assertEquals(1, emissions[1].size)
    }
}

/**
 * 💡 Testing best practices:
 *    - Use in-memory database for DAO unit tests (fast, no disk I/O)
 *    - Use MigrationTestHelper for migration tests (catches schema issues)
 *    - allowMainThreadQueries() only in tests — never in production
 *    - Test reactive queries (Flow) with Turbine or take(n) + advanceUntilIdle
 */


// ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
// PRACTICE QUESTIONS
// ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

/**
 * ❓ Q1: Why does Room require @Transaction for @Relation queries?
 *    A: Without it, the parent query and child query run as separate DB ops.
 *       Another thread could modify data between them, causing inconsistency.
 *       @Transaction wraps both queries in a single SQLite transaction.
 *
 * ❓ Q2: How would you handle a schema migration that renames a table?
 *    A: Use AutoMigration with @RenameTable spec, OR write a manual migration
 *       that creates the new table, copies data, drops old table, and renames.
 *       Always test migrations with Room's MigrationTestHelper.
 *
 * ❓ Q3: Room vs SQLite — what does Room add?
 *    A: Compile-time SQL verification, type-safe queries, Flow/LiveData support,
 *       automatic threading (suspend functions), migration support, testing helpers.
 *       ❌ Raw SQLite gives you none of these.
 *
