11_testing.kt · Kotlin reference

/**
 * ============================================================================
 * 11 — TESTING
 * Google SDE-2 Interview Prep
 * ============================================================================
 *
 * Topics Covered:
 *   1. Testing Pyramid & Strategy
 *   2. Unit Testing (JUnit, MockK, Turbine)
 *   3. Testing ViewModels
 *   4. Testing Repositories & UseCases
 *   5. Testing Coroutines & Flows
 *   6. UI Testing — Espresso (View System)
 *   7. UI Testing — Compose Test
 *   8. Fakes vs Mocks
 *   9. Robolectric
 *  10. Room Testing
 *  11. MockWebServer
 *  12. Screenshot / Visual Regression Testing
 *
 * ============================================================================
 */

// ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
// 1. TESTING PYRAMID & STRATEGY
// ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

/**
 * Testing Pyramid (bottom to top):
 *
 *        /  E2E Tests  \          ← Slow, flaky, few
 *       / Integration   \         ← Medium speed, moderate
 *      / Unit Tests      \        ← Fast, reliable, many
 *     /___________________\
 *
 * Google's recommendation for Android:
 *   70% Unit Tests      → ViewModel, Repository, UseCase, utils
 *   20% Integration     → Screen tests, Navigation, Room + DAO
 *   10% E2E Tests       → Critical user flows
 *
 * Key principles:
 *   - Test behavior, not implementation
 *   - Tests should be fast, reliable, and independent
 *   - One assertion per test (when practical)
 *   - Test names describe the scenario and expected outcome
 *   - Use Given-When-Then or Arrange-Act-Assert pattern
 */


// ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
// 2. UNIT TESTING FUNDAMENTALS
// ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

/**
 * Tools:
 *   JUnit 4/5  → test framework
 *   MockK      → mocking library (Kotlin-friendly)
 *   Truth      → assertion library (Google)
 *   Turbine    → Flow testing library
 *   kotlinx-coroutines-test → coroutine testing utilities
 */

// Basic unit test structure
class ArticleMapperTest {

    @Test
    fun `toEntity maps all fields correctly`() {
        // Arrange
        val response = ArticleResponse(
            id = "123",
            title = "Kotlin Coroutines",
            content = "Deep dive into coroutines",
            authorName = "John",
            publishedAt = 1700000000000,
            tags = listOf("kotlin", "android")
        )

        // Act
        val entity = response.toEntity()

        // Assert
        assertEquals("123", entity.id)
        assertEquals("Kotlin Coroutines", entity.title)
        assertEquals("John", entity.authorName)
        assertEquals(2, entity.tags.size)
    }

    @Test
    fun `toEntity uses default values for nullable fields`() {
        val response = ArticleResponse(
            id = "123",
            title = "Title",
            content = "Content",
            authorName = "Author",
            publishedAt = 0,
            tags = emptyList(),
            readTimeMinutes = null
        )

        val entity = response.toEntity()

        assertEquals(0, entity.readTimeMinutes)
    }
}


// ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
// 3. TESTING VIEWMODELS
// ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

class ArticleViewModelTest {

    // Replace Main dispatcher with test dispatcher
    @get:Rule
    val mainDispatcherRule = MainDispatcherRule()

    private lateinit var viewModel: ArticleViewModel
    private val fakeRepository = FakeArticleRepository()
    private val savedStateHandle = SavedStateHandle()

    @Before
    fun setup() {
        viewModel = ArticleViewModel(
            getArticlesUseCase = GetArticlesUseCase(fakeRepository),
            savedStateHandle = savedStateHandle
        )
    }

    @Test
    fun `initial state is loading`() {
        val state = viewModel.uiState.value
        assertTrue(state.isLoading)
        assertTrue(state.articles.isEmpty())
        assertNull(state.errorMessage)
    }

    @Test
    fun `loadArticles success updates state with articles`() = runTest {
        // Arrange
        val articles = listOf(
            Article(id = "1", title = "Article 1"),
            Article(id = "2", title = "Article 2")
        )
        fakeRepository.setArticles(articles)

        // Act
        viewModel.loadArticles()
        advanceUntilIdle()

        // Assert
        val state = viewModel.uiState.value
        assertFalse(state.isLoading)
        assertEquals(2, state.articles.size)
        assertNull(state.errorMessage)
    }

    @Test
    fun `loadArticles failure updates state with error`() = runTest {
        fakeRepository.setShouldFail(true)

        viewModel.loadArticles()
        advanceUntilIdle()

        val state = viewModel.uiState.value
        assertFalse(state.isLoading)
        assertNotNull(state.errorMessage)
        assertTrue(state.articles.isEmpty())
    }

    @Test
    fun `search saves query in savedStateHandle`() = runTest {
        viewModel.search("kotlin")
        advanceUntilIdle()

        assertEquals("kotlin", savedStateHandle.get<String>("query"))
    }

    // Testing with Turbine (Flow testing)
    @Test
    fun `uiState emits loading then success`() = runTest {
        val articles = listOf(Article(id = "1", title = "Test"))
        fakeRepository.setArticles(articles)
        fakeRepository.setDelay(100)

        viewModel.uiState.test {
            // Initial state
            val initial = awaitItem()
            assertTrue(initial.isLoading || initial.articles.isEmpty())

            viewModel.loadArticles()

            // May see loading state
            val loading = awaitItem()
            assertTrue(loading.isLoading)

            // Then success
            val success = awaitItem()
            assertFalse(success.isLoading)
            assertEquals(1, success.articles.size)

            cancelAndIgnoreRemainingEvents()
        }
    }
}


// ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
// 4. TESTING REPOSITORIES & USECASES
// ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

class ArticleRepositoryTest {

    private val fakeApi = FakeArticleApi()
    private val fakeDao = FakeArticleDao()
    private val testDispatcher = StandardTestDispatcher()

    private lateinit var repository: ArticleRepositoryImpl

    @Before
    fun setup() {
        repository = ArticleRepositoryImpl(
            api = fakeApi,
            dao = fakeDao,
            dispatcher = testDispatcher
        )
    }

    @Test
    fun `getArticles returns cached data when fresh`() = runTest(testDispatcher) {
        // Arrange - populate cache
        val cached = listOf(ArticleEntity(id = "1", title = "Cached", lastFetched = System.currentTimeMillis()))
        fakeDao.insertAll(cached)

        // Act
        val result = repository.getArticles("user1")

        // Assert - should NOT call API
        assertEquals(1, result.size)
        assertEquals(0, fakeApi.callCount)
    }

    @Test
    fun `getArticles fetches from API when cache is stale`() = runTest(testDispatcher) {
        // Arrange - stale cache
        val stale = listOf(ArticleEntity(id = "1", title = "Old", lastFetched = 0))
        fakeDao.insertAll(stale)
        fakeApi.setArticles(listOf(ArticleResponse(id = "2", title = "Fresh")))

        // Act
        val result = repository.getArticles("user1")

        // Assert - should call API and update cache
        assertEquals("Fresh", result.first().title)
        assertEquals(1, fakeApi.callCount)
    }

    @Test
    fun `observeArticles emits when database changes`() = runTest(testDispatcher) {
        repository.observeArticles().test {
            // Initially empty
            assertEquals(emptyList<Article>(), awaitItem())

            // Add data
            fakeDao.insertAll(listOf(ArticleEntity(id = "1", title = "New")))

            // Should emit new list
            val updated = awaitItem()
            assertEquals(1, updated.size)

            cancelAndIgnoreRemainingEvents()
        }
    }
}


// ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
// 5. TESTING COROUTINES & FLOWS
// ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

/**
 * Key testing utilities:
 *
 *   runTest { }              → runs test with virtual time (no real delays)
 *   advanceUntilIdle()       → advance all pending coroutines
 *   advanceTimeBy(ms)        → advance virtual clock by specific amount
 *   StandardTestDispatcher() → manual advancement (deterministic)
 *   UnconfinedTestDispatcher → immediate execution (less control)
 *   Turbine (.test { })      → structured Flow testing with assertions
 */

class FlowTestExample {

    @Test
    fun `debounced search flow emits correct results`() = runTest {
        val searchFlow = MutableSharedFlow<String>()
        val resultFlow = searchFlow
            .debounce(300)
            .filter { it.length >= 2 }
            .distinctUntilChanged()
            .map { query -> "Results for: $query" }

        resultFlow.test {
            searchFlow.emit("k")       // Too short, filtered
            searchFlow.emit("ko")      // Will be debounced
            searchFlow.emit("kot")     // Replaces "ko" during debounce

            advanceTimeBy(301)

            assertEquals("Results for: kot", awaitItem())

            searchFlow.emit("kot")     // Same value, distinctUntilChanged skips
            advanceTimeBy(301)
            expectNoEvents()           // No emission

            searchFlow.emit("kotlin")
            advanceTimeBy(301)
            assertEquals("Results for: kotlin", awaitItem())

            cancelAndIgnoreRemainingEvents()
        }
    }
}

// MainDispatcherRule for ViewModel tests
class MainDispatcherRule(
    private val testDispatcher: TestDispatcher = UnconfinedTestDispatcher()
) : TestWatcher() {
    override fun starting(description: Description) {
        Dispatchers.setMain(testDispatcher)
    }
    override fun finished(description: Description) {
        Dispatchers.resetMain()
    }
}


// ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
// 6. UI TESTING — ESPRESSO
// ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

/**
 * Espresso = UI testing framework for the View system.
 *
 * Core API:
 *   onView(matcher)          → find a view
 *   .perform(action)         → interact with it
 *   .check(assertion)        → verify its state
 */

@RunWith(AndroidJUnit4::class)
class ArticleListFragmentTest {

    @get:Rule
    val activityRule = ActivityScenarioRule(MainActivity::class.java)

    @Test
    fun displaysArticleList() {
        // Wait for list to load
        onView(withId(R.id.recycler_view))
            .check(matches(isDisplayed()))

        // Check first item
        onView(withText("Kotlin Coroutines"))
            .check(matches(isDisplayed()))
    }

    @Test
    fun clickArticleNavigatesToDetail() {
        onView(withText("Kotlin Coroutines"))
            .perform(click())

        // Verify navigation
        onView(withId(R.id.article_detail_container))
            .check(matches(isDisplayed()))
    }

    @Test
    fun swipeToRefreshUpdatesArticles() {
        onView(withId(R.id.swipe_refresh))
            .perform(swipeDown())

        // Verify new data appears
        onView(withText("New Article"))
            .check(matches(isDisplayed()))
    }
}


// ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
// 7. UI TESTING — COMPOSE
// ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

/**
 * Compose Testing API:
 *   composeTestRule.onNodeWithText("text")   → find by text
 *   .onNodeWithTag("tag")                    → find by test tag
 *   .onNodeWithContentDescription("desc")    → find by accessibility
 *   .performClick()                          → click action
 *   .performTextInput("text")                → type text
 *   .assertIsDisplayed()                     → visibility check
 *   .assertTextEquals("text")                → text content check
 */

class ArticleListScreenTest {

    @get:Rule
    val composeTestRule = createComposeRule()

    @Test
    fun `displays loading indicator initially`() {
        composeTestRule.setContent {
            ArticleListContent(
                uiState = ArticleListUiState(isLoading = true),
                onFilterChanged = {},
                onRefresh = {},
                onBookmarkClicked = {},
                onErrorDismissed = {}
            )
        }

        composeTestRule
            .onNodeWithTag("loading_indicator")
            .assertIsDisplayed()
    }

    @Test
    fun `displays articles when loaded`() {
        val articles = listOf(
            Article(id = "1", title = "Kotlin Coroutines"),
            Article(id = "2", title = "Jetpack Compose")
        )

        composeTestRule.setContent {
            ArticleListContent(
                uiState = ArticleListUiState(articles = articles),
                onFilterChanged = {},
                onRefresh = {},
                onBookmarkClicked = {},
                onErrorDismissed = {}
            )
        }

        composeTestRule.onNodeWithText("Kotlin Coroutines").assertIsDisplayed()
        composeTestRule.onNodeWithText("Jetpack Compose").assertIsDisplayed()
    }

    @Test
    fun `bookmark click calls callback with article id`() {
        var clickedId: String? = null
        val articles = listOf(Article(id = "1", title = "Test Article"))

        composeTestRule.setContent {
            ArticleListContent(
                uiState = ArticleListUiState(articles = articles),
                onFilterChanged = {},
                onRefresh = {},
                onBookmarkClicked = { clickedId = it },
                onErrorDismissed = {}
            )
        }

        composeTestRule
            .onNodeWithContentDescription("Bookmark Test Article")
            .performClick()

        assertEquals("1", clickedId)
    }

    @Test
    fun `error snackbar appears and can be dismissed`() {
        composeTestRule.setContent {
            ArticleListContent(
                uiState = ArticleListUiState(errorMessage = "Network error"),
                onFilterChanged = {},
                onRefresh = {},
                onBookmarkClicked = {},
                onErrorDismissed = {}
            )
        }

        composeTestRule.onNodeWithText("Network error").assertIsDisplayed()
    }
}


// ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
// 8. FAKES VS MOCKS
// ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

/**
 * Google prefers FAKES over MOCKS.
 *
 * Fake:
 *   - Working implementation with shortcuts (in-memory DB, static data)
 *   - Tests behavior, not implementation details
 *   - Reusable across tests
 *   - More resilient to refactoring
 *
 * Mock:
 *   - Verifies method calls and arguments
 *   - Tightly coupled to implementation
 *   - Brittle — breaks when implementation changes
 *   - Good for verifying interactions (e.g., analytics calls)
 *
 * 💡 Interview Tip: "I prefer fakes for data-providing dependencies
 *    (repositories, APIs, DAOs) and mocks for verifying interactions
 *    (analytics, logging, notifications)."
 */

// Fake Repository
class FakeArticleRepository : ArticleRepository {
    private val articles = mutableListOf<Article>()
    private val flow = MutableStateFlow<List<Article>>(emptyList())
    private var shouldFail = false
    private var delayMs = 0L

    fun setArticles(list: List<Article>) {
        articles.clear()
        articles.addAll(list)
        flow.value = list
    }

    fun setShouldFail(fail: Boolean) { shouldFail = fail }
    fun setDelay(ms: Long) { delayMs = ms }

    override fun observeArticles(): Flow<List<Article>> = flow

    override suspend fun getArticles(userId: String): List<Article> {
        if (delayMs > 0) delay(delayMs)
        if (shouldFail) throw IOException("Fake error")
        return articles.toList()
    }

    override suspend fun refreshArticles(userId: String): List<Article> {
        if (shouldFail) throw IOException("Fake error")
        return articles.toList()
    }
}

// Using MockK for interaction verification
class AnalyticsTest {
    private val analytics = mockk<AnalyticsHelper>(relaxed = true)

    @Test
    fun `screen view logged on navigation`() {
        val tracker = ScreenTracker(analytics)

        tracker.onScreenViewed("article_detail")

        verify { analytics.logEvent("screen_view", mapOf("screen" to "article_detail")) }
    }

    @Test
    fun `analytics not called for excluded screens`() {
        val tracker = ScreenTracker(analytics)

        tracker.onScreenViewed("splash")

        verify(exactly = 0) { analytics.logEvent(any(), any()) }
    }
}


// ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
// 9. ROBOLECTRIC
// ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

/**
 * Robolectric lets you run tests that depend on Android framework classes
 * on the JVM — no emulator or device needed.
 *
 * How it works:
 *   - Provides "shadow" classes that simulate Android framework behavior
 *   - Intercepts calls to android.* classes and routes them to shadows
 *   - Runs as a regular JUnit test (in test/ not androidTest/)
 *
 * When to use Robolectric vs instrumented tests:
 *   Robolectric   → fast feedback loop, CI-friendly, Activity/Fragment lifecycle tests,
 *                    testing code that uses Context, SharedPreferences, Resources
 *   Instrumented  → real device behavior, hardware-dependent features, Espresso UI tests,
 *                    Room with actual SQLite, accurate rendering tests
 *
 * Shadow classes:
 *   - ShadowApplication, ShadowActivity, ShadowLooper, ShadowAlarmManager, etc.
 *   - You can create custom shadows for your own classes
 *   - Access via Shadows.shadowOf(instance) to inspect internal state
 *
 * Limitations & gotchas:
 *   ⚠️ Rendering is not pixel-accurate (no real GPU)
 *   ⚠️ Some native APIs are not fully simulated (camera, Bluetooth)
 *   ⚠️ Shadow behavior may differ from real Android in edge cases
 *   ⚠️ Slower startup than pure unit tests (class loading overhead)
 *   ⚠️ Needs @Config(sdk = [X]) to pin SDK version for consistent behavior
 *
 * 💡 Interview Tip: "I use Robolectric when I need Android framework classes
 *    but want JVM speed. For anything that depends on real device behavior —
 *    especially UI rendering and SQLite — I use instrumented tests."
 */

@RunWith(RobolectricTestRunner::class)
@Config(sdk = [34])
class NotificationSchedulerTest {

    private val context: Context = ApplicationProvider.getApplicationContext()

    @Test
    fun `scheduleReminder sets alarm via AlarmManager`() {
        val alarmManager = context.getSystemService(Context.ALARM_SERVICE) as AlarmManager
        val shadowAlarmManager = Shadows.shadowOf(alarmManager)

        val scheduler = ReminderAlarmScheduler(context)
        val triggerTime = System.currentTimeMillis() + 60_000

        scheduler.scheduleReminder(1, triggerTime, "Test Reminder")

        val nextAlarm = shadowAlarmManager.nextScheduledAlarm
        assertNotNull(nextAlarm)
        assertEquals(AlarmManager.RTC_WAKEUP, nextAlarm.type)
    }

    @Test
    fun `activity launches with correct intent extras`() {
        val intent = Intent(context, ArticleDetailActivity::class.java).apply {
            putExtra("ARTICLE_ID", "123")
        }

        val scenario = ActivityScenario.launch<ArticleDetailActivity>(intent)
        scenario.onActivity { activity ->
            assertEquals("123", activity.intent.getStringExtra("ARTICLE_ID"))
        }
    }
}

@RunWith(RobolectricTestRunner::class)
class SharedPreferencesTest {

    @Test
    fun `saves and reads user preference`() {
        val context: Context = ApplicationProvider.getApplicationContext()
        val prefs = context.getSharedPreferences("settings", Context.MODE_PRIVATE)

        prefs.edit().putBoolean("dark_mode", true).apply()

        assertTrue(prefs.getBoolean("dark_mode", false))
    }
}


// ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
// 10. ROOM TESTING
// ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

/**
 * Room testing strategy:
 *
 *   In-memory database  → fast, no disk I/O, auto-cleared between tests
 *   MigrationTestHelper → verifies schema migrations don't lose data
 *   DAO tests           → instrumented tests (real SQLite) for accuracy
 *
 * Why instrumented (not Robolectric)?
 *   Room uses real SQLite under the hood. Robolectric's SQLite shadow can
 *   differ in behavior (e.g., foreign key enforcement, WAL mode). Google
 *   recommends instrumented tests for Room.
 *
 * 💡 Interview Tip: "I always test Room with an in-memory database on a real
 *    device or emulator to ensure SQLite behavior matches production."
 */

@RunWith(AndroidJUnit4::class)
class ArticleDaoTest {

    private lateinit var database: AppDatabase
    private lateinit var dao: ArticleDao

    @Before
    fun setup() {
        val context = ApplicationProvider.getApplicationContext<Context>()
        database = Room.inMemoryDatabaseBuilder(context, AppDatabase::class.java)
            .allowMainThreadQueries()  // OK for tests only
            .build()
        dao = database.articleDao()
    }

    @After
    fun tearDown() {
        database.close()
    }

    @Test
    fun insertAndRetrieveArticle() = runTest {
        val article = ArticleEntity(
            id = "1", title = "Testing Room", content = "Content",
            authorName = "Author", publishedAt = System.currentTimeMillis()
        )

        dao.insert(article)

        val result = dao.getArticleById("1")
        assertNotNull(result)
        assertEquals("Testing Room", result?.title)
    }

    @Test
    fun upsertReplacesExistingArticle() = runTest {
        val original = ArticleEntity(id = "1", title = "Original", content = "V1")
        val updated = ArticleEntity(id = "1", title = "Updated", content = "V2")

        dao.insert(original)
        dao.upsert(updated)

        val result = dao.getArticleById("1")
        assertEquals("Updated", result?.title)
        assertEquals("V2", result?.content)
    }

    @Test
    fun deleteRemovesArticle() = runTest {
        val article = ArticleEntity(id = "1", title = "To Delete", content = "Content")
        dao.insert(article)

        dao.deleteById("1")

        val result = dao.getArticleById("1")
        assertNull(result)
    }

    @Test
    fun observeArticlesEmitsOnChange() = runTest {
        dao.observeAll().test {
            assertEquals(emptyList<ArticleEntity>(), awaitItem())

            val article = ArticleEntity(id = "1", title = "New", content = "Content")
            dao.insert(article)

            val updated = awaitItem()
            assertEquals(1, updated.size)
            assertEquals("New", updated.first().title)

            cancelAndIgnoreRemainingEvents()
        }
    }
}

// Migration testing
@RunWith(AndroidJUnit4::class)
class MigrationTest {

    @get:Rule
    val helper = MigrationTestHelper(
        InstrumentationRegistry.getInstrumentation(),
        AppDatabase::class.java.canonicalName,
        FrameworkSQLiteOpenHelperFactory()
    )

    @Test
    fun migrate1To2() {
        // Create database at version 1
        helper.createDatabase("test_db", 1).apply {
            execSQL("INSERT INTO articles (id, title, content) VALUES ('1', 'Test', 'Content')")
            close()
        }

        // Run migration to version 2
        val db = helper.runMigrationsAndValidate("test_db", 2, true, MIGRATION_1_2)

        // Verify data survived migration and new column has default value
        val cursor = db.query("SELECT * FROM articles WHERE id = '1'")
        assertTrue(cursor.moveToFirst())
        assertEquals("Test", cursor.getString(cursor.getColumnIndex("title")))
        assertEquals(0, cursor.getInt(cursor.getColumnIndex("bookmark_count")))
        cursor.close()
    }
}


// ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
// 11. MOCKWEBSERVER
// ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

/**
 * OkHttp MockWebServer provides a local HTTP server for testing API interactions.
 *
 * Why use it:
 *   - Test real HTTP behavior (serialization, headers, error codes)
 *   - Verify request format (path, method, headers, body)
 *   - Simulate network conditions (slow responses, timeouts, errors)
 *   - No flaky network dependency in CI
 *
 * How it works:
 *   1. Start MockWebServer on localhost
 *   2. Point Retrofit at mockWebServer.url("/")
 *   3. Enqueue mock responses
 *   4. Execute API calls — they hit the local server
 *   5. Verify requests with takeRequest()
 *
 * 💡 Interview Tip: "I use MockWebServer to test the full networking stack —
 *    Retrofit, converters, interceptors — without real network calls.
 *    Fakes are great for repository tests, but MockWebServer catches
 *    serialization bugs that fakes miss."
 */

class ArticleApiTest {

    private lateinit var mockWebServer: MockWebServer
    private lateinit var api: ArticleApi

    @Before
    fun setup() {
        mockWebServer = MockWebServer()
        mockWebServer.start()

        val retrofit = Retrofit.Builder()
            .baseUrl(mockWebServer.url("/"))
            .client(OkHttpClient.Builder().build())
            .addConverterFactory(Json.asConverterFactory("application/json".toMediaType()))
            .build()

        api = retrofit.create(ArticleApi::class.java)
    }

    @After
    fun tearDown() {
        mockWebServer.shutdown()
    }

    @Test
    fun `getArticles parses response correctly`() = runTest {
        val json = """
            [
                {"id": "1", "title": "Kotlin Coroutines", "content": "Deep dive"},
                {"id": "2", "title": "Jetpack Compose", "content": "Modern UI"}
            ]
        """.trimIndent()

        mockWebServer.enqueue(
            MockResponse()
                .setResponseCode(200)
                .setBody(json)
                .setHeader("Content-Type", "application/json")
        )

        val articles = api.getArticles("user1")

        assertEquals(2, articles.size)
        assertEquals("Kotlin Coroutines", articles[0].title)
    }

    @Test
    fun `getArticles sends correct request`() = runTest {
        mockWebServer.enqueue(MockResponse().setBody("[]").setResponseCode(200))

        api.getArticles("user1")

        val request = mockWebServer.takeRequest()
        assertEquals("GET", request.method)
        assertTrue(request.path!!.contains("user1"))
    }

    @Test
    fun `getArticles throws on server error`() = runTest {
        mockWebServer.enqueue(MockResponse().setResponseCode(500))

        try {
            api.getArticles("user1")
            fail("Expected HttpException")
        } catch (e: HttpException) {
            assertEquals(500, e.code())
        }
    }

    @Test
    fun `getArticles handles slow response`() = runTest {
        mockWebServer.enqueue(
            MockResponse()
                .setBody("[]")
                .setBodyDelay(2, TimeUnit.SECONDS)
        )

        val client = OkHttpClient.Builder()
            .readTimeout(1, TimeUnit.SECONDS)
            .build()

        val slowApi = Retrofit.Builder()
            .baseUrl(mockWebServer.url("/"))
            .client(client)
            .addConverterFactory(Json.asConverterFactory("application/json".toMediaType()))
            .build()
            .create(ArticleApi::class.java)

        try {
            slowApi.getArticles("user1")
            fail("Expected timeout")
        } catch (e: Exception) {
            assertTrue(e is java.net.SocketTimeoutException)
        }
    }
}


// ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
// 12. SCREENSHOT / VISUAL REGRESSION TESTING
// ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

/**
 * Screenshot testing catches unintended visual changes by comparing UI
 * snapshots against golden (reference) images.
 *
 * Tools:
 *
 * 1. Paparazzi (Cash App / Square):
 *    - Runs on JVM — no emulator needed
 *    - Renders Android views and Compose using layoutlib (the same renderer
 *      Android Studio uses for previews)
 *    - Records golden images, then verifies against them in CI
 *    - Commands: ./gradlew :app:recordPaparazziDebug (record)
 *               ./gradlew :app:verifyPaparazziDebug  (verify)
 *
 * 2. Compose Preview Screenshot Testing (Google, experimental):
 *    - Uses @Preview composables as screenshot test sources
 *    - Runs on JVM with layoutlib
 *    - Tightly integrated with Compose preview workflow
 *    - Commands: ./gradlew updateDebugScreenshotTest (record)
 *               ./gradlew validateDebugScreenshotTest (verify)
 *
 * When to use:
 *   ✅ Design system components (buttons, cards, inputs)
 *   ✅ Complex layouts with multiple states (loading, error, empty, populated)
 *   ✅ Dark mode / light mode verification
 *   ✅ Accessibility — large font / RTL layout checks
 *   ✅ Preventing accidental regressions in shared components
 *
 * CI integration:
 *   - Run verify task in CI pipeline on every PR
 *   - Record task generates/updates golden images (commit to repo)
 *   - Failed verification produces a diff image showing what changed
 *   - Review diffs in PR to decide if changes are intentional
 *
 * ⚠️ Gotchas:
 *   - Golden images are platform-sensitive (record + verify on same OS/JDK)
 *   - Dynamic content (timestamps, animations) must be pinned for determinism
 *   - Large golden image repos can bloat git — consider LFS
 *
 * 💡 Interview Tip: "I use Paparazzi for screenshot tests because it runs
 *    on JVM, making it fast and CI-friendly. It's especially valuable for
 *    design system components where visual regressions are costly."
 */

// Paparazzi test example
class ArticleCardScreenshotTest {

    @get:Rule

// ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
// PRACTICE QUESTIONS
// ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

/**
 * ❓ Q1: How do you test a ViewModel that uses viewModelScope?
 *    A: Use MainDispatcherRule to replace Dispatchers.Main with TestDispatcher.
 *       Use runTest {} for coroutine testing. Use advanceUntilIdle() to
 *       complete all pending coroutines. Use Turbine for Flow assertions.
 *
 * ❓ Q2: How do you test Room database operations?
 *    A: Use Room.inMemoryDatabaseBuilder() for fast, isolated tests.
 *       Run on Android device/emulator (instrumented test).
 *       Test: insert → query → verify. Test: migration paths.
 *
 * ❓ Q3: What makes a good test?
 *    A: Fast, isolated, repeatable, self-validating, timely (FIRST).
 *       Tests behavior not implementation. Clear name describes scenario.
 *       Single reason to fail. Arranges minimal context.
 *
 * ❓ Q4: How do you handle flaky tests?
 *    A: Identify the flakiness source: timing (use TestDispatcher, IdlingResource),
 *       shared state (isolate tests), network (use fakes).
 *       For Espresso: use IdlingResource for async operations.
 *       For Compose: use waitUntil {} for async assertions.
 *
 * ❓ Q5: When do you use instrumented tests vs local tests?
 *    A: Local (JVM): ViewModel, UseCase, Repository, mappers — fast feedback.
 *       Instrumented (device): Room DB, UI tests, integration tests.
 *       Rule of thumb: run as much as possible locally.
 *
 * ❓ Q6: When would you use Robolectric vs instrumented tests?
 *    A: Robolectric: when you need Android framework classes (Context,
 *       SharedPreferences, Activity lifecycle) but want JVM speed. Great for
 *       CI pipelines — no emulator needed. Instrumented: when behavior must
 *       match real Android exactly — Room/SQLite, hardware features, pixel-accurate
 *       UI rendering, multi-process scenarios. Robolectric's shadows approximate
 *       real behavior but can diverge in edge cases, so anything SQLite or
 *       rendering-sensitive should run instrumented.
 *
 * ❓ Q7: How do you use MockWebServer to test API interactions?
 *    A: Start MockWebServer, point Retrofit's baseUrl at mockWebServer.url("/").
 *       Enqueue MockResponse objects with status codes, JSON bodies, and headers.
 *       Execute API calls — they hit the local server. Use takeRequest() to
 *       verify the request method, path, headers, and body. Simulate errors
 *       with setResponseCode(500), slow responses with setBodyDelay(),
 *       and timeouts by combining delay with a short client timeout. This tests
 *       the full networking stack including serialization and interceptors.
 */

12 Security

12_security.kt · Kotlin reference

/**
 * ============================================================================
 * 12 — SECURITY
 * Google SDE-2 Interview Prep
 * ============================================================================
 *
 * Topics Covered:
 *   1. Runtime Permissions Model
 *   2. Network Security Configuration
 *   3. Certificate Pinning
 *   4. Android Keystore System
 *   5. Encrypted Storage (EncryptedSharedPreferences, EncryptedFile)
 *   6. Biometric Authentication
 *   7. Common Vulnerabilities & Mitigations
 *   8. ProGuard / R8 — Code Obfuscation
 *   9. Content Provider Security
 *  10. WebView Security
 *  11. Play Integrity API
 *
 * ============================================================================
 */

// ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
// 1. RUNTIME PERMISSIONS MODEL
// ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

/**
 * Permission types:
 *   Normal      → granted automatically at install (INTERNET, VIBRATE)
 *   Dangerous   → requires runtime request (CAMERA, LOCATION, CONTACTS)
 *   Special     → requires Settings intent (SYSTEM_ALERT_WINDOW, WRITE_SETTINGS)
 *
 * Permission groups:
 *   ⚠️ Don't rely on groups — Google can change grouping at any time.
 *   Always request the specific permission you need.
 *
 * API 33+ changes:
 *   - POST_NOTIFICATIONS is now a runtime permission
 *   - Photo picker doesn't need READ_MEDIA_IMAGES for basic access
 *   - Granular media permissions (READ_MEDIA_IMAGES, READ_MEDIA_VIDEO, READ_MEDIA_AUDIO)
 *
 * API 34+ changes:
 *   - Partial photo/video access (user selects specific files)
 *   - READ_MEDIA_VISUAL_USER_SELECTED for partial access
 */

// Modern permission handling with Activity Result API
class PermissionActivity : AppCompatActivity() {

    // Single permission
    private val cameraPermissionLauncher = registerForActivityResult(
        ActivityResultContracts.RequestPermission()
    ) { isGranted ->
        if (isGranted) {
            openCamera()
        } else {
            // Check if we should show rationale
            if (shouldShowRequestPermissionRationale(Manifest.permission.CAMERA)) {
                showRationaleDialog()
            } else {
                // User permanently denied — direct to Settings
                showSettingsDialog()
            }
        }
    }

    // Multiple permissions
    private val locationPermissionLauncher = registerForActivityResult(
        ActivityResultContracts.RequestMultiplePermissions()
    ) { permissions ->
        val fineLocationGranted = permissions[Manifest.permission.ACCESS_FINE_LOCATION] ?: false
        val coarseLocationGranted = permissions[Manifest.permission.ACCESS_COARSE_LOCATION] ?: false

        when {
            fineLocationGranted -> enablePreciseLocation()
            coarseLocationGranted -> enableApproximateLocation()
            else -> showLocationDeniedMessage()
        }
    }

    fun requestCameraPermission() {
        when {
            ContextCompat.checkSelfPermission(
                this, Manifest.permission.CAMERA
            ) == PackageManager.PERMISSION_GRANTED -> {
                openCamera()
            }
            shouldShowRequestPermissionRationale(Manifest.permission.CAMERA) -> {
                showRationaleDialog()
            }
            else -> {
                cameraPermissionLauncher.launch(Manifest.permission.CAMERA)
            }
        }
    }
}


// ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
// 2. NETWORK SECURITY CONFIGURATION
// ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

/**
 * Network Security Config (res/xml/network_security_config.xml):
 *   Controls app's network security behavior declaratively.
 *
 * Key features:
 *   - Cleartext traffic policy (HTTP vs HTTPS)
 *   - Custom trust anchors (CA certificates)
 *   - Certificate pinning
 *   - Debug overrides
 *
 * API 28+: Cleartext (HTTP) blocked by default.
 * API 24+: User-installed CAs not trusted by default.
 */

// Example network_security_config.xml
/*
<?xml version="1.0" encoding="utf-8"?>
<network-security-config>
    <!-- Default: HTTPS only, system CAs only -->
    <base-config cleartextTrafficPermitted="false">
        <trust-anchors>
            <certificates src="system" />
        </trust-anchors>
    </base-config>

    <!-- Exception: allow cleartext for local dev server -->
    <domain-config cleartextTrafficPermitted="true">
        <domain includeSubdomains="true">10.0.2.2</domain>
    </domain-config>

    <!-- Certificate pinning for production API -->
    <domain-config>
        <domain includeSubdomains="true">api.example.com</domain>
        <pin-set expiration="2027-12-31">
            <pin digest="SHA-256">base64EncodedPin1=</pin>
            <pin digest="SHA-256">base64EncodedBackupPin=</pin>
        </pin-set>
    </domain-config>

    <!-- Debug only: trust user-installed CAs for proxy tools -->
    <debug-overrides>
        <trust-anchors>
            <certificates src="user" />
        </trust-anchors>
    </debug-overrides>
</network-security-config>
*/


// ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
// 3. CERTIFICATE PINNING — DEEP DIVE
// ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

/**
 * Certificate pinning = only trust specific public keys (SPKI hashes) for a domain.
 * Prevents MITM even if a CA is compromised or a rogue cert is installed.
 *
 * What to pin:
 *   - Public key hash (SHA-256 of SubjectPublicKeyInfo) — survives cert renewal
 *     if the same key pair is reused
 *   - NOT the full certificate — expires with cert and breaks on every rotation
 *
 * Approaches:
 *   1. Network Security Config (XML) — recommended, declarative, debug overrides
 *   2. OkHttp CertificatePinner — programmatic, per-client control
 *   3. Custom TrustManager — last resort; easy to get wrong
 *
 * ⚠️ Pin rotation strategy (critical for production):
 *   - Always ship TWO pins: current + backup (next cert's SPKI hash)
 *   - Set pin-set expiration in XML so old pins auto-expire
 *   - Coordinate with backend/infra before cert rotation
 *   - Have a remote-config kill switch to disable pinning in emergencies
 *   - Without backup pin, a cert rotation BRICKS the app for all users
 *
 * How to extract SPKI pin (for OkHttp or XML):
 *   openssl s_client -connect api.example.com:443 | \
 *     openssl x509 -pubkey -noout | \
 *     openssl pkey -pubin -outform der | \
 *     openssl dgst -sha256 -binary | openssl enc -base64
 *
 * 💡 Interview tip: Pinning protects against MITM; it does NOT stop reverse
 *    engineering or rooted-device tampering — pair with Play Integrity for that.
 */

// OkHttp — programmatic pinning with backup pin
fun createPinnedClient(): OkHttpClient {
    val certificatePinner = CertificatePinner.Builder()
        .add(
            "api.example.com",
            "sha256/CURRENT_SPKI_HASH=",   // Active cert public key
            "sha256/BACKUP_SPKI_HASH="     // Next rotation — ship before switch
        )
        .build()

    return OkHttpClient.Builder()
        .certificatePinner(certificatePinner)
        .build()
}

// Pinning failure handling — log for monitoring, don't crash silently
class PinningEventListener : EventListener() {
    override fun secureConnectEnd(call: Call, handshake: Handshake?) {
        // Handshake succeeded — pin matched
    }
    // CertificatePinningException thrown on mismatch — catch at call site
}

// Remote-config fallback pattern (pseudo-code)
/*
fun createApiClient(remoteConfig: RemoteConfig): OkHttpClient {
    val builder = OkHttpClient.Builder()
    if (remoteConfig.getBoolean("cert_pinning_enabled", default = true)) {
        builder.certificatePinner(buildCertificatePinner())
    }
    return builder.build()
}
*/

/**
 * ❓ Follow-up: What happens when a pin doesn't match?
 *    A: OkHttp throws SSLPeerUnverifiedException / CertificatePinningException.
 *       All API calls fail. Users see network errors. Fix: ship app update with
 *       new pin OR disable pinning via remote config (if you planned for it).
 *
 * ❓ Follow-up: XML pinning vs OkHttp pinning — when to use which?
 *    A: XML for app-wide default (WebView, Cronet, default OkHttp if configured).
 *       OkHttp for per-client pins (different APIs, staging vs prod clients).
 *       Use both consistently — mismatched configs cause confusing debug sessions.
 */


// ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
// 11. PLAY INTEGRITY API (replaces SafetyNet Attestation)
// ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

/**
 * Play Integrity API verifies the app's runtime environment before trusting
 * sensitive operations (payments, premium features, anti-fraud).
 *
 * Verdict tokens (server-side decode only — never trust client-side):
 *   - deviceIntegrity: MEETS_DEVICE_INTEGRITY, MEETS_BASIC_INTEGRITY, etc.
 *   - appIntegrity: PLAY_RECOGNIZED, UNRECOGNIZED_VERSION
 *   - accountDetails: app licensing status
 *
 * vs SafetyNet (deprecated):
 *   SafetyNet Attestation → replaced by Play Integrity
 *   SafetyNet Verify Apps → replaced by Play Protect APIs
 *
 * Architecture:
 *   App → requestIntegrityToken() → Google Play → signed JWT token
 *   App → send token to YOUR backend → decode with Google API → allow/deny
 *
 * ⚠️ Never make security decisions solely on-device. Always verify server-side.
 *
 * 💡 Interview tip: Play Integrity detects rooted/emulated/tampered environments.
 *    It's not foolproof (Magisk hide, custom ROMs) — use as one signal in a
 *    layered defense, not the only gate.
 */

// Client-side: request integrity token before sensitive API call
class IntegrityChecker(
    private val integrityManager: IntegrityManager,
    private val cloudProjectNumber: Long  // from Google Cloud Console
) {
    suspend fun requestToken(): String = suspendCancellableCoroutine { cont ->
        val request = IntegrityTokenRequest.builder()
            .setCloudProjectNumber(cloudProjectNumber)
            .build()

        integrityManager.requestIntegrityToken(request)
            .addOnSuccessListener { response ->
                if (cont.isActive) cont.resume(response.token())
            }
            .addOnFailureListener { e ->
                if (cont.isActive) cont.resumeWithException(e)
            }
    }
}

// Usage in repository — token sent to backend, NOT decoded on device
class PaymentRepository(
    private val api: PaymentApi,
    private val integrityChecker: IntegrityChecker
) {
    suspend fun initiatePayment(amount: Cents): Result<PaymentResult> {
        return try {
            val integrityToken = integrityChecker.requestToken()
            val response = api.createPayment(
                PaymentRequest(amount = amount, integrityToken = integrityToken)
            )
            Result.success(response)
        } catch (e: Exception) {
            Result.failure(e)
        }
    }
}

/**
 * Server-side verification (conceptual — runs on your backend, not Android):
 *
 *   POST https://playintegrity.googleapis.com/v1/{packageName}:decodeIntegrityToken
 *   Body: { "integrityToken": "<token from app>" }
 *
 *   Check response.payload:
 *     deviceIntegrity.deviceRecognitionVerdict contains "MEETS_DEVICE_INTEGRITY"
 *     appIntegrity.appRecognitionVerdict == "PLAY_RECOGNIZED"
 *     requestDetails.requestPackageName matches your app
 *
 * Decision matrix (example):
 *   ┌─────────────────────────────┬────────────────────────────────────┐
 *   │ Verdict                     │ Action                             │
 *   ├─────────────────────────────┼────────────────────────────────────┤
 *   │ Full integrity + recognized │ Allow sensitive operation          │
 *   │ Basic integrity only        │ Allow read-only, block payments    │
 *   │ App unrecognized (sideload) │ Block or limit features            │
 *   │ Token decode fails          │ Reject — possible tampering        │
 *   └─────────────────────────────┴────────────────────────────────────┘
 */

/**
 * ❓ Q6: How does Play Integrity differ from certificate pinning?
 *    A: Pinning = protect network traffic from MITM (transport layer).
 *       Play Integrity = verify app binary + device environment (runtime layer).
 *       Complementary: pin API calls, verify device before high-value actions.
 *
 * ❓ Q7: What if Play Integrity API is unavailable (no Play Store, China devices)?
 *    A: Graceful degradation — don't hard-crash. Fall back to reduced functionality,
 *       server-side rate limiting, or alternate auth. Document the trade-off.
 *
 * ❓ Q8: How do you test Play Integrity in development?
 *    A: Use Play Console → App Integrity → test responses. Register test devices.
 *       Debug builds won't get PLAY_RECOGNIZED — test the failure path too.
 */

// ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

/**
 * Android Keystore = hardware-backed secure storage for cryptographic keys.
 *
 * Key features:
 *   - Keys never leave the secure hardware (TEE/Strongbox)
 *   - Keys can require user authentication (biometric)
 *   - Keys can have validity periods
 *   - Extraction is computationally infeasible
 *
 * Use cases:
 *   - Encrypting local data
 *   - Signing data for API authentication
 *   - Biometric-gated key access
 */

object KeystoreHelper {

    private const val KEY_ALIAS = "app_encryption_key"

    fun getOrCreateKey(): SecretKey {
        val keyStore = KeyStore.getInstance("AndroidKeyStore")
        keyStore.load(null)

        // Return existing key if available
        keyStore.getKey(KEY_ALIAS, null)?.let { return it as SecretKey }

        // Generate new key
        val keyGenerator = KeyGenerator.getInstance(
            KeyProperties.KEY_ALGORITHM_AES, "AndroidKeyStore"
        )
        keyGenerator.init(
            KeyGenParameterSpec.Builder(
                KEY_ALIAS,
                KeyProperties.PURPOSE_ENCRYPT or KeyProperties.PURPOSE_DECRYPT
            )
                .setBlockModes(KeyProperties.BLOCK_MODE_GCM)
                .setEncryptionPaddings(KeyProperties.ENCRYPTION_PADDING_NONE)
                .setKeySize(256)
                .setUserAuthenticationRequired(false)
                .build()
        )
        return keyGenerator.generateKey()
    }

    fun encrypt(data: ByteArray): Pair<ByteArray, ByteArray> {
        val key = getOrCreateKey()
        val cipher = Cipher.getInstance("AES/GCM/NoPadding")
        cipher.init(Cipher.ENCRYPT_MODE, key)
        val iv = cipher.iv
        val encrypted = cipher.doFinal(data)
        return Pair(iv, encrypted)
    }

    fun decrypt(iv: ByteArray, encryptedData: ByteArray): ByteArray {
        val key = getOrCreateKey()
        val cipher = Cipher.getInstance("AES/GCM/NoPadding")
        val spec = GCMParameterSpec(128, iv)
        cipher.init(Cipher.DECRYPT_MODE, key, spec)
        return cipher.doFinal(encryptedData)
    }
}


// ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
// 5. ENCRYPTED STORAGE
// ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

/**
 * EncryptedSharedPreferences (AndroidX Security):
 *   - AES-256 encryption for keys and values
 *   - Backed by Android Keystore
 *   - Drop-in replacement for SharedPreferences
 */

fun createEncryptedPrefs(context: Context): SharedPreferences {
    val masterKey = MasterKey.Builder(context)
        .setKeyScheme(MasterKey.KeyScheme.AES256_GCM)
        .build()

    return EncryptedSharedPreferences.create(
        context,
        "secure_prefs",
        masterKey,
        EncryptedSharedPreferences.PrefKeyEncryptionScheme.AES256_SIV,
        EncryptedSharedPreferences.PrefValueEncryptionScheme.AES256_GCM
    )
}

// Store sensitive tokens
fun storeAuthToken(prefs: SharedPreferences, token: String) {
    prefs.edit().putString("auth_token", token).apply()
    // Stored encrypted on disk — safe even if device is rooted
}


// ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
// 6. BIOMETRIC AUTHENTICATION
// ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

class BiometricHelper(private val activity: FragmentActivity) {

    fun authenticate(
        onSuccess: () -> Unit,
        onError: (String) -> Unit
    ) {
        val biometricManager = BiometricManager.from(activity)
        when (biometricManager.canAuthenticate(BiometricManager.Authenticators.BIOMETRIC_STRONG)) {
            BiometricManager.BIOMETRIC_SUCCESS -> {
                showBiometricPrompt(onSuccess, onError)
            }
            BiometricManager.BIOMETRIC_ERROR_NO_HARDWARE ->
                onError("No biometric hardware")
            BiometricManager.BIOMETRIC_ERROR_HW_UNAVAILABLE ->
                onError("Biometric hardware unavailable")
            BiometricManager.BIOMETRIC_ERROR_NONE_ENROLLED ->
                onError("No biometrics enrolled")
        }
    }

    private fun showBiometricPrompt(
        onSuccess: () -> Unit,
        onError: (String) -> Unit
    ) {
        val promptInfo = BiometricPrompt.PromptInfo.Builder()
            .setTitle("Authenticate")
            .setSubtitle("Verify your identity")
            .setNegativeButtonText("Cancel")
            .setAllowedAuthenticators(BiometricManager.Authenticators.BIOMETRIC_STRONG)
            .build()

        val biometricPrompt = BiometricPrompt(
            activity,
            ContextCompat.getMainExecutor(activity),
            object : BiometricPrompt.AuthenticationCallback() {
                override fun onAuthenticationSucceeded(result: BiometricPrompt.AuthenticationResult) {
                    onSuccess()
                }

                override fun onAuthenticationError(errorCode: Int, errString: CharSequence) {
                    onError(errString.toString())
                }

                override fun onAuthenticationFailed() {
                    // Don't close dialog — user can retry
                }
            }
        )

        biometricPrompt.authenticate(promptInfo)
    }
}


// ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
// 7. COMMON VULNERABILITIES & MITIGATIONS
// ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

/**
 * ┌─────────────────────────────┬──────────────────────────────────────────┐
 * │ Vulnerability               │ Mitigation                               │
 * ├─────────────────────────────┼──────────────────────────────────────────┤
 * │ Insecure data storage       │ EncryptedSharedPreferences, Keystore     │
 * │ (plaintext tokens)          │                                          │
 * ├─────────────────────────────┼──────────────────────────────────────────┤
 * │ Man-in-the-middle           │ Certificate pinning, HTTPS only          │
 * │                             │                                          │
 * ├─────────────────────────────┼──────────────────────────────────────────┤
 * │ SQL injection               │ Room parameterized queries (never        │
 * │                             │ concatenate user input in queries)       │
 * ├─────────────────────────────┼──────────────────────────────────────────┤
 * │ Intent spoofing             │ exported=false, explicit intents,        │
 * │                             │ signature permissions                    │
 * ├─────────────────────────────┼──────────────────────────────────────────┤
 * │ WebView vulnerabilities     │ Disable JavaScript when not needed,      │
 * │                             │ validate URLs, disable file access       │
 * ├─────────────────────────────┼──────────────────────────────────────────┤
 * │ Reverse engineering         │ R8 obfuscation, tamper detection,        │
 * │                             │ Play Integrity API                       │
 * ├─────────────────────────────┼──────────────────────────────────────────┤
 * │ Insecure broadcast          │ LocalBroadcastManager (deprecated) →     │
 * │                             │ use SharedFlow. Or signature permission  │
 * ├─────────────────────────────┼──────────────────────────────────────────┤
 * │ Clipboard data theft        │ Don't put sensitive data on clipboard    │
 * │                             │ Use FLAG_SECURE for sensitive screens    │
 * └─────────────────────────────┴──────────────────────────────────────────┘
 */


// ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
// 8. PROGUARD / R8 — CODE OBFUSCATION
// ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

/**
 * R8 is the default code shrinker/obfuscator in Android (replaced ProGuard).
 * Same rule format as ProGuard, but faster and Kotlin-aware.
 *
 * Three stages:
 *   1. Shrinking  — removes unused classes, methods, fields
 *   2. Obfuscation — renames classes/members to short meaningless names (a, b, c)
 *   3. Optimization — inlines methods, removes dead branches, simplifies code
 *
 * R8 full mode vs compat mode:
 *   Compat (default): behaves like ProGuard — safe, fewer surprises
 *   Full mode: more aggressive optimizations — may break reflection-based code
 *     Enable: android.enableR8.fullMode=true in gradle.properties
 *
 * Mapping files:
 *   R8 produces mapping.txt (maps obfuscated names → original names).
 *   Upload to Play Console → crash stack traces are auto-deobfuscated.
 *   Keep mapping files for every release build you ship.
 */

// Example proguard-rules.pro
/*
# ── Keep rules ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────

# Keep entire class (class name + all members)
-keep class com.example.model.** { *; }

# Keep class members only (class can be renamed)
-keepclassmembers class com.example.model.User {
    <fields>;
}

# Keep class name only (members can be renamed)
-keepnames class com.example.api.ApiResponse

# ── Common rules needed ─────────────────────────────────────────────

# Retrofit (uses reflection for interface proxies)
-keepattributes Signature
-keepattributes *Annotation*
-keep class retrofit2.** { *; }
-keepclassmembers,allowobfuscation interface * {
    @retrofit2.http.* <methods>;
}

# Kotlinx Serialization
-keepattributes *Annotation*, InnerClasses
-keep,includedescriptorclasses class com.example.**$$serializer { *; }
-keepclassmembers class com.example.** {
    *** Companion;
}

# Parcelable
-keep class * implements android.os.Parcelable {
    public static final ** CREATOR;
}

# Enum classes
-keepclassmembers enum * {
    public static **[] values();
    public static ** valueOf(java.lang.String);
}

# Keep R8 from stripping Google Play Integrity / SafetyNet
-keep class com.google.android.play.core.** { *; }
*/


// ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
// 9. CONTENT PROVIDER SECURITY
// ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

/**
 * Content Providers expose structured data between apps.
 * Major security concern: they're an IPC attack surface.
 *
 * Key attributes:
 *   exported="false" → only your app can access (default for API 31+ if no intent-filter)
 *   exported="true"  → other apps can query/insert/update/delete
 *
 * Permissions:
 *   android:readPermission  → required to query
 *   android:writePermission → required to insert/update/delete
 *   android:permission      → required for both read and write
 *
 * Path-level permissions (android:pathPermission):
 *   Different permissions for different URI paths.
 *
 * grantUriPermissions:
 *   Allows temporary access to specific URIs even without the full permission.
 *   Essential for sharing files via FileProvider.
 *
 * SQL injection prevention:
 *   NEVER concatenate user input into queries.
 *   Use selection args (parameterized queries) or Room.
 */

// Secure ContentProvider with FileProvider for sharing files
/*
<!-- AndroidManifest.xml -->
<provider
    android:name="androidx.core.content.FileProvider"
    android:authorities="${applicationId}.fileprovider"
    android:exported="false"
    android:grantUriPermissions="true">
    <meta-data
        android:name="android.support.FILE_PROVIDER_PATHS"
        android:resource="@xml/file_paths" />
</provider>
*/

// Sharing a file securely with another app
fun shareFile(context: Context, file: File) {
    val uri = FileProvider.getUriForFile(
        context,
        "${context.packageName}.fileprovider",
        file
    )

    val shareIntent = Intent(Intent.ACTION_SEND).apply {
        type = "application/pdf"
        putExtra(Intent.EXTRA_STREAM, uri)
        // Grant read-only access to the receiving app
        addFlags(Intent.FLAG_GRANT_READ_URI_PERMISSION)
    }
    context.startActivity(Intent.createChooser(shareIntent, "Share file"))
}

// Parameterized queries prevent SQL injection
class SecureProvider : ContentProvider() {

    override fun query(
        uri: Uri,
        projection: Array<String>?,
        selection: String?,
        selectionArgs: Array<String>?,  // ← always use selection args
        sortOrder: String?
    ): Cursor? {
        // ✅ Safe: parameterized
        // SELECT * FROM notes WHERE author = ?   selectionArgs = ["Alice"]

        // ❌ Dangerous: string concatenation
        // "SELECT * FROM notes WHERE author = '$userInput'"
        // userInput = "'; DROP TABLE notes; --"

        return db.query("notes", projection, selection, selectionArgs, null, null, sortOrder)
    }

    // ... other required overrides
    override fun onCreate(): Boolean = true
    override fun getType(uri: Uri): String? = null
    override fun insert(uri: Uri, values: ContentValues?): Uri? = null
    override fun delete(uri: Uri, sel: String?, selArgs: Array<String>?): Int = 0
    override fun update(uri: Uri, values: ContentValues?, sel: String?, selArgs: Array<String>?): Int = 0
}


// ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
// 10. WEBVIEW SECURITY
// ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

/**
 * WebView is one of the most dangerous components on Android.
 * It's essentially a browser engine with access to native APIs.
 *
 * JavaScript interface risks:
 *   @JavascriptInterface exposes Kotlin/Java methods to JS code.
 *   Only annotated methods are exposed (API 17+), but any loaded page can call them.
 *   ⚠️ Never expose sensitive methods. Validate the calling URL.
 *
 * Common attack vectors:
 *   - XSS via injected scripts in loaded content
 *   - URL scheme hijacking (intent://, javascript://)
 *   - File access (file:// can read local files if enabled)
 *   - Mixed content (HTTPS page loading HTTP resources)
 *   - Deep link manipulation
 *
 * Cookie isolation:
 *   WebView cookies are shared across all WebViews in the app.
 *   Use CookieManager to clear cookies after sensitive sessions.
 *   Third-party cookies are blocked by default on API 33+.
 *
 * Safe Browsing (API 26+):
 *   Google Safe Browsing checks URLs against known malicious sites.
 *   Enabled by default. Shows an interstitial warning for dangerous sites.
 *   Set android:enableSafeBrowsing="true" in manifest (opt-in for older APIs).
 */

class SecureWebViewActivity : AppCompatActivity() {

    private lateinit var webView: WebView

    fun setupSecureWebView() {
        webView = WebView(this)

        webView.settings.apply {
            // Only enable JavaScript if absolutely necessary
            javaScriptEnabled = true

            // ❌ Disable file access — prevents file:// attacks
            allowFileAccess = false
            allowFileAccessFromFileURLs = false
            allowUniversalAccessFromFileURLs = false

            // ❌ Disable content provider access via WebView
            allowContentAccess = false

            // Block mixed content (HTTPS page loading HTTP resources)
            mixedContentMode = WebSettings.MIXED_CONTENT_NEVER_ALLOW

            // Disable geolocation
            setGeolocationEnabled(false)
        }

        // Validate URLs before loading
        webView.webViewClient = object : WebViewClient() {
            private val allowedHosts = setOf("example.com", "www.example.com")

            override fun shouldOverrideUrlLoading(
                view: WebView,
                request: WebResourceRequest
            ): Boolean {
                val url = request.url
                // Block non-HTTPS and untrusted hosts
                if (url.scheme != "https" || url.host !in allowedHosts) {
                    return true  // Block navigation
                }
                return false  // Allow navigation
            }
        }

        // Safe JavaScript interface — minimal exposure
        webView.addJavascriptInterface(SafeBridge(), "AndroidBridge")
    }
}

class SafeBridge {
    @JavascriptInterface
    fun getAppVersion(): String {
        // ✅ Safe: returns non-sensitive info
        return BuildConfig.VERSION_NAME
    }

    // ❌ Never expose methods like: getAuthToken(), executeCommand(), readFile()
}


// ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
// PRACTICE QUESTIONS
// ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

/**
 * ❓ Q1: How do you securely store an auth token on Android?
 *    A: Use EncryptedSharedPreferences backed by Android Keystore.
 *       Keys are stored in hardware-backed security (TEE/Strongbox).
 *       Token is AES-256 encrypted at rest. Never store in plain SharedPreferences
 *       or in code/BuildConfig.
 *
 * ❓ Q2: What's the difference between certificate pinning in XML vs OkHttp?
 *    A: XML (network_security_config): declarative, handles backup pins,
 *       expiration dates, debug overrides. Recommended by Google.
 *       OkHttp CertificatePinner: programmatic, more control, but must
 *       handle updates manually. Both prevent MITM attacks.
 *
 * ❓ Q3: How do you handle the "Don't ask again" permission scenario?
 *    A: shouldShowRequestPermissionRationale returns false in two cases:
 *       1. First time asking (never denied) — just request
 *       2. User selected "Don't ask again" — show dialog directing to Settings
 *       Distinguish by tracking if you've asked before (DataStore flag).
 *
 * ❓ Q4: What is FLAG_SECURE and when would you use it?
 *    A: window.setFlags(FLAG_SECURE, FLAG_SECURE) prevents:
 *       - Screenshots of the Activity
 *       - Screen recording of the Activity
 *       - Preview in recent apps (shows blank)
 *       Use for: banking, password, sensitive data screens.
 *
 * ❓ Q5: How does Android Keystore differ from just encrypting with a password?
 *    A: Keystore keys never leave the secure hardware (TEE).
 *       Even root access can't extract the key material.
 *       Password-based encryption: key is in app memory — extractable.
 *       Keystore also supports biometric-gated access.
 *
 * ❓ Q6: How does Play Integrity differ from certificate pinning?
 *    A: Pinning protects transport (MITM). Play Integrity verifies app binary
 *       and device environment at runtime. Use both for layered security.
 *
 * ❓ Q7: What's your pin rotation strategy?
 *    A: Ship current + backup SPKI hash, set XML expiration, coordinate with infra,
 *       remote-config kill switch for emergencies. Never pin only one hash.
 */

/**
 * ============================================================================
 * 13 — MODERN ANDROID DEVELOPMENT (2025-2026)
 * Google SDE-2 Interview Prep
 * ============================================================================

 *   1. Kotlin Multiplatform (KMP)

 *   4. Baseline Profiles (summary → 08_performance_optimization.kt)
 *   5. Android 15/16 Changes
 *   6. Predictive Back Gesture
 *   7. Accessibility
 *   8. App Links & Deep Links
 *   9. Dynamic Feature Modules
 *  10. Privacy Sandbox (Overview)
 *  11. Large Screen & Adaptive Layouts
 *  12. Credential Manager API
 *  13. Photo Picker
 *  14. CameraX & Media3
 *
 * ============================================================================
 */

// ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
// 1. KOTLIN MULTIPLATFORM (KMP)
// ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

/**
 * KMP = share Kotlin code between Android, iOS, Desktop, Web.
 *
 * What to share:
 *   ✅ Business logic (UseCases, domain models)
 *   ✅ Networking (Ktor client)
 *   ✅ Data persistence (SQLDelight)
 *   ✅ Serialization (kotlinx.serialization)
 *   ✅ State management
 *   ❌ UI (platform-specific; though Compose Multiplatform is emerging)
 *   ❌ Platform APIs (camera, sensors, notifications)
 *
 * Project structure:
 *   shared/
 *     commonMain/       → shared Kotlin code
 *     androidMain/      → Android-specific implementations
 *     iosMain/          → iOS-specific implementations
 *   androidApp/         → Android app module
 *   iosApp/             → iOS Xcode project
 *
 * expect/actual mechanism:
 *   expect → declares platform-specific API in commonMain
 *   actual → provides implementation in each platform module
 */

// Common code (commonMain)
expect class PlatformLogger() {
    fun log(message: String)
}

// Android implementation (androidMain)
actual class PlatformLogger {
    actual fun log(message: String) {
        android.util.Log.d("KMP", message)
    }
}

// Shared UseCase
class GetArticlesUseCase(
    private val repository: ArticleRepository
) {
    suspend operator fun invoke(): Result<List<Article>> {
        return repository.getArticles()
    }
}

/**
 * 💡 Google's KMP investment:
 *   - Official support for KMP in Android Studio
 *   - Jetpack libraries being made KMP-compatible (Room, DataStore)
 *   - Google uses KMP internally for cross-platform code sharing
 *
 * ❓ Interview Question: When would you recommend KMP?
 *    A: When you have Android + iOS apps with significant shared business logic.
 *       Not worth it for: small apps, UI-heavy apps, or if team has no iOS devs.
 *       Start by sharing data/domain layers. Don't share UI initially.
 */


// ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
// 2. APP MODULARIZATION
// ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

/**
 * Modularization = splitting your app into multiple Gradle modules.
 *
 * Benefits:
 *   ✅ Build speed (only changed modules recompile)
 *   ✅ Code ownership (team per module)
 *   ✅ Strict dependency boundaries (can't accidentally access internals)
 *   ✅ Reusability (library modules shared across apps)
 *   ✅ Testability (test modules in isolation)
 *   ✅ Dynamic delivery (download features on demand)
 *
 * Module types:
 *   :app              → main application module (entry point)
 *   :core:network     → networking abstractions
 *   :core:database    → Room database
 *   :core:common      → shared utilities
 *   :core:ui          → shared composables, theme
 *   :core:model       → domain models
 *   :feature:articles → article list & detail screens
 *   :feature:profile  → user profile screens
 *   :feature:settings → settings screens
 *
 * Dependency rules:
 *   :feature modules → depend on :core modules (not other :feature modules)
 *   :core modules    → can depend on other :core modules
 *   :app module      → depends on all :feature modules
 *
 * 💡 Navigation between feature modules:
 *   Use Navigation Component with deep links or a shared navigation module.
 *   Features don't know about each other — they navigate via routes/URIs.
 */

// Module structure example
/*
project/
├── app/                          // Entry point, DI setup
│   └── build.gradle.kts
├── core/
│   ├── common/                   // Extensions, utils
│   ├── model/                    // Domain data classes
│   ├── database/                 // Room DB, DAOs, entities
│   ├── network/                  // Retrofit, API services
│   ├── data/                     // Repositories
│   └── ui/                       // Shared Compose components, theme
├── feature/
│   ├── articles/                 // Article screens + ViewModel
│   ├── profile/                  // Profile screens + ViewModel
│   └── settings/                 // Settings screens + ViewModel
└── build-logic/                  // Convention plugins
    └── convention/
*/

/**
 * ❓ Interview Question: How do you handle navigation between feature modules
 *    that don't know about each other?
 *
 *    A: Options:
 *    1. Deep link routes — each feature declares routes, navigation module maps them
 *    2. Interface-based — navigation interface in :core, implemented in :app
 *    3. Navigation Component with NavGraphs per feature + shared routes
 */

// ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
// 4. BASELINE PROFILES (SUMMARY)
// ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

/**
 * Baseline Profiles tell ART which code paths to AOT-compile at install time.
 *
 * Impact: 20-30% faster startup, smoother interactions.
 *
 * Quick summary:
 *   1. Write a Macrobenchmark that exercises critical user journeys
 *   2. BaselineProfileRule generates a profile file
 *   3. Profile is bundled with AAB/APK
 *   4. At install, ART AOT-compiles listed methods as native code
 *   5. Cloud Profiles from Play Store complement developer-provided profiles
 *
 * → See 08_performance_optimization.kt § "BASELINE PROFILES" for the full
 *   implementation: BaselineProfileGenerator, StartupBenchmark, CI integration,
 *   and Gradle setup.
 */


// ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
// 5. ANDROID 15/16 PLATFORM CHANGES
// ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

/**
 * Android 15 (API 35):
 *   - 16KB page size support (for newer hardware)
 *   - Foreground service dataSync type has 6-hour limit
 *   - Package visibility enforcement tightened
 *   - Private Space for hiding apps
 *
 * Android 16 (API 36 — 2026):
 *   - Predictive back gesture enforcement (must support or breaks)
 *   - Photo picker improvements
 *   - Health Connect integration
 *   - Richer widget APIs
 *   - Stricter background work limits
 *
 * Key patterns:
 *   - Target the latest SDK but test on older devices
 *   - Use AndroidX compat libraries for backward compatibility
 *   - Handle new permission requirements gracefully
 */


// ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
// 6. PREDICTIVE BACK GESTURE
// ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

/**
 * Predictive Back = system-level back gesture with preview animation.
 * User swipes from edge and sees a preview of where they'll go.
 *
 * Requirements (API 34+ enforced on API 36):
 *   1. Set android:enableOnBackInvokedCallback="true" in manifest
 *   2. Migrate from onBackPressed() to OnBackPressedCallback
 *   3. Support system back animations (cross-activity, cross-task)
 */

// Migrating back handling
class ModernActivity : AppCompatActivity() {

    override fun onCreate(savedInstanceState: Bundle?) {
        super.onCreate(savedInstanceState)

        // ✅ Modern back handling with OnBackPressedCallback
        val callback = object : OnBackPressedCallback(true) {
            override fun handleOnBackPressed() {
                if (hasUnsavedChanges()) {
                    showDiscardDialog()
                } else {
                    // Let system handle the back (default animation)
                    isEnabled = false
                    onBackPressedDispatcher.onBackPressed()
                }
            }
        }
        onBackPressedDispatcher.addCallback(this, callback)
    }

    // ❌ Deprecated — breaks predictive back
    // override fun onBackPressed() { super.onBackPressed() }
}


// ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
// 7. ACCESSIBILITY
// ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

/**
 * Google values accessibility. Mentioning it in interviews shows maturity.
 *
 * Key practices:
 *   - contentDescription on all interactive elements
 *   - Sufficient color contrast (4.5:1 for text)
 *   - Touch targets >= 48dp
 *   - Semantic headings for screen readers
 *   - Focus order matches visual order
 *   - Support TalkBack navigation
 */

// Compose accessibility
@Composable
fun AccessibleButton(
    text: String,
    onClick: () -> Unit,
    isBookmarked: Boolean
) {
    IconButton(
        onClick = onClick,
        modifier = Modifier
            .size(48.dp)  // Minimum touch target
            .semantics {
                contentDescription = if (isBookmarked) {
                    "Remove $text from bookmarks"
                } else {
                    "Add $text to bookmarks"
                }
                role = Role.Button
                stateDescription = if (isBookmarked) "Bookmarked" else "Not bookmarked"
            }
    ) {
        Icon(
            imageVector = if (isBookmarked) Icons.Filled.Bookmark else Icons.Outlined.BookmarkBorder,
            contentDescription = null  // Described by parent semantics
        )
    }
}


// ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
// 8. APP LINKS & DEEP LINKS
// ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

/**
 * Three types of links that open your app:
 *
 * Deep Links:
 *   - Custom scheme URIs: myapp://article/123
 *   - Matched via intent-filter
 *   - ⚠️ Any app can register the same scheme — not verified
 *
 * Web Links:
 *   - HTTP/HTTPS URIs: https://example.com/article/123
 *   - Shows disambiguation dialog if multiple apps handle it
 *
 * App Links (verified, recommended):
 *   - HTTPS only: https://example.com/article/123
 *   - Verified via Digital Asset Links (assetlinks.json on your domain)
 *   - No disambiguation dialog — opens directly in your app
 *   - Falls back to browser if app not installed
 *
 * Digital Asset Links:
 *   Host a JSON file at: https://example.com/.well-known/assetlinks.json
 *   Contains your app's package name + signing certificate fingerprint.
 *   Android verifies this at install time.
 */

// Intent filter for App Links (AndroidManifest.xml)
/*
<activity android:name=".DeepLinkActivity"
    android:exported="true">

    <!-- Deep link (custom scheme) -->
    <intent-filter>
        <action android:name="android.intent.action.VIEW" />
        <category android:name="android.intent.category.DEFAULT" />
        <category android:name="android.intent.category.BROWSABLE" />
        <data android:scheme="myapp" android:host="article" />
    </intent-filter>

    <!-- Verified App Link (HTTPS + autoVerify) -->
    <intent-filter android:autoVerify="true">
        <action android:name="android.intent.action.VIEW" />
        <category android:name="android.intent.category.DEFAULT" />
        <category android:name="android.intent.category.BROWSABLE" />
        <data android:scheme="https"
              android:host="example.com"
              android:pathPrefix="/article" />
    </intent-filter>
</activity>
*/

// assetlinks.json (hosted at https://example.com/.well-known/assetlinks.json)
/*
[{
    "relation": ["delegate_permission/common.handle_all_urls"],
    "target": {
        "namespace": "android_app",
        "package_name": "com.example.app",
        "sha256_cert_fingerprints": [
            "AB:CD:EF:12:34:56:..."
        ]
    }
}]
*/

// Navigation Component deep links
class ArticleNavigation {

    // Type-safe deep link in Navigation Component
    fun setupNavGraph(navController: NavController) {
        navController.graph = navController.createGraph(
            startDestination = "home"
        ) {
            composable(
                route = "article/{articleId}",
                deepLinks = listOf(
                    navDeepLink {
                        uriPattern = "https://example.com/article/{articleId}"
                    }
                )
            ) { backStackEntry ->
                val articleId = backStackEntry.arguments?.getString("articleId")
                ArticleScreen(articleId = articleId)
            }
        }
    }
}

// Testing deep links
/*
# Via ADB:
adb shell am start -a android.intent.action.VIEW \
    -d "https://example.com/article/123" \
    com.example.app

# Verify app link status:
adb shell pm get-app-links com.example.app
*/


// ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
// 9. DYNAMIC FEATURE MODULES
// ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

/**
 * Play Feature Delivery: Download parts of your app on demand.
 * Requires Android App Bundle (AAB) — not APKs.
 *
 * Delivery modes:
 *   install-time   → installed with the base app (like a regular module)
 *   on-demand      → downloaded when user triggers the feature
 *   conditional    → installed only on devices matching conditions (API, country, hardware)
 *
 * Benefits:
 *   - Smaller initial download size (base APK is smaller)
 *   - Users only download features they actually use
 *   - Reduces install abandonment (large apps lose users)
 *
 * When to use:
 *   - Large features used by a small % of users (e.g., AR try-on, video editor)
 *   - Country-specific features (payment methods, compliance)
 *   - Premium/paid features
 *
 * SplitInstallManager API:
 *   - Request feature install, monitor progress, handle errors
 *   - Deferred install (download when on Wi-Fi)
 */

// On-demand feature installation
class FeatureInstaller(private val context: Context) {

    private val splitInstallManager: SplitInstallManager =
        SplitInstallManagerFactory.create(context)

    fun installFeature(
        featureName: String,
        onProgress: (Float) -> Unit,
        onSuccess: () -> Unit,
        onError: (Exception) -> Unit
    ) {
        val request = SplitInstallRequest.newBuilder()
            .addModule(featureName)
            .build()

        splitInstallManager.startInstall(request)
            .addOnSuccessListener { sessionId ->
                // Monitor installation progress
                splitInstallManager.registerListener { state ->
                    when (state.status()) {
                        SplitInstallSessionStatus.DOWNLOADING -> {
                            val progress = state.bytesDownloaded().toFloat() /
                                state.totalBytesToDownload()
                            onProgress(progress)
