                        }
                        SplitInstallSessionStatus.INSTALLED -> {
                            onSuccess()
                        }
                        SplitInstallSessionStatus.REQUIRES_USER_CONFIRMATION -> {
                            // Large download — system asks for user confirmation
                            splitInstallManager.startConfirmationDialogForResult(
                                state, context as Activity, REQUEST_CODE
                            )
                        }
                        SplitInstallSessionStatus.FAILED -> {
                            onError(Exception("Install failed: ${state.errorCode()}"))
                        }
                    }
                }
            }
            .addOnFailureListener { exception ->
                onError(exception)
            }
    }

    fun isFeatureInstalled(featureName: String): Boolean {
        return splitInstallManager.installedModules.contains(featureName)
    }

    companion object {
        private const val REQUEST_CODE = 1001
    }
}


// ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
// 10. PRIVACY SANDBOX (OVERVIEW)
// ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

/**
 * Android Privacy Sandbox = Google's initiative to replace cross-app tracking
 * with privacy-preserving alternatives. Phasing out Advertising ID.
 *
 * Key APIs:
 *
 * 1. Topics API (interest-based ads without cross-app tracking):
 *    - Device locally classifies apps into interest "topics" (e.g., Sports, Travel)
 *    - Returns 3 topics per epoch (1 week) — coarse-grained, not per-app
 *    - Ad SDKs receive topics to show relevant ads without knowing which apps are installed
 *    - User can view and remove topics in Settings
 *
 * 2. Attribution Reporting API:
 *    - Measures ad conversions (ad click → app install/purchase)
 *    - Reports are delayed and aggregated — no real-time per-user tracking
 *    - Replaces direct referrer tracking
 *
 * 3. SDK Runtime (API 34+):
 *    - Third-party ad SDKs run in an isolated sandbox process
 *    - Cannot access the app's storage, permissions, or identifiers
 *    - Reduces data leakage from ad SDKs
 *
 * Why this matters for Google interviews:
 *   - Shows awareness of Google's current strategic direction
 *   - Privacy-first thinking is valued at Google
 *   - Understanding the trade-offs between privacy and ad monetization
 *   - May be asked: "How would you handle ad attribution without GAID?"
 *     Answer: Use Attribution Reporting API with aggregated reports.
 */


// ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
// 11. LARGE SCREEN & ADAPTIVE LAYOUTS
// ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

/**
 * Google is heavily pushing support for foldables, tablets, and Chromebooks.
 * Apps that don't adapt lose ranking and users on these growing form factors.
 *
 * Window Size Classes (Jetpack):
 *   Compact  → phone portrait (< 600dp width)
 *   Medium   → tablet portrait, foldable unfolded, large phone landscape (600-840dp)
 *   Expanded → tablet landscape, desktop (> 840dp)
 *
 * Use WindowSizeClass to choose layout strategy, NOT screen orientation.
 *
 * Canonical Layouts (Google's recommended patterns):
 *   List-Detail       → list on left, detail on right (email, messaging)
 *   Feed              → single-column on compact, multi-column on expanded
 *   Supporting Panel  → main content + side panel (maps, editors)
 *
 * Key APIs:
 *   - WindowSizeClass (Material 3): calculateWindowSizeClass(activity)
 *   - adaptive-navigation (Jetpack): NavigationSuiteScaffold auto-switches
 *     between bottom nav (compact), nav rail (medium), and nav drawer (expanded)
 *   - WindowInfoTracker: observe fold state and hinge position for foldables
 */

@Composable
fun AdaptiveArticleScreen(windowSizeClass: WindowSizeClass) {
    when (windowSizeClass.widthSizeClass) {
        WindowWidthSizeClass.Compact -> {
            // Phone: single-pane, navigate to detail screen
            ArticleListScreen()
        }
        WindowWidthSizeClass.Medium, WindowWidthSizeClass.Expanded -> {
            // Tablet/Foldable: list-detail side by side
            Row {
                ArticleListScreen(modifier = Modifier.weight(0.4f))
                ArticleDetailScreen(modifier = Modifier.weight(0.6f))
            }
        }
    }
}

// NavigationSuiteScaffold — auto-adapts nav component to window size
@Composable
fun AdaptiveNavigation(windowSizeClass: WindowSizeClass) {
    NavigationSuiteScaffold(
        navigationSuiteItems = {
            item(
                icon = { Icon(Icons.Default.Home, contentDescription = "Home") },
                label = { Text("Home") },
                selected = true,
                onClick = { /* navigate */ }
            )
            item(
                icon = { Icon(Icons.Default.Search, contentDescription = "Search") },
                label = { Text("Search") },
                selected = false,
                onClick = { /* navigate */ }
            )
        }
    ) {
        // Screen content
        // On Compact: renders BottomNavigation
        // On Medium: renders NavigationRail
        // On Expanded: renders NavigationDrawer
    }
}

/**
 * ❓ Interview Question: How do you make your app work well on foldables?
 *    A: Use WindowSizeClass to choose layout (not orientation).
 *       Observe fold state via WindowInfoTracker to avoid placing content on the hinge.
 *       Support drag-and-drop for multi-window.
 *       Test with the foldable emulator profiles in Android Studio.
 *
 * 💡 Interview Tip: Google evaluates apps on large screens for Play Store featuring.
 *    Mentioning adaptive layouts shows awareness of Google's current priorities.
 */


// ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
// 12. CREDENTIAL MANAGER API
// ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

/**
 * Credential Manager = unified Jetpack API for sign-in (replaces deprecated
 * SmartLock, One Tap Sign-In, and legacy FIDO2 APIs).
 *
 * Supports:
 *   - Passkeys (FIDO2 WebAuthn — passwordless, phishing-resistant)
 *   - Passwords (saved in Google Password Manager)
 *   - Federated identity (Sign in with Google)
 *
 * Why passkeys matter:
 *   - Google is actively pushing passkey adoption
 *   - No password to phish, leak, or reuse
 *   - Backed by device biometrics (fingerprint/face)
 *   - Cross-device via Google Account sync
 *
 * Key classes:
 *   CredentialManager           → entry point, created via CredentialManager.create(context)
 *   GetCredentialRequest        → request to retrieve an existing credential
 *   CreatePublicKeyCredentialRequest → request to create a new passkey
 *   GetPasswordOption           → option to retrieve a saved password
 *   GetPublicKeyCredentialOption → option to retrieve a passkey
 */

suspend fun signIn(context: Context): String? {
    val credentialManager = CredentialManager.create(context)

    val request = GetCredentialRequest(listOf(
        GetPasswordOption(),                    // Saved passwords
        GetPublicKeyCredentialOption(           // Passkeys
            requestJson = generatePasskeyRequestJson()
        )
    ))

    return try {
        val result = credentialManager.getCredential(context, request)
        when (val credential = result.credential) {
            is PasswordCredential -> {
                // Authenticate with username/password
                authenticate(credential.id, credential.password)
            }
            is PublicKeyCredential -> {
                // Verify passkey assertion with your server
                verifyPasskeyAssertion(credential.authenticationResponseJson)
            }
            else -> null
        }
    } catch (e: GetCredentialCancellationException) {
        null  // User cancelled
    } catch (e: NoCredentialException) {
        null  // No saved credentials — show manual sign-in
    }
}

/**
 * 💡 Interview Tip: "For new apps, I implement Credential Manager with passkey
 *    support as the primary sign-in method, with password as fallback. Passkeys
 *    are Google's strategic direction and eliminate entire categories of attacks."
 */


// ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
// 13. PHOTO PICKER
// ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

/**
 * Photo Picker (API 33+, backported to API 19 via Google Play services):
 *   System-provided UI for selecting photos/videos.
 *   NO runtime permissions required — the system grants access only to
 *   the specific items the user selects.
 *
 * Why use it:
 *   ✅ No READ_MEDIA_IMAGES permission needed
 *   ✅ Privacy-preserving — app only sees selected items
 *   ✅ Consistent UI across devices
 *   ✅ Supports single/multi selection and media type filtering
 *
 * When NOT to use it:
 *   ❌ If you need to browse all media (gallery app, file manager)
 *   ❌ If you need access to file metadata beyond the content URI
 */

// Single photo selection
class PhotoPickerActivity : AppCompatActivity() {

    private val pickMedia = registerForActivityResult(
        ActivityResultContracts.PickVisualMedia()
    ) { uri ->
        if (uri != null) {
            // User selected a photo — use the URI
            loadImage(uri)
        }
    }

    private val pickMultipleMedia = registerForActivityResult(
        ActivityResultContracts.PickMultipleVisualMedia(maxItems = 5)
    ) { uris ->
        if (uris.isNotEmpty()) {
            processSelectedMedia(uris)
        }
    }

    fun openPhotoPicker() {
        // Pick images only (no video)
        pickMedia.launch(PickVisualMediaRequest(
            ActivityResultContracts.PickVisualMedia.ImageOnly
        ))
    }

    fun openMultiPicker() {
        // Pick images and video
        pickMultipleMedia.launch(PickVisualMediaRequest(
            ActivityResultContracts.PickVisualMedia.ImageAndVideo
        ))
    }
}

/**
 * ❓ Interview Question: How do you handle image selection in a modern Android app?
 *    A: Use the system Photo Picker — no permissions, privacy-preserving, consistent UX.
 *       For custom gallery UI, use READ_MEDIA_IMAGES (API 33+) or READ_MEDIA_VISUAL_USER_SELECTED
 *       (API 34+) for partial access. For document files, use Storage Access Framework (SAF).
 */


// ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
// 14. CAMERAX & MEDIA3
// ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

/**
 * CameraX (Jetpack):
 *   Lifecycle-aware camera API that abstracts device-specific quirks.
 *
 *   Use cases:
 *     Preview      → show camera viewfinder
 *     ImageCapture → take photos
 *     VideoCapture → record video
 *     ImageAnalysis → process frames (barcode scanning, ML)
 *
 *   Key benefits over Camera2:
 *     ✅ Lifecycle-aware — binds to LifecycleOwner, auto start/stop
 *     ✅ Device-specific fixes built in (CameraX Extensions for HDR, Night, etc.)
 *     ✅ Much less boilerplate than Camera2
 *     ✅ Concurrent use cases (preview + analysis + capture)
 *
 *   Architecture:
 *     ProcessCameraProvider → bind use cases to a lifecycle
 *     CameraSelector → choose front/back camera
 *     Preview.SurfaceProvider → surface for viewfinder
 */

fun bindCamera(
    lifecycleOwner: LifecycleOwner,
    previewView: PreviewView
) {
    val cameraProviderFuture = ProcessCameraProvider.getInstance(previewView.context)
    cameraProviderFuture.addListener({
        val cameraProvider = cameraProviderFuture.get()

        val preview = Preview.Builder().build().apply {
            setSurfaceProvider(previewView.surfaceProvider)
        }

        val imageCapture = ImageCapture.Builder()
            .setCaptureMode(ImageCapture.CAPTURE_MODE_MINIMIZE_LATENCY)
            .build()

        val imageAnalysis = ImageAnalysis.Builder()
            .setBackpressureStrategy(ImageAnalysis.STRATEGY_KEEP_ONLY_LATEST)
            .build()
            .apply {
                setAnalyzer(Executors.newSingleThreadExecutor()) { imageProxy ->
                    // Process frame (e.g., ML Kit barcode scanning)
                    imageProxy.close()
                }
            }

        val cameraSelector = CameraSelector.DEFAULT_BACK_CAMERA

        cameraProvider.unbindAll()
        cameraProvider.bindToLifecycle(
            lifecycleOwner, cameraSelector, preview, imageCapture, imageAnalysis
        )
    }, ContextCompat.getMainExecutor(previewView.context))
}

/**
 * Media3 (Jetpack, replaces ExoPlayer standalone):
 *   Unified media playback library.
 *
 *   Key components:
 *     ExoPlayer          → the player engine (now under Media3 namespace)
 *     MediaSession       → publish playback state to system (lock screen, Bluetooth)
 *     MediaController    → client-side control of a MediaSession
 *     PlayerView         → ready-made UI for video playback (View system)
 *
 *   When to mention in interviews:
 *     - Chat app system design → video message playback
 *     - News feed design → auto-play video in feed
 *     - Music player → background playback with MediaSession
 *
 *   Key features:
 *     ✅ Adaptive streaming (HLS, DASH)
 *     ✅ Caching (CacheDataSource for offline playback)
 *     ✅ DRM (Widevine, PlayReady)
 *     ✅ Compositor for transitions and effects
 *
 * 💡 Interview Tip: "For video playback, I use Media3 ExoPlayer with lifecycle-aware
 *    binding. For feed auto-play, I observe item visibility in LazyColumn and
 *    play/pause the player based on the >50% visibility threshold."
 */


// ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
// PRACTICE QUESTIONS
// ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

/**
 * ❓ Q1: How do you decide what to put in each module?
 *    A: Follow the dependency rule: features depend on core, not each other.
 *       Core modules provide shared infrastructure (network, DB, models).
 *       Feature modules contain screens + ViewModels for one feature.
 *       Use the "could another team own this?" test.
 *
 * ❓ Q2: How do Baseline Profiles interact with Cloud Profiles?
 *    A: Developer Baseline Profiles are bundled with the app.
 *       Cloud Profiles are crowd-sourced from user devices via Play.
 *       Both are merged by ART for optimal compilation.
 *       Baseline Profiles help first-time users; Cloud Profiles help over time.
 *
 * ❓ Q3: When would you use KMP vs Flutter vs React Native?
 *    A: KMP: native UI per platform, share business logic, gradual adoption.
 *       Flutter: single codebase for UI + logic, fast prototyping, custom UI.
 *       React Native: JS ecosystem, web team doing mobile.
 *       Google recommends KMP for Android+iOS with native quality.
 *
 * ❓ Q4: How do you handle the predictive back gesture for custom back handling?
 *    A: Use OnBackPressedCallback with isEnabled flag. Enable when there's
 *       custom back behavior (unsaved changes, bottom sheet). Disable to let
 *       system handle default animation. Never override onBackPressed().
 *
 * ❓ Q5: What are the key Gradle performance optimizations?
 *    A: Enable configuration cache, build cache, parallel builds.
 *       Use version catalogs (centralized deps). Convention plugins (DRY config).
 *       Avoid unnecessary plugins. Use buildSrc sparingly (invalidates all caches).
 *
 * ❓ Q6: How do you support foldables and tablets in a Compose app?
 *    A: Use WindowSizeClass from Material 3 to pick layout strategy:
 *       Compact → single pane, Medium → nav rail + content, Expanded → list-detail.
 *       Use NavigationSuiteScaffold to auto-switch between bottom nav, rail, and drawer.
 *       Observe fold state via WindowInfoTracker to avoid placing content on the hinge.
 *       Test with foldable emulator profiles in Android Studio.
 *
 * ❓ Q7: What is the Credential Manager API and why does it matter?
 *    A: Unified Jetpack API for sign-in that replaces SmartLock, One Tap, and FIDO2.
 *       Supports passkeys (phishing-resistant, biometric-backed), passwords, and
 *       federated identity (Sign in with Google). Passkeys are Google's strategic
 *       direction for authentication — no password to leak or phish.
 *
 * ❓ Q8: When should you use the system Photo Picker vs requesting media permissions?
 *    A: Photo Picker for most cases — no permissions needed, privacy-preserving.
 *       Request READ_MEDIA_IMAGES only if you need to browse all media (gallery app).
 *       On API 34+, READ_MEDIA_VISUAL_USER_SELECTED enables partial access where
 *       the user selects specific files each session.
 */

14 Design Patterns Kotlin

14_design_patterns_kotlin.kt · Kotlin reference

/**
 * ============================================================================
 * 14 — DESIGN PATTERNS & KOTLIN ADVANCED
 * Google SDE-2 Interview Prep
 * ============================================================================
 *
 * Topics Covered:
 *   1. Kotlin Sealed Classes & Interfaces
 *   2. Kotlin Delegates (by lazy, observable, custom)
 *   3. Extension Functions & Properties
 *   4. Generics & Variance (in, out, star projection)
 *   5. Kotlin DSL
 *   6. Inline Functions, Reified Types, Crossinline, Noinline
 *   7. Kotlin Scope Functions (let, run, with, apply, also)
 *   8. Design Patterns in Android (GoF + Kotlin idioms)
 *   9. Value Classes (inline classes)
 *  10. Kotlin Contracts
 *
 * ============================================================================
 */

// ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
// 1. SEALED CLASSES & INTERFACES
// ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

/**
 * Sealed = restricted hierarchy. All subclasses known at compile time.
 * Compiler can verify exhaustive when expressions.
 *
 * sealed class → can have state (constructor parameters)
 * sealed interface → multiple inheritance, no state in the sealed itself
 */

// Sealed interface for UI state — compiler enforces handling all cases
sealed interface UiState<out T> {
    data object Loading : UiState<Nothing>
    data class Success<T>(val data: T) : UiState<T>
    data class Error(val message: String, val cause: Throwable? = null) : UiState<Nothing>
    data object Empty : UiState<Nothing>
}

// Usage — exhaustive when (no else needed)
fun <T> renderState(state: UiState<T>) {
    when (state) {
        is UiState.Loading -> showLoadingSpinner()
        is UiState.Success -> showData(state.data)
        is UiState.Error -> showError(state.message)
        is UiState.Empty -> showEmptyState()
        // Compiler error if any case is missing
    }
}

// Sealed class for network result with data
sealed class NetworkResult<out T> {
    data class Success<T>(val data: T) : NetworkResult<T>()
    data class HttpError(val code: Int, val body: String) : NetworkResult<Nothing>()
    data class NetworkError(val exception: IOException) : NetworkResult<Nothing>()
    data class UnknownError(val exception: Throwable) : NetworkResult<Nothing>()

    val isSuccess get() = this is Success

    fun getOrNull(): T? = (this as? Success)?.data

    fun getOrThrow(): T = when (this) {
        is Success -> data
        is HttpError -> throw HttpException(code, body)
        is NetworkError -> throw exception
        is UnknownError -> throw exception
    }

    fun <R> map(transform: (T) -> R): NetworkResult<R> = when (this) {
        is Success -> Success(transform(data))
        is HttpError -> this
        is NetworkError -> this
        is UnknownError -> this
    }
}


// ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
// 2. KOTLIN DELEGATES
// ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

/**
 * Kotlin delegates allow reusing property behavior.
 *
 * Built-in delegates:
 *   by lazy {}       → initialized on first access, thread-safe by default
 *   by Delegates.observable() → notified on every change
 *   by Delegates.vetoable()   → can reject changes
 *   by map            → reads from a Map
 *
 * Custom delegates: implement ReadOnlyProperty or ReadWriteProperty
 */

// by lazy — thread-safe lazy initialization
class ExpensiveClass {
    val database: AppDatabase by lazy {
        Room.databaseBuilder(context, AppDatabase::class.java, "app.db").build()
    }
    // Created only when first accessed. Thread-safe (LazyThreadSafetyMode.SYNCHRONIZED)
}

// by Delegates.observable — react to changes
class UserPreferences {
    var theme: String by Delegates.observable("light") { _, old, new ->
        if (old != new) {
            applyTheme(new)
            analytics.logThemeChange(old, new)
        }
    }
}

// Custom delegate — SharedPreferences delegate
class PreferenceDelegate<T>(
    private val prefs: SharedPreferences,
    private val key: String,
    private val defaultValue: T
) : ReadWriteProperty<Any?, T> {

    @Suppress("UNCHECKED_CAST")
    override fun getValue(thisRef: Any?, property: KProperty<*>): T {
        return when (defaultValue) {
            is Boolean -> prefs.getBoolean(key, defaultValue) as T
            is String -> prefs.getString(key, defaultValue) as T
            is Int -> prefs.getInt(key, defaultValue) as T
            is Long -> prefs.getLong(key, defaultValue) as T
            is Float -> prefs.getFloat(key, defaultValue) as T
            else -> throw IllegalArgumentException("Unsupported type")
        }
    }

    override fun setValue(thisRef: Any?, property: KProperty<*>, value: T) {
        prefs.edit().apply {
            when (value) {
                is Boolean -> putBoolean(key, value)
                is String -> putString(key, value)
                is Int -> putInt(key, value)
                is Long -> putLong(key, value)
                is Float -> putFloat(key, value)
                else -> throw IllegalArgumentException("Unsupported type")
            }
            apply()
        }
    }
}

// Usage
class Settings(prefs: SharedPreferences) {
    var darkMode: Boolean by PreferenceDelegate(prefs, "dark_mode", false)
    var username: String by PreferenceDelegate(prefs, "username", "")
    var fontSize: Int by PreferenceDelegate(prefs, "font_size", 14)
}


// ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
// 3. EXTENSION FUNCTIONS & PROPERTIES
// ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

/**
 * Extensions add functions/properties to existing classes without inheritance.
 * Resolved STATICALLY (at compile time), not dynamically.
 *
 * ⚠️ If the class has a member function with the same signature,
 *    the member function wins (extensions don't override members).
 */

// Context extensions — commonly used in Android
fun Context.showToast(message: String, duration: Int = Toast.LENGTH_SHORT) {
    Toast.makeText(this, message, duration).show()
}

fun Context.dpToPx(dp: Float): Int {
    return (dp * resources.displayMetrics.density).roundToInt()
}

val Context.screenWidth: Int
    get() = resources.displayMetrics.widthPixels

// Flow extensions
fun <T> Flow<T>.throttleFirst(windowDuration: Long): Flow<T> = flow {
    var lastEmissionTime = 0L
    collect { value ->
        val currentTime = System.currentTimeMillis()
        if (currentTime - lastEmissionTime >= windowDuration) {
            lastEmissionTime = currentTime
            emit(value)
        }
    }
}

// Result extensions for cleaner error handling
inline fun <T> Result<T>.onEach(
    onSuccess: (T) -> Unit,
    onFailure: (Throwable) -> Unit
): Result<T> {
    this.onSuccess(onSuccess)
    this.onFailure(onFailure)
    return this
}


// ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
// 4. GENERICS & VARIANCE
// ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

/**
 * Variance defines subtyping relationship for generic types.
 *
 * Invariant (default):
 *   MutableList<Dog> is NOT a subtype of MutableList<Animal>
 *   → Can read AND write
 *
 * Covariant (out):
 *   List<Dog> IS a subtype of List<Animal>
 *   → Can only READ (produce), not write
 *   → Use for return types, immutable collections
 *
 * Contravariant (in):
 *   Comparator<Animal> IS a subtype of Comparator<Dog>
 *   → Can only WRITE (consume), not read
 *   → Use for parameter types, consumers
 *
 * Star projection (*):
 *   List<*> → unknown type, can read as Any?, can't write
 */

// Covariant — produces T
interface Source<out T> {
    fun next(): T  // ✅ Can return T
    // fun add(item: T)  // ❌ Compile error — can't consume T
}

// Contravariant — consumes T
interface Sink<in T> {
    fun put(item: T)  // ✅ Can accept T
    // fun get(): T    // ❌ Compile error — can't produce T
}

// Real-world example
class Repository<out T>(private val items: List<T>) {
    fun getAll(): List<T> = items
    fun getFirst(): T = items.first()
}

// Can assign Repository<Dog> to Repository<Animal>
// val animalRepo: Repository<Animal> = Repository<Dog>(listOf(Dog()))  // ✅

// Generic function with upper bound
fun <T : Comparable<T>> findMax(list: List<T>): T {
    return list.max()
}

// Reified type parameters (requires inline)
inline fun <reified T> isInstanceOf(value: Any): Boolean {
    return value is T  // Only works with reified
}

inline fun <reified T : Activity> Context.startActivity() {
    startActivity(Intent(this, T::class.java))
}
// Usage: context.startActivity<DetailActivity>()


// ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
// 5. KOTLIN DSL
// ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

/**
 * DSL = Domain-Specific Language built with Kotlin's lambda-with-receiver.
 * Used extensively: Gradle build scripts, Compose, Ktor, testing.
 */

// Building a simple DSL for network request configuration
class RequestBuilder {
    var url: String = ""
    var method: String = "GET"
    private val headers = mutableMapOf<String, String>()
    private var body: String? = null

    fun headers(block: HeaderBuilder.() -> Unit) {
        HeaderBuilder(headers).apply(block)
    }

    fun body(content: String) { body = content }

    fun build(): Request = Request(url, method, headers, body)
}

class HeaderBuilder(private val headers: MutableMap<String, String>) {
    infix fun String.to(value: String) { headers[this] = value }
}

fun request(block: RequestBuilder.() -> Unit): Request {
    return RequestBuilder().apply(block).build()
}

// Usage — reads like English
val req = request {
    url = "https://api.example.com/articles"
    method = "POST"
    headers {
        "Authorization" to "Bearer token123"
        "Content-Type" to "application/json"
    }
    body("""{"title": "New Article"}""")
}


// ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
// 6. INLINE FUNCTIONS
// ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

/**
 * inline → function body is inserted at the call site (no function object created)
 *
 * Benefits:
 *   - No lambda allocation overhead
 *   - Can use reified type parameters
 *   - Non-local returns allowed
 *
 * noinline → prevents specific lambda parameter from being inlined
 * crossinline → prevents non-local returns in lambda used in another context
 */

// inline with reified — avoids reflection
inline fun <reified T> fromJson(json: String): T {
    return Json.decodeFromString<T>(json)  // T is known at compile time
}

// crossinline — lambda used in another execution context
inline fun executeOnBackground(crossinline block: () -> Unit) {
    Thread {
        block()  // crossinline prevents `return` here from exiting outer function
    }.start()
}

// noinline — when you need to store the lambda
inline fun logAndExecute(
    tag: String,
    noinline callback: () -> Unit  // Stored, so can't be inlined
) {
    Log.d(tag, "Executing callback")
    handler.post(callback)  // Stored for later execution
}


// ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
// 7. SCOPE FUNCTIONS
// ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

/**
 * ┌──────────┬───────────┬────────────┬──────────────────────────┐
 * │ Function │ Object    │ Return     │ Use Case                 │
 * │          │ Reference │ Value      │                          │
 * ├──────────┼───────────┼────────────┼──────────────────────────┤
 * │ let      │ it        │ Lambda     │ Null checks, transforms  │
 * │ run      │ this      │ Lambda     │ Object config + compute  │
 * │ with     │ this      │ Lambda     │ Grouping calls on object │
 * │ apply    │ this      │ Object     │ Object configuration     │
 * │ also     │ it        │ Object     │ Side effects (logging)   │
 * └──────────┴───────────┴────────────┴──────────────────────────┘
 */

fun scopeFunctionExamples() {
    // let — null-safe operations, transformations
    val name: String? = user?.name?.let { name ->
        "Hello, $name"
    }

    // apply — configure objects (returns the object)
    val intent = Intent(context, DetailActivity::class.java).apply {
        putExtra("ID", articleId)
        flags = Intent.FLAG_ACTIVITY_SINGLE_TOP
    }

    // also — side effects without changing the chain
    val articles = repository.getArticles()
        .also { Log.d("TAG", "Fetched ${it.size} articles") }
        .filter { it.isPublished }

    // run — execute block and return result
    val result = service.run {
        authenticate(username, password)
        fetchData()
    }

    // with — multiple operations on same object
    with(binding) {
        titleText.text = article.title
        bodyText.text = article.content
        authorText.text = article.author
    }
}


// ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
// 8. DESIGN PATTERNS IN ANDROID
// ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

/**
 * Observer Pattern:
 *   LiveData, StateFlow, Flow — observe data changes
 *   Compose's state system — UI recomposes on state change
 *
 * Factory Pattern:
 *   ViewModelProvider.Factory — creates ViewModels with dependencies
 *   Retrofit.create() — creates API service implementations
 *
 * Builder Pattern:
 *   OkHttpClient.Builder, Notification.Builder, Room.databaseBuilder
 *   Kotlin: use default parameters + apply() instead of explicit builders
 *
 * Adapter Pattern:
 *   RecyclerView.Adapter — adapts data model to views
 *   Retrofit Converter — adapts JSON to Kotlin objects
 *
 * Repository Pattern:
 *   Abstract data access, provide single source of truth
 *
 * Strategy Pattern:
 *   Different Dispatchers for different work types
 *   Different serializers based on content type
 *
 * Singleton Pattern:
 *   Kotlin's `object` keyword
 *   Hilt's @Singleton scope
 *   Room.databaseBuilder with .build() cached
 *
 * Decorator Pattern:
 *   OkHttp Interceptor chain — each interceptor wraps the next
 *   Compose Modifier chain — each modifier decorates the composable
 */


// ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
// 9. VALUE CLASSES (INLINE CLASSES)
// ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

/**
 * Value classes wrap a single value without runtime overhead.
 * The wrapper is erased at compile time — zero-cost abstraction.
 *
 * Use cases: type-safe IDs, units, branded types
 */

@JvmInline
value class UserId(val value: String) {
    init { require(value.isNotBlank()) { "UserId cannot be blank" } }
}

@JvmInline
value class ArticleId(val value: String)

@JvmInline
value class Pixels(val value: Int)

@JvmInline
value class Dp(val value: Float)

// Type safety: can't accidentally pass ArticleId where UserId is expected
fun getUser(id: UserId): User = TODO()
fun getArticle(id: ArticleId): Article = TODO()

// At runtime, these are just String/Int — no wrapper object allocated


// ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
// 10. KOTLIN CONTRACTS
// ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

/**
 * Contracts = compiler hints that tell the Kotlin compiler about
 * function behavior it can't otherwise infer.
 *
 * Why they exist:
 *   The compiler can't always analyze function bodies — especially for
 *   higher-order functions or custom check functions. Contracts bridge this gap.
 *
 * Two main contract types:
 *
 * 1. callsInPlace — tells compiler how many times a lambda is invoked
 *    → Enables smart initialization analysis
 *    → Used by: run, let, apply, also, with, synchronized
 *
 * 2. returns(true/false) implies (condition) — conditional effects
 *    → Enables smart casts after custom check functions
 *    → Used by: require(), check(), isNullOrEmpty(), isNullOrBlank()
 */

// callsInPlace contract — enables definite initialization
@OptIn(ExperimentalContracts::class)
inline fun <R> executeExactlyOnce(block: () -> R): R {
    contract {
        callsInPlace(block, InvocationKind.EXACTLY_ONCE)
    }
    return block()
}

// Without contract, this would be a compile error:
//   "Variable 'result' must be initialized"
fun demonstrateCallsInPlace() {
    val result: String
    executeExactlyOnce {
        result = "initialized"  // Compiler trusts this runs exactly once
    }
    println(result)  // ✅ Compiles — compiler knows result is initialized
}

// returns implies contract — enables smart casts
@OptIn(ExperimentalContracts::class)
fun requireNotBlank(value: String?): Boolean {
    contract {
        returns(true) implies (value != null)
    }
    return value != null && value.isNotBlank()
}

fun demonstrateReturnsImplies(name: String?) {
    if (requireNotBlank(name)) {
        // ✅ Smart cast: name is String (not String?) here
        println(name.length)
    }
}

// Custom type check with contract
@OptIn(ExperimentalContracts::class)
fun Any?.isNonNullString(): Boolean {
    contract {
        returns(true) implies (this@isNonNullString is String)
    }
    return this is String
}

fun demonstrateTypeCheck(value: Any?) {
    if (value.isNonNullString()) {
        // ✅ Smart cast: value is String here
        println(value.uppercase())
    }
}

/**
 * How stdlib uses contracts (why require/check enable smart casts):
 *
 *   public inline fun require(value: Boolean) {
 *       contract { returns() implies value }
 *       if (!value) throw IllegalArgumentException()
 *   }
 *
 *   public inline fun checkNotNull<T>(value: T?): T {
 *       contract { returns() implies (value != null) }
 *       if (value == null) throw IllegalStateException()
 *       return value
 *   }
 *
 * ⚠️ Contracts are experimental (as of Kotlin 2.x) and unchecked by the compiler —
 *   the compiler trusts them. A wrong contract can lead to unsound code.
 *
 * 💡 Interview tip: You don't need to write contracts often, but understanding
 *   them explains why require(), check(), and scope functions work so smoothly
 *   with Kotlin's type system.
 */


// ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
// 8. DESIGN PATTERNS IN ANDROID (GoF + Android-specific)
// ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

/**
 * Classic GoF patterns mapped to Android — interviewers ask "where have you seen X?"
 *
 * ┌─────────────────────┬──────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
 * │ Pattern             │ Android Example                                      │
 * ├─────────────────────┼──────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
 * │ Observer            │ StateFlow, LiveData, Compose state, BroadcastReceiver│
 * │ Singleton           │ object DatabaseModule, Application instance          │
 * │ Factory             │ ViewModelProvider.Factory, Fragment.instantiate()    │
 * │ Builder             │ OkHttpClient.Builder, Notification.Builder           │
 * │ Adapter             │ RecyclerView.Adapter, ListAdapter (DiffUtil)           │
 * │ Decorator           │ OkHttp Interceptors, Compose Modifier chain          │
 * │ Facade              │ Repository hiding Room + Retrofit + DataStore        │
 * │ Strategy            │ PaymentProcessor interface, multiple implementations │
 * │ Template Method     │ AsyncTask (deprecated), CoroutineWorker.doWork()     │
 * │ Proxy               │ Retrofit interface → dynamic proxy implementation    │
 * └─────────────────────┴──────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
 *
 * Kotlin idioms often replace GoF boilerplate:
 *   - Builder → default/named parameters
 *   - Singleton → object or @Singleton (Hilt)
 *   - Decorator → extension functions + Modifier
 *   - Strategy → higher-order functions / lambdas
 *
 * 💡 Interview tip: Don't recite all 23 GoF patterns. Know 5–6 with Android examples
 *    and explain when Kotlin features make the classic pattern unnecessary.
 */


// ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
// PRACTICE QUESTIONS
// ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

/**
 * ❓ Q1: When would you use sealed interface vs sealed class?
 *    A: sealed interface when you need multiple inheritance or don't need
 *       shared state. sealed class when subtypes share common properties.
 *       Prefer sealed interface for ADTs (algebraic data types) like UiState.
 *
 * ❓ Q2: Explain covariance with a real Android example.
 *    A: List<Button> can be assigned to List<View> because List is covariant
 *       (declared as List<out E>). You can read Views from it but can't add
 *       Buttons to a List<View> reference.
 *
 * ❓ Q3: Why is lazy initialization important on Android?
 *    A: Reduces cold start time — don't initialize expensive objects until
 *       needed. Example: by lazy for Database, Retrofit, heavy computations.
 *       Thread-safe by default (SYNCHRONIZED mode).
 *
 * ❓ Q4: What's the difference between object and companion object?
 *    A: object: singleton, exists independently. Has its own class.
 *       companion object: associated with a class, one per class.
 *       Members accessible via ClassName.member. Can implement interfaces.
 *
 * ❓ Q5: How do extension functions work under the hood?
 *    A: Compiled to static functions with the receiver as the first parameter.
 *       fun String.addExclamation() → StringKt.addExclamation(String str)
 *       Resolved statically — no virtual dispatch. If class has member with
 *       same signature, member wins.
 */

15 System Design Android

15_system_design_android.kt · Kotlin reference

/**
 * ============================================================================
 * 15 — SYSTEM DESIGN FOR ANDROID
 * Google SDE-2 Interview Prep
 * ============================================================================
 *
 * Topics Covered:
 *   1. System Design Framework (6-Step Approach)
 *   2. Design: Offline-First Notes App
 *   3. Design: Image Loading Library
 *   4. Design: Chat/Messaging App
 *   5. Design: News Feed (Instagram-like)
 *   6. Design: Analytics SDK
 *   7. Design: File Downloader Library
 *   8. Design: Observability / Logging SDK
 *   9. Common Mobile-Specific Constraints
 *
 * ============================================================================
 */

// ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
// 1. SYSTEM DESIGN FRAMEWORK — 6-STEP APPROACH
// ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

/**
 * Step 1: REQUIREMENTS FRAMING (5 min)
 *   Functional Requirements:
 *     - What can the user do? (CRUD, search, share, etc.)
 *     - What are the core screens/flows?
 *   Non-Functional Requirements:
 *     - Offline support?
 *     - Real-time updates?
 *     - Performance targets? (startup < 500ms, scroll 60fps)
 *     - Scale? (DAU, data volume)
 *     - Security requirements?
 *
 * Step 2: SCALE ESTIMATION (2 min)
 *   - Back-of-envelope calculations
 *   - DAU → concurrent users → requests/second
 *   - Data size per user → total storage → cache budget
 *   - Network bandwidth requirements
 *
 * Step 3: HIGH-LEVEL ARCHITECTURE (5 min)
 *   - Name your pattern and justify it in one sentence
 *   - Draw the layer diagram (UI → ViewModel → Repository → DataSources)
 *   - Identify major components
 *
 * Step 4: COMPONENT WALKTHROUGH (15 min)
 *   - Detail each major component
 *   - Data models and their relationships
 *   - API contract (request/response)
 *   - Caching strategy
 *   - Sync mechanism
 *
 * Step 5: FAILURE MODE ANALYSIS (5 min)
 *   - What happens when network drops?
 *   - What happens on process death?
 *   - What happens with conflicting edits?
 *   - Auth token expiration mid-request?
 *   - Database migration failure?
 *
 * Step 6: TRADE-OFFS & ALTERNATIVES (3 min)
 *   - What would you do differently at 10x scale?
 *   - Alternatives considered and why you rejected them
 *   - What technical debt does this design accept?
 */


// ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
// 2. DESIGN: OFFLINE-FIRST NOTES APP (Google Keep-like)
// ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

/**
 * Requirements:
 *   FR: Create, edit, delete notes. Rich text, images. Search. Labels/categories.
 *   NFR: Full offline support. Cross-device sync. Conflict resolution.
 *
 * Architecture: MVVM + Offline-First (Room as SSOT)
 *
 *   ┌─────────────┐
 *   │  Compose UI  │  ← observes StateFlow
 *   └──────┬───────┘
 *          │ events
 *   ┌──────▼───────┐
 *   │  ViewModel   │  ← processes intents, exposes state
 *   └──────┬───────┘
 *          │
 *   ┌──────▼───────┐
 *   │  Repository   │  ← coordinates sync, provides data
 *   └──┬────────┬───┘
 *      │        │
 *  ┌───▼──┐ ┌──▼────────┐
 *  │ Room │ │ Sync Engine │
 *  │(SSOT)│ │ (WorkMgr)  │
 *  └──────┘ └────────────┘
 */

// Data model with sync metadata
@Entity(tableName = "notes")
data class NoteEntity(
    @PrimaryKey val id: String = UUID.randomUUID().toString(),
    val title: String,
    val content: String,
    val labelIds: List<String> = emptyList(),
    val isPinned: Boolean = false,
    val createdAt: Long = System.currentTimeMillis(),
    val updatedAt: Long = System.currentTimeMillis(),
    // Sync metadata
    val syncStatus: SyncStatus = SyncStatus.PENDING,
    val serverVersion: Long = 0,
    val localVersion: Long = 1,
    val isDeleted: Boolean = false  // Soft delete for sync
)

enum class SyncStatus { SYNCED, PENDING, CONFLICT }

// Conflict resolution strategy
class ConflictResolver {
    /**
     * Conflict Resolution Strategies:
     *
     * 1. Last-Write-Wins (LWW):
     *    - Simple: higher timestamp wins
     *    - Risk: silent data loss
     *
     * 2. Server-Wins:
     *    - Server version always takes priority
     *    - Client changes may be lost
     *
     * 3. Client-Wins:
     *    - Local version always takes priority
     *    - Other devices' changes may be lost
     *
     * 4. Merge (recommended for text):
     *    - 3-way merge using common ancestor
     *    - If auto-merge fails, show both versions to user
     *
     * For Google interview: discuss trade-offs of each approach.
     */
    fun resolve(local: NoteEntity, remote: NoteEntity, ancestor: NoteEntity?): NoteEntity {
        // Field-level merge strategy
        return NoteEntity(
            id = local.id,
            title = if (local.title != ancestor?.title) local.title else remote.title,
            content = mergeContent(local.content, remote.content, ancestor?.content),
            updatedAt = maxOf(local.updatedAt, remote.updatedAt),
            serverVersion = remote.serverVersion,
            localVersion = local.localVersion + 1,
            syncStatus = SyncStatus.SYNCED
        )
    }

    private fun mergeContent(local: String, remote: String, ancestor: String?): String {
        // Simplified — real implementation would use OT or CRDT
        if (ancestor == null) return local
        if (local == ancestor) return remote
        if (remote == ancestor) return local
        // Both changed — conflict, keep both
        return "$local\n---CONFLICT---\n$remote"
    }
}

// Sync engine using WorkManager
class NoteSyncWorker(
    context: Context,
    params: WorkerParameters,
    private val noteDao: NoteDao,
    private val api: NoteApi,
    private val conflictResolver: ConflictResolver
) : CoroutineWorker(context, params) {

    override suspend fun doWork(): Result {
        return try {
            // 1. Push local changes
            val pendingNotes = noteDao.getPendingSync()
            for (note in pendingNotes) {
                val result = api.upsertNote(note.toRequest())
                when {
                    result.isSuccess -> {
                        noteDao.update(note.copy(
                            syncStatus = SyncStatus.SYNCED,
                            serverVersion = result.getOrThrow().version
                        ))
                    }
                    result.isConflict -> {
                        val serverNote = api.getNote(note.id).getOrThrow()
                        val resolved = conflictResolver.resolve(note, serverNote.toEntity(), null)
                        noteDao.update(resolved)
                    }
                }
            }

            // 2. Pull remote changes
            val lastSyncTime = noteDao.getLastSyncTime()
            val remoteChanges = api.getChanges(since = lastSyncTime)
            for (remote in remoteChanges) {
                val local = noteDao.getById(remote.id)
                if (local == null || local.syncStatus == SyncStatus.SYNCED) {
                    noteDao.upsert(remote.toEntity().copy(syncStatus = SyncStatus.SYNCED))
                }
                // If local has pending changes, skip — will resolve on next push
            }

            Result.success()
        } catch (e: IOException) {
            if (runAttemptCount < 5) Result.retry() else Result.failure()
        }
    }
}


// ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
// 3. DESIGN: IMAGE LOADING LIBRARY (Coil/Glide-like)
// ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

/**
 * Requirements:
 *   - Load images from URL into ImageView/Compose
 *   - Memory cache (LRU) + Disk cache
 *   - Transformations (resize, crop, round corners)
 *   - Placeholder and error images
 *   - Lifecycle-aware (cancel on destroy)
 *   - Request deduplication
 *
 * Architecture:
 *
 *   ImageLoader.load(url)
 *       → Check Memory Cache (LruCache)
 *           → Hit: return Bitmap immediately
 *           → Miss: Check Disk Cache
 *               → Hit: decode, store in memory, return
 *               → Miss: Download from network
 *                   → Decode, apply transformations
 *                   → Store in disk cache + memory cache
 *                   → Return to caller
 */

class SimpleImageLoader(context: Context) {

    // Memory cache: ~1/8 of available heap
    private val maxMemory = (Runtime.getRuntime().maxMemory() / 1024).toInt()
    private val cacheSize = maxMemory / 8
    private val memoryCache = object : LruCache<String, Bitmap>(cacheSize) {
        override fun sizeOf(key: String, bitmap: Bitmap): Int {
            return bitmap.byteCount / 1024
        }
    }

    // Disk cache
    private val diskCache = DiskLruCache.open(
        File(context.cacheDir, "image_cache"),
        1, 1, 100L * 1024 * 1024  // 100 MB
    )

    // Request deduplication
    private val inflightRequests = ConcurrentHashMap<String, Deferred<Bitmap?>>()
    private val scope = CoroutineScope(Dispatchers.IO + SupervisorJob())

    suspend fun load(
        url: String,
        width: Int = 0,
        height: Int = 0
    ): Bitmap? {
        val cacheKey = generateKey(url, width, height)

        // 1. Memory cache check
        memoryCache.get(cacheKey)?.let { return it }

        // 2. Deduplicate concurrent requests for same image
        inflightRequests[cacheKey]?.let {
            return it.await()
        }

        val deferred = scope.async {
            try {
                // 3. Disk cache check
                val diskBitmap = loadFromDisk(cacheKey)
                if (diskBitmap != null) {
                    memoryCache.put(cacheKey, diskBitmap)
                    return@async diskBitmap
                }

                // 4. Network download
                val networkBitmap = downloadAndDecode(url, width, height)
                if (networkBitmap != null) {
                    memoryCache.put(cacheKey, networkBitmap)
                    saveToDisk(cacheKey, networkBitmap)
                }
                networkBitmap
            } finally {
                inflightRequests.remove(cacheKey)
            }
        }
        inflightRequests[cacheKey] = deferred

        return deferred.await()
    }

    private fun generateKey(url: String, width: Int, height: Int): String {
        return "${url}_${width}x${height}".hashCode().toString()
    }

    private fun downloadAndDecode(url: String, reqWidth: Int, reqHeight: Int): Bitmap? {
        // Download → sample decode → resize
        return null // Implementation
    }

    private fun loadFromDisk(key: String): Bitmap? = null // Implementation
    private fun saveToDisk(key: String, bitmap: Bitmap) {} // Implementation
}

/**
 * Key Discussion Points:
 *
 * 1. Memory pressure: LruCache eviction, respond to onTrimMemory()
 * 2. Disk cache: DiskLruCache with journaling for crash safety
 * 3. Sampling: BitmapFactory.Options.inSampleSize to decode at target size
 * 4. Lifecycle: cancel requests when target View is recycled/detached
 * 5. Bitmap pooling: reuse Bitmap memory (inBitmap) to reduce GC
 * 6. Thread pool: dedicated for decoding (CPU-bound), separate for network (IO)
 */


// ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
// 4. DESIGN: CHAT/MESSAGING APP
// ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

/**
 * Requirements:
 *   FR: Send/receive text messages, images. Group chats. Read receipts.
 *       Typing indicators. Push notifications.
 *   NFR: Real-time delivery. Offline message queueing. E2E encryption.
 *        Message ordering guaranteed.
 *
 * Architecture:
 *
 *   ┌─────────────────┐
 *   │   Compose UI     │  ← LazyColumn with pagination
 *   └────────┬─────────┘
 *            │
 *   ┌────────▼─────────┐
 *   │   ViewModel      │  ← UI state, send/receive actions
 *   └────────┬─────────┘
 *            │
 *   ┌────────▼─────────┐
 *   │  ChatRepository   │  ← coordinates local + remote
 *   └───┬──────────┬────┘
 *       │          │
 *   ┌───▼──┐  ┌───▼──────────────┐
 *   │ Room │  │ WebSocket Client  │
 *   │(msgs)│  │ (real-time)       │
 *   └──────┘  └──────────────────┘
 *                     ↕
 *             ┌───────────────┐
 *             │  FCM (push)   │  ← when app is backgrounded
 *             └───────────────┘
 *
 * Message Flow:
 *   Send: UI → ViewModel → Repository → Room (PENDING) → WebSocket
 *              → Server ACK → Room (SENT) → Server delivers → Room (DELIVERED)
 *              → Recipient reads → Room (READ)
 *
 *   Receive: WebSocket → Repository → Room → Flow → ViewModel → UI
 *            (or FCM → WorkManager → Repository → Room when backgrounded)
 */

// Message data model
@Entity(tableName = "messages")
data class MessageEntity(
    @PrimaryKey val id: String = UUID.randomUUID().toString(),
    val chatId: String,
    val senderId: String,
    val content: String,
    val type: MessageType = MessageType.TEXT,
    val status: MessageStatus = MessageStatus.PENDING,
    val timestamp: Long = System.currentTimeMillis(),
    val serverTimestamp: Long? = null,
    val localSequence: Long = 0  // For ordering before server assigns timestamp
)

enum class MessageType { TEXT, IMAGE, VIDEO, AUDIO, FILE }
enum class MessageStatus { PENDING, SENT, DELIVERED, READ, FAILED }

// WebSocket connection management
class ChatWebSocketClient(
    private val okHttpClient: OkHttpClient,
    private val messageDao: MessageDao,
    private val scope: CoroutineScope
) {
    private var webSocket: WebSocket? = null
    private val _connectionState = MutableStateFlow<ConnectionState>(ConnectionState.Disconnected)
    val connectionState: StateFlow<ConnectionState> = _connectionState.asStateFlow()

    private val _incomingMessages = MutableSharedFlow<ServerMessage>(
        extraBufferCapacity = 100,
        onBufferOverflow = BufferOverflow.DROP_OLDEST
    )
    val incomingMessages: SharedFlow<ServerMessage> = _incomingMessages.asSharedFlow()

    fun connect(authToken: String) {
        val request = Request.Builder()
            .url("wss://chat.example.com/ws")
            .header("Authorization", "Bearer $authToken")
            .build()

        webSocket = okHttpClient.newWebSocket(request, object : WebSocketListener() {
            override fun onOpen(webSocket: WebSocket, response: Response) {
                _connectionState.value = ConnectionState.Connected
            }

            override fun onMessage(webSocket: WebSocket, text: String) {
                val message = Json.decodeFromString<ServerMessage>(text)
                scope.launch { _incomingMessages.emit(message) }
            }

            override fun onFailure(webSocket: WebSocket, t: Throwable, response: Response?) {
                _connectionState.value = ConnectionState.Error(t)
                scheduleReconnect()
            }

            override fun onClosed(webSocket: WebSocket, code: Int, reason: String) {
                _connectionState.value = ConnectionState.Disconnected
            }
        })
    }

    private fun scheduleReconnect() {
        scope.launch {
            var delay = 1000L
            while (_connectionState.value !is ConnectionState.Connected) {
                delay(delay)
                delay = minOf(delay * 2, 30_000)  // Exponential backoff, max 30s
                connect(getStoredToken())
            }
        }
    }

    fun send(message: String): Boolean {
        return webSocket?.send(message) ?: false
    }

    fun disconnect() {
        webSocket?.close(1000, "User disconnected")
    }
}

sealed interface ConnectionState {
    data object Connected : ConnectionState
    data object Connecting : ConnectionState
    data object Disconnected : ConnectionState
    data class Error(val throwable: Throwable) : ConnectionState
}

/**
 * Key Design Decisions to Discuss:
 *
 * 1. Message Ordering: Use server-assigned timestamps for final ordering,
 *    local sequence numbers for optimistic display.
 *
 * 2. Offline Queue: Messages saved to Room with PENDING status.
 *    When online, WorkManager or WebSocket reconnect sends pending messages.
 *
 * 3. Pagination: Load messages from Room with Paging 3.
 *    Older messages fetched from API and cached in Room.
 *
 * 4. Push Notifications: FCM for when WebSocket is disconnected.
 *    Data message (not notification message) for custom handling.
 *
 * 5. E2E Encryption: Signal Protocol (libsignal).
 *    Keys stored in Android Keystore. Key exchange on first contact.
 *
 * 6. Reconnection: Exponential backoff with jitter.
 *    On reconnect, fetch missed messages since last known timestamp.
 */


// ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
// 5. DESIGN: NEWS FEED
// ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

/**
 * Requirements:
 *   - Scrollable feed of posts (text, images, video)
 *   - Pull-to-refresh
 *   - Infinite scroll with pagination
 *   - Like, comment, share actions
 *   - Efficient image/video loading
 *   - Offline reading of cached content
 *
 * Key Components:
 *
 *   1. Feed ViewModel:
 *      - Paging 3 with RemoteMediator
 *      - Room as cache, API as remote source
 *
 *   2. Image Loading:
 *      - Coil with memory + disk cache
 *      - Placeholder → low-res thumbnail → full image
 *
 *   3. Video:
 *      - ExoPlayer with preloading
 *      - Auto-play when >50% visible
 *      - Pause when scrolled away
 *
 *   4. RecyclerView/LazyColumn Optimization:
 *      - Stable keys for items
 *      - DiffUtil for minimal updates
 *      - View recycling across view types
 *      - Prefetch for smooth scrolling
 *
 *   5. Real-time Updates:
 *      - New post indicator (don't auto-scroll)
 *      - Like count updates via WebSocket/SSE
 *      - Optimistic UI updates for user actions
 */


// ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
// 6. DESIGN: ANALYTICS SDK
// ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

/**
 * Requirements:
 *   - Track events (screen views, clicks, custom events)
 *   - Batch and send periodically (minimize network calls)
 *   - Persist events if network unavailable
 *   - Minimal performance impact
 *   - Thread-safe
 *   - Battery-friendly
 *
 * Architecture:
 *   Event → Queue (in-memory) → Batcher → Disk (if threshold/timer)
 *         → WorkManager → API upload → Clear from disk
 */

class AnalyticsSDK private constructor(private val context: Context) {

    private val eventQueue = Channel<AnalyticsEvent>(Channel.UNLIMITED)
    private val scope = CoroutineScope(Dispatchers.IO + SupervisorJob())
    private val db: AnalyticsDatabase by lazy { AnalyticsDatabase.create(context) }

    init {
        // Consumer: batch events and persist
        scope.launch {
            val batch = mutableListOf<AnalyticsEvent>()
            for (event in eventQueue) {
                batch.add(event)
                if (batch.size >= BATCH_SIZE) {
                    persistBatch(batch.toList())
                    batch.clear()
                    scheduleUpload()
                }
            }
        }

        // Periodic flush
        scope.launch {
            while (true) {
                delay(FLUSH_INTERVAL_MS)
                flushPendingEvents()
            }
        }
    }

    fun track(eventName: String, properties: Map<String, Any> = emptyMap()) {
        val event = AnalyticsEvent(
            name = eventName,
            properties = properties,
            timestamp = System.currentTimeMillis(),
            sessionId = SessionManager.currentSessionId
        )
        eventQueue.trySend(event)
    }

    private suspend fun persistBatch(events: List<AnalyticsEvent>) {
        db.eventDao().insertAll(events.map { it.toEntity() })
    }

    private fun scheduleUpload() {
        val uploadWork = OneTimeWorkRequestBuilder<AnalyticsUploadWorker>()
            .setConstraints(Constraints.Builder()
                .setRequiredNetworkType(NetworkType.CONNECTED)
                .build())
            .build()
        WorkManager.getInstance(context)
            .enqueueUniqueWork("analytics_upload", ExistingWorkPolicy.KEEP, uploadWork)
    }

    companion object {
        private const val BATCH_SIZE = 50
        private const val FLUSH_INTERVAL_MS = 30_000L

        @Volatile private var instance: AnalyticsSDK? = null

        fun init(context: Context): AnalyticsSDK {
            return instance ?: synchronized(this) {
                instance ?: AnalyticsSDK(context.applicationContext).also { instance = it }
            }
        }
    }
}


// ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
// 7. DESIGN: FILE DOWNLOADER LIBRARY
// ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

/**
 * Requirements:
 *   - Download files from URL with pause/resume support
 *   - Progress reporting per download
 *   - Multiple parallel downloads with queue management
 *   - Survive process death and device reboot
 *   - Retry on failure with backoff
 *   - Notification for each active download
 *
 * Architecture:
 *   UI → DownloadManager API → Room (state) → WorkManager (execution)
 *       → OkHttp with Range headers → Disk
 *
 *   Room stores: URL, file path, status, bytes downloaded, total bytes
 *   WorkManager: one worker per download, unique work by URL
 *   OkHttp: HTTP Range header for resume support
 *   Flow: progress updates from worker → UI
 */

// Download state persisted in Room
@Entity(tableName = "downloads")
data class DownloadEntity(
    @PrimaryKey val id: String = UUID.randomUUID().toString(),
    val url: String,
    val fileName: String,
    val destinationPath: String,
    val status: DownloadStatus = DownloadStatus.QUEUED,
    val bytesDownloaded: Long = 0,
    val totalBytes: Long = -1,  // -1 = unknown until server responds
    val createdAt: Long = System.currentTimeMillis(),
    val error: String? = null,
    val retryCount: Int = 0
)

enum class DownloadStatus { QUEUED, DOWNLOADING, PAUSED, COMPLETED, FAILED, CANCELLED }

@Dao
interface DownloadDao {
    @Query("SELECT * FROM downloads ORDER BY createdAt DESC")
    fun observeAll(): Flow<List<DownloadEntity>>

    @Query("SELECT * FROM downloads WHERE id = :id")
    suspend fun getById(id: String): DownloadEntity?

    @Upsert
    suspend fun upsert(download: DownloadEntity)

    @Query("UPDATE downloads SET status = :status WHERE id = :id")
    suspend fun updateStatus(id: String, status: DownloadStatus)

    @Query("UPDATE downloads SET bytesDownloaded = :bytes WHERE id = :id")
    suspend fun updateProgress(id: String, bytes: Long)
}

// Download worker with resume support via HTTP Range headers
class FileDownloadWorker(
    context: Context,
    params: WorkerParameters,
    private val downloadDao: DownloadDao,
    private val okHttpClient: OkHttpClient
) : CoroutineWorker(context, params) {

    override suspend fun doWork(): Result {
        val downloadId = inputData.getString("download_id") ?: return Result.failure()
        val download = downloadDao.getById(downloadId) ?: return Result.failure()

        downloadDao.updateStatus(downloadId, DownloadStatus.DOWNLOADING)

        return try {
            val file = File(download.destinationPath)
            val existingBytes = if (file.exists()) file.length() else 0L

            // Range header for resume support
            val request = Request.Builder()
                .url(download.url)
                .apply {
                    if (existingBytes > 0) {
                        addHeader("Range", "bytes=$existingBytes-")
                    }
                }
                .build()

            okHttpClient.newCall(request).execute().use { response ->
                if (!response.isSuccessful && response.code != 206) {
                    throw IOException("Unexpected code: ${response.code}")
                }

                val totalBytes = if (response.code == 206) {
                    existingBytes + (response.body?.contentLength() ?: 0)
                } else {
                    response.body?.contentLength() ?: -1
                }

                downloadDao.upsert(download.copy(totalBytes = totalBytes))

                val sink = file.appendingSink().buffer()
                val source = response.body?.source() ?: throw IOException("Empty body")

                var bytesRead = existingBytes
                val buffer = ByteArray(8192)
                var read: Int

                while (source.read(buffer).also { read = it.toInt() } != -1L) {
                    sink.write(buffer, 0, read)
                    bytesRead += read

                    // Report progress
                    downloadDao.updateProgress(downloadId, bytesRead)
                    setProgress(workDataOf(
                        "progress" to bytesRead,
                        "total" to totalBytes
                    ))
                }
                sink.flush()
                sink.close()
            }

            downloadDao.updateStatus(downloadId, DownloadStatus.COMPLETED)
            Result.success()
        } catch (e: IOException) {
            if (runAttemptCount < 3) {
                downloadDao.updateStatus(downloadId, DownloadStatus.QUEUED)
                Result.retry()
            } else {
                downloadDao.upsert(download.copy(
                    status = DownloadStatus.FAILED,
                    error = e.message
                ))
                Result.failure()
            }
        }
    }
}

/**
 * Key Discussion Points:
 *
 * 1. Resume: HTTP Range header + append to existing file. Server must support
 *    206 Partial Content. If not, restart from scratch.
 *
 * 2. Parallel downloads: Each download is a separate WorkManager unique work.
 *    Limit concurrency with a custom Configuration (max workers).
 *
 * 3. Queue management: Room tracks all downloads. UI observes via Flow.
 *    Priority queue can be implemented with a priority field in entity.
 *
 * 4. Pause/Resume: Pause = cancel the worker + save bytesDownloaded in Room.
 *    Resume = re-enqueue work, worker reads bytesDownloaded and sets Range header.
 *
 * 5. Notifications: setForeground(ForegroundInfo) in CoroutineWorker
 *    for ongoing notification with progress bar.
 *
 * 6. Integrity: After download, verify checksum (MD5/SHA-256) if server provides one.
 */


// ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
// 8. DESIGN: OBSERVABILITY / LOGGING SDK
// ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

/**
 * Requirements:
 *   - Structured logging (tag, level, message, metadata)
 *   - Crash reporting (uncaught exception handler)
 *   - Performance metrics (startup time, frame drops, network latency)
 *   - Batched upload to backend (minimize network calls)
 *   - Persist logs to disk if network unavailable
 *   - Minimal impact on app performance and battery
 *
 * Architecture:
 *   App code → Logger API → In-memory ring buffer → Flush to disk
 *             → WorkManager → Batch upload to backend
 *             → Crash handler → Immediate disk write → Upload on next launch
 *
 * Log levels: VERBOSE, DEBUG, INFO, WARN, ERROR, FATAL
 *
 * Sampling:
 *   - DEBUG/VERBOSE: only in debug builds (never ship)
 *   - INFO: sample at 10-50% of sessions to reduce volume
 *   - WARN/ERROR: always log, always upload
 *   - FATAL (crash): always log, persist immediately, upload on next launch
 *
 * Privacy considerations:
 *   - NEVER log PII (emails, passwords, tokens, phone numbers)
 *   - Strip sensitive fields before logging
 *   - Allow users to opt out of analytics/logging
 *   - Comply with GDPR/CCPA — include session ID, not user ID, unless consented
 *
 * Integration with existing tools:
 *   - Firebase Crashlytics: crash reports + non-fatals, auto stack deobfuscation
 *   - Play Console Android Vitals: ANRs, crash rates, startup time, battery
 *   - Perfetto: system-level traces for debugging performance
 *   - Custom backend: for proprietary metrics and business-specific logging
 */

data class LogEntry(
    val level: LogLevel,
    val tag: String,
    val message: String,
    val metadata: Map<String, String> = emptyMap(),
    val timestamp: Long = System.currentTimeMillis(),
    val sessionId: String,
    val threadName: String = Thread.currentThread().name,
    val stackTrace: String? = null
)

enum class LogLevel { VERBOSE, DEBUG, INFO, WARN, ERROR, FATAL }

class ObservabilitySDK private constructor(private val context: Context) {

    private val buffer = ArrayDeque<LogEntry>(BUFFER_CAPACITY)
    private val scope = CoroutineScope(Dispatchers.IO + SupervisorJob())
    private val sessionId = UUID.randomUUID().toString()

    fun log(level: LogLevel, tag: String, message: String, metadata: Map<String, String> = emptyMap()) {
        if (level.ordinal < minLogLevel.ordinal) return

        val entry = LogEntry(
            level = level,
            tag = tag,
            message = message,
            metadata = metadata,
            sessionId = sessionId
        )

        synchronized(buffer) {
            buffer.addLast(entry)
            if (buffer.size >= FLUSH_THRESHOLD) {
                flushBufferToDisk()
            }
        }
    }

    fun installCrashHandler() {
        val defaultHandler = Thread.getDefaultUncaughtExceptionHandler()
        Thread.setDefaultUncaughtExceptionHandler { thread, throwable ->
            log(
                level = LogLevel.FATAL,
                tag = "CRASH",
                message = throwable.message ?: "Unknown crash",
                metadata = mapOf("thread" to thread.name)
            )
            // Flush immediately — process is about to die
            flushBufferToDisk()
            defaultHandler?.uncaughtException(thread, throwable)
        }
    }

    private fun flushBufferToDisk() {
        val entries: List<LogEntry>
        synchronized(buffer) {
            entries = buffer.toList()
            buffer.clear()
        }
        scope.launch {
            // Persist to local file or Room, then schedule upload
            persistEntries(entries)
            scheduleUpload()
        }
    }

    private suspend fun persistEntries(entries: List<LogEntry>) { /* Write to disk */ }

    private fun scheduleUpload() {
        WorkManager.getInstance(context).enqueueUniqueWork(
            "log_upload",
            ExistingWorkPolicy.KEEP,
            OneTimeWorkRequestBuilder<LogUploadWorker>()
                .setConstraints(Constraints.Builder()
                    .setRequiredNetworkType(NetworkType.CONNECTED)
                    .build())
                .build()
        )
    }

    companion object {
        private const val BUFFER_CAPACITY = 500
        private const val FLUSH_THRESHOLD = 100
        private var minLogLevel = LogLevel.INFO
    }
}

/**
 * Key Discussion Points:
 *
 * 1. Ring buffer: Fixed-size in-memory buffer avoids OOM.
 *    Oldest entries evicted if not flushed fast enough.
 *
 * 2. Crash reporting: Install UncaughtExceptionHandler, flush synchronously,
 *    then delegate to original handler. Upload on next app launch.
 *
 * 3. Performance metrics: Track via FrameMetrics API (API 24+) for jank,
 *    reportFullyDrawn() for startup. Send as structured log entries.
 *
 * 4. Batched upload: Reduces battery and network usage. Compress (gzip) before upload.
 *    Back-pressure: if disk queue grows too large, drop lowest-severity entries.
 */


// ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
// 9. COMMON MOBILE-SPECIFIC CONSTRAINTS
// ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

/**
 * Always discuss these in mobile system design:
 *
 * 1. Network: Hostile environment — drops, slow, metered
 *    → Offline-first, retry with backoff, request deduplication
 *
 * 2. Battery: Background work is heavily restricted
 *    → WorkManager, batch operations, avoid polling
 *
 * 3. Memory: Limited heap (256MB-512MB typical)
 *    → LRU caches, bitmap sampling, leak detection
 *
 * 4. Storage: Users care about app size
 *    → On-demand resources, aggressive caching policy with eviction
 *
 * 5. Process Death: App can be killed at any time
 *    → Restore state from SavedStateHandle + persistent storage
 *
 * 6. Security: Device can be rooted, APK can be decompiled
 *    → Keystore for secrets, cert pinning, obfuscation
 *
 * 7. Lifecycle: Screens can be created/destroyed unpredictably
 *    → Lifecycle-aware components, scoped work
 *
 * 8. Fragmentation: Thousands of device configs
 *    → Responsive layouts, API level checks, Compat libraries
 */


// ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
// PRACTICE QUESTIONS
// ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

/**
 * ❓ Q1: How do you handle sync conflicts in an offline-first app?
 *    A: Depends on the data type:
 *       - Last-Write-Wins for simple fields (user profile)
 *       - 3-way merge for text (notes, documents)
 *       - CRDTs for concurrent data structures (counters, sets)
 *       - Manual resolution (show both versions to user)
 *       Always discuss: What's the cost of data loss vs. complexity?
 *
 * ❓ Q2: Design a file download manager with pause/resume support.
 *    A: Room tracks download state (URL, progress, status, temp file path).
 *       OkHttp with Range headers for resume.
 *       WorkManager for background execution.
 *       CoroutineWorker with progress reporting.
 *       Notification for progress. Unique work per URL.
 *
 * ❓ Q3: How would you implement optimistic UI updates?
 *    A: Update local state immediately (Room + StateFlow).
 *       Send request to server in background.
 *       On success: update sync status.
 *       On failure: revert local state, show error.
 *       Example: Like button — show liked immediately, sync later.
 *
 * ❓ Q4: How do you handle token expiration in a WebSocket connection?
 *    A: Server sends a "token_expired" message.
 *       Client refreshes token, reconnects with new token.
 *       During refresh, queue outgoing messages.
 *       After reconnect, replay pending messages + fetch missed messages
 *       using last known server timestamp.
 */

17_amit_shekhar_questions_answers.kt · Kotlin reference

/**
 * ============================================================================
 * 17 — AMIT SHEKHAR'S ANDROID INTERVIEW QUESTIONS — DETAILED ANSWERS
 * Google SDE-2 Interview Prep
 * ============================================================================
 *
 * Topics Covered:
 *   Section 1:  Kotlin Coroutines
 *   Section 2:  Kotlin Flow API

// SECTION 1: KOTLIN COROUTINES
// ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

/**
 * Q1: What are Coroutines in Kotlin?
 *
 * Coroutines are a concurrency design pattern that allows you to write
 * asynchronous, non-blocking code in a sequential style. They are lightweight
 * threads — you can launch thousands of coroutines without the overhead of
 * OS threads because they are scheduled cooperatively on a thread pool.
 *
 * Key points for interview:
 *  - Coroutines SUSPEND execution without blocking the thread.
 *  - The Kotlin compiler transforms suspend functions into a state machine
 *    under the hood (Continuation Passing Style — CPS).
 *  - They are built on top of actual threads but multiplex many coroutines
 *    onto a small number of threads.
 *  - They simplify callback-based code ("callback hell") into linear code.
 */

fun coroutineBasicExample() {
    val scope = CoroutineScope(Dispatchers.Main + SupervisorJob())

    scope.launch {
        val user = fetchUser()        // suspends, doesn't block Main thread
        val posts = fetchPosts(user)  // sequential — runs after fetchUser completes
        updateUI(user, posts)         // back on Main thread
    }
}

// Under the hood the compiler turns this into a state machine with labels:
// state 0 → call fetchUser, suspend
// state 1 → call fetchPosts, suspend
// state 2 → call updateUI, return

/**
 * Q2: What is a suspend function?
 *
 * A suspend function is a function that can be paused and resumed later.
 * It can only be called from another suspend function or from a coroutine.
 * The `suspend` keyword tells the compiler to add a hidden `Continuation`
 * parameter to the function signature (CPS transformation).
 *
 * A suspend function does NOT automatically run on a background thread.
 * It simply has the ABILITY to suspend. You must use withContext() or
 * other coroutine builders to switch dispatchers if needed.
 */

suspend fun fetchUser(): User {
    return withContext(Dispatchers.IO) {
        // This block executes on an IO thread
        api.getUser()   // network call
    }
    // After withContext completes, execution resumes on the original dispatcher
}

// Decompiled signature (simplified):
// fun fetchUser(continuation: Continuation<User>): Any?
// Returns COROUTINE_SUSPENDED if it actually suspends, or the result if it completes immediately.

/**
 * Q3: Suspending vs Blocking in Kotlin Coroutines
 *
 * BLOCKING: The thread is occupied and cannot do anything else.
 *   Thread.sleep(1000) → the thread sits idle for 1 second.
 *
 * SUSPENDING: The coroutine pauses but the thread is FREE to do other work.
 *   delay(1000) → the coroutine suspends, and the thread can execute other
 *   coroutines or tasks during that time.
 *
 * Analogy: Blocking = you stand in line at the restaurant doing nothing.
 *          Suspending = you give your number and go do errands; they call
 *          you when your table is ready.
 *
 * In interview: "Suspending frees the thread; blocking holds it hostage."
 */

fun blockingVsSuspending() {
    // BLOCKING — holds the Main thread for 2 seconds, causes ANR
    // Thread.sleep(2000)

    // SUSPENDING — Main thread is free during the 2-second wait
    CoroutineScope(Dispatchers.Main).launch {
        delay(2000)  // suspends, doesn't block
        println("Resumed after 2 seconds")
    }
}

/**
 * Q4: Launch vs Async in Kotlin Coroutines
 *
 * launch { } — "fire and forget". Returns a Job. Does NOT return a result.
 *   Use when you don't need a return value (e.g., updating UI, writing to DB).
 *
 * async { } — returns a Deferred<T> (which is a Job + a future result).
 *   Call .await() to get the result. Use when you need a return value,
 *   especially for parallel decomposition.
 *
 * Both are coroutine BUILDERS that create new child coroutines.
 *
 * Key difference for exceptions:
 *   - launch propagates exceptions immediately to the parent.
 *   - async stores the exception in the Deferred and throws on await().
 */

fun launchVsAsync() {
    val scope = CoroutineScope(Dispatchers.Main + Job())

    // launch — fire and forget
    scope.launch {
        saveToDatabase(data)  // no return value needed
    }

    // async — returns a result
    scope.launch {
        val deferredUser = async(Dispatchers.IO) { api.getUser() }
        val deferredPosts = async(Dispatchers.IO) { api.getPosts() }
        // Both requests run in PARALLEL
        val user = deferredUser.await()
        val posts = deferredPosts.await()
        showData(user, posts)
    }
}

/**
 * Q5: What are Dispatchers in Kotlin Coroutines?
 *
 * Dispatchers determine WHICH THREAD or thread pool a coroutine runs on.
 *
 * Dispatchers.Main      — Main/UI thread. Use for UI updates, lightweight work.
 *                          Backed by Android's Main Looper.
 *
 * Dispatchers.IO        — Optimized for blocking I/O (network, disk, DB).
 *                          Uses a shared pool that can grow up to 64 threads
 *                          (or more based on cores). Shares threads with Default.
 *
 * Dispatchers.Default   — CPU-intensive work (sorting, parsing JSON, DiffUtil).
 *                          Thread pool size = number of CPU cores (min 2).
 *
 * Dispatchers.Unconfined — Starts on the caller thread, but after the first
 *                          suspension point resumes on whatever thread the
 *                          suspending function used. Rarely used in production.
 *
 * You can also create custom dispatchers:
 *   val singleThread = newSingleThreadContext("MyThread")
 *   val limitedPool = Dispatchers.IO.limitedParallelism(4)
 */

suspend fun dispatcherExample() {
    withContext(Dispatchers.IO) {
        val data = readFromDisk()           // IO-bound
        val parsed = withContext(Dispatchers.Default) {
            parseJson(data)                  // CPU-bound
        }
        withContext(Dispatchers.Main) {
            textView.text = parsed.title     // UI update
        }
    }
}

/**
 * Q6: What is Coroutine Scope? What is Coroutine Context?
 *
